# 07 — Roadmap and task cards

Estimates are for a single developer. The user picks the card: "do T03".

## Stage 0 — Decisions (before code)
User's answers to the questions from `08-risks-open-questions.md`. Recorded in `02`/`03`.

## Stage 1 — Core without a simulator (MVP logic)

### T01 — Monorepo skeleton
pnpm workspace, TypeScript strict, Biome, Vitest. Packages `core`, `scenarios`, `transport`. Scripts typecheck/lint/test. Update the commands in CLAUDE.md.
**Done when:** empty packages build, the placeholder test is green, the CI script passes locally.

### T02 — SimBrief OFP normalization
`Ofp` type, parser from JSON v2, units (kg/lbs → kg), unit tests on 2–3 anonymized examples.
**Done when:** all fields from `06` are mapped, format errors raise a clear exception.

### T03 — Clock, Rng, TelemetrySnapshot
Interfaces, seeded Rng, snapshot type (see `03`), synthetic flight profile generator.

### T04 — Flight phase FSM
Per `04`: phases, hysteresis, pause and simRate, discontinuity, OOOI, serialization.
**Done when:** synthetic profiles (normal, go-around, pause, teleport) produce the expected phase sequences in snapshot tests.

### T05 — Formatter and DisplayProfile
Line wrapping by width, uppercase, character filter, separators. Default profile + B77x/B78x.

### T06 — Scenario engine v0
YAML + JSON Schema, triggers `phase_enter/time/distance/event`, `probability`, `cooldown`, steps `send/wait`. No `reply/branch`.

### T07 — 15 base scenarios
From the MVP list in `02`. Test: each one runs on 3 synthetic flights.

## Stage 2 — The real world

### T08 — MSFS bridge (C#)
SimConnect → WebSocket 127.0.0.1, reconnect, pause/simrate. **Human check** in MSFS.

### T09 — Hoppie transport + Mock
Queue, rate limit, backoff, ping/telex/poll. Mock for tests. **Human check**: telex arrives in Fenix/FBW.

### T10 — Desktop shell + UI
Entry of the SimBrief username and the Hoppie code (in the keychain), start/stop, message feed, current phase, scenario settings.

### T11 — Telemetry recording and replay
JSONL recording from the bridge, run through the core, CLI for debugging scenarios.

## Stage 3 — Variety

### T12 — Two-way communication: `reply`, `branch`, pilot commands
### T13 — Data adapters: METAR/TAF, VATSIM, EONET, OSM stands (with cache)
### T14 — 15+ irregular scenarios with chains and flags between flights
### T15 — Display profiles of popular add-ons (based on the results of human checks)

## Stage 4 — Optional
### T16 — Backend: LLM proxy (`llm_flavor`), paid APIs
### T17 — SayIntentions transport
### T18 — X-Plane 12 bridge
Done 2026-10-22: the `xplane` source in the same C# bridge (`bridge/msfs/src/AcarsBridge/XPlaneSource.cs`, `XPlane.cs`; `--source xplane`, `--xplane-port`, default 8086) through the built-in X-Plane Web API (12.1.1+, `06`, "X-Plane 12 Web API"). The protocol to the app did not change; `hello.source` = `xplane`, commands — only `ping`. Datarefs are translated into the same `SimFrame` as SimConnect, so `SnapshotPump`/`TelemetryMapper` are shared. In the app there is the setting "Simulator: MSFS / X-Plane 12" (`simulator`, default `msfs`); the installed app restarts the bridge when the simulator or port changes. With X-Plane: "Sim time" and "Rescue services" are dimmed and have no effect, smoke and rescue vehicles are not requested (no lines in the feed).
**Mapping:** `latitude`/`longitude`; `elevation`, `y_agl` m → ft; `indicated_airspeed` kias; `groundspeed` m/s → kt; `vh_ind_fpm`; `true_psi`; `onground_any`; `beacon_on`; `acf_num_engines`; `ENGN_running[0..3]`; `cockpit2/engine/indicators/N2_percent[0..3]`; `m_fuel_total` kg; `gear_handle_down`; `sim/time/paused` or `sim_speed` = 0 → pause; `sim_speed` → `simRate`.
**Assumptions (approved by the user 2026-10-22):** 1) parking brake — `parking_brake_ratio` ≥ 0.5; 2) `flapsIdx` = round(`flap_handle_request_ratio` × `acf_flap_detents`) — X-Plane has no lever index; 3) `doorsOpen` — `door_open_ratio[0..3]` × 100, door order as defined by the aircraft author; 4) X-Plane transponder → MSFS enum: off→0, stby→1, test→2, on (A)→3, alt (C)/TA/TA-RA→4, GND→5; 5) `aircraftTitle` = `acf_ui_name`, if empty — `acf_descrip`, plus `(acf_ICAO)` if it is not in the title; 6) "sim running" = datarefs found and subscription accepted (there is no "in menu" signal); 7) `touchdownFpm` is computed by the bridge: |`vh_ind_fpm`| of the last update in the air before `onground_any` 0→1 (~10 Hz), held until the next touchdown; 8) `simUtcMs` is not sent (the datarefs have no year).
**Hot Start CL650 (2026-10-22, X-Plane 12.4.3, verified on a live aircraft by watching the datarefs while the user worked in the cockpit):** the standard `parking_brake_ratio` stays 0 with the brake set, `flap_handle_request_ratio` jumps 0 → 1 already at 20°. Therefore, when the CL650 is loaded, the bridge takes `CL650/pedestal/park_brake` (lever 0…1, ≥ 0.5 = set) and `CL650/pedestal/flaps` (lever position: 0 UP, 1 = 20, 2 = 30, 3 = 45, rounded). The beacon (`beacon_on`), the main door (`door_open_ratio[0]`) and the transponder (`transponder_mode`, TA/RA = 7) work in the standard way on the CL650. The bridge reconnects when the aircraft changes (`acf_ui_name`/`acf_ICAO`) to find the new aircraft's datarefs. Not verified: engines (`ENGN_running`, N2; candidate — `CL650/fo_state/engine/{left,right}/n2`), fuel (`m_fuel_total` = 2105 kg on the stand, not checked against the cockpit gauge), touchdown.
**Human check:** 0) CL650: engine start — `engRunning`/`engN2` in the bridge telemetry; check the fuel in kg against the CL650 indication. 1) X-Plane 12.1.1+, Settings > Network — incoming traffic allowed. In the app "Simulator" → "X-Plane 12": in the status bar the bridge is connected, the simulator — "waiting for flight" until a flight is loaded; after the aircraft is loaded on the stand — "connected", telemetry flows (position, fuel, brake). 2) Pause in X-Plane → status "paused". 3) A full flight of any airliner (for example, Zibo 737-800): phases from passenger boarding to IN as in MSFS; after landing `flight.touchdown_fpm` is close to the touchdown vertical speed shown by X-Plane itself or by the add-on (Landing Rate). 4) Check with DataRefTool: the number of flap detents (`flapsIdx` in the recording log), open doors (the first four), transponder mode (STBY/ALT/TA-RA). 5) `aircraftTitle` in the flight recording — with Zibo the 737 display profile is chosen automatically. 6) Changing "Simulator" in the installed app — the bridge restarts without restarting the app.
### T59 — Simulator autoselect in the bridge (MSFS / X-Plane 12)
User request 2026-10-23, continuation of T18. Right now the bridge works with the simulator chosen in the "Simulator" setting: the installed app restarts the bridge on change, and `ACARS Link.bat` reads the setting only at startup. The bridge should connect to the running simulator by itself, without a manual choice.
**Included:** bridge source `auto` (default): alternately tries SimConnect (MSFS) and the X-Plane Web API (`localhost:8086`), works with whichever answered; when it closes — searches for both again. `hello.source` and `status` say which simulator is connected, the app shows it in the status bar. In the app, whatever depends on the simulator (sim time, smoke and rescue vehicles — MSFS only) is decided by the actual source from `hello`/`status`, not by the setting. The "Simulator" setting gets the value "Automatic" (default) and remains for manual selection; `ACARS Link.bat` and the installed app start the bridge without `--source` when "Automatic" is selected.
**To decide before implementation (Q, realism rule — assumptions with approval):** 1) what to do if both simulators are running (priority of one or first to answer); 2) probe frequency while neither is running (backoff as now: 1, 2, 4 … 30 s, shared or separate); 3) whether the bridge protocol must change (new field in `status`, protocol version) or `hello.source` on reconnect is enough; 4) what the user sees when the simulator changes during a flight (a line in the feed, stopping the flight or nothing).
**User decisions (2026-10-23, assumptions approved):** 1) both running — first to answer: in each round SimConnect first, then X-Plane; the connected one holds the bridge until it closes, the other is not tried in the meantime; 2) one shared backoff 1, 2, 4 … 30 s per round, reset when a simulator answered; 3) the protocol stays v2: `hello.source` = `auto`, in `status` an optional field `source` (`simconnect` | `xplane`, only when `connected`), old clients do not notice it; in `hello.commands` — the commands of both simulators, a command goes to the connected one if it knows it, otherwise to the first one that knows it (with X-Plane `smoke_start` → `sim not connected`); 4) a simulator change during a flight — a "System" line in the feed `sim_changed` ("Simulator changed: X-Plane 12 → MSFS; the flight continues"), the flight does not stop; 5) migration: the setting is now `simChoice` (`auto` | `msfs` | `xplane`, default `auto`); old key `simulator`: `msfs` (the former default) → `auto`, `xplane` stays.
**Done (2026-10-23):** bridge — `SimLoop` and `AutoSimSource` (`bridge/msfs/src/AcarsBridge/SimSource.cs`), `SimConnectSource` and `XPlaneSource` became `ISimSession` sessions (one connection attempt; the reconnect loop is shared), `--source auto` by default (`Program.cs`; without SimConnect in the build — X-Plane only), `BridgeStatus.Source`. App — `BridgeView.source` (`status.source`, for a bridge with a fixed source — `hello.source`), `activeSim()` in `shared/api.ts`: sim time, smoke, rescue services and dimmed settings — by the actual simulator; status bar line "connected · MSFS / X-Plane 12"; `bridgeArgs`: `auto` — without `--source`, `msfs` — `--source simconnect`; `ACARS Link.bat` reads `simChoice` (and the old `simulator`). If a flight is started while the simulator is not yet known (`auto`, the bridge is not connected yet), "sim time" is enabled as for MSFS; if X-Plane connects afterwards, the flight will wait for the simulator time — see "Notes" of the report.
**Not included:** other simulators (P3D, FSX); checking the CL650 engines and fuel (stays in T18, "Human check").
**Done when:** `pnpm check` and `pnpm bridge:test` are green; bridge tests: with no simulators — repeated probes of both; X-Plane answered — source `xplane`; X-Plane closed, MSFS started — switch without restarting the bridge; app tests: capabilities (sim time, smoke, rescue services) follow the actual source.
**Human check:** 1) Start the app with "Automatic", then X-Plane 12 with a loaded flight — the bridge connects to X-Plane by itself. 2) Close X-Plane, start MSFS — the bridge switches to MSFS without restarting the app and the bridge. 3) The status bar shows which simulator is connected.

**User decisions 2026-10-22:** license — proprietary (All rights reserved + EULA): `LICENSE`, `apps/desktop/build/license.txt` (EULA in two languages, shown by the installer; the text was written without a lawyer, country/jurisdiction and contact are not specified), `THIRD-PARTY-NOTICES.md` (placed in `resources`). Code signing — **not doing it**, the installer is unsigned (SmartScreen will warn; in the user instructions — "More info → Run anyway"). The license texts of the bundle's npm packages and of the .NET runtime packages are collected by `pnpm licenses` (`tools/licenses/collect.mjs`) into `resources\THIRD-PARTY-LICENSES.txt`. Checked 2026-10-22: USGS — public domain, give credit (done); no terms found for SWPC, TheSpaceDevs, natTrak, EUROCONTROL/ICAO. Open `[AT RELEASE]`: the right to distribute `SimConnect.dll` (the SDK EULA allows "distributable code", but the file is not named that way; on devsupport.flightsimulator.com topics 18233 and 18295 have no reply from Microsoft/Asobo as of 2026-10-13); written replies from the owners of TheSpaceDevs, natTrak/VATSIM, EUROCONTROL/ICAO and NOAA.
App protection (authorship marks, Electron Fuses, canaries, EULA addendum) — T62.

### T20 — Community scenario packs (user folder + validation)

## Stage 5 — Advanced features

User decision 2026-10-09: implement the features offered by comparable ACARS products (free and announced paid tiers) that we do not have yet. No code from other ACARS apps is copied (invariant 1), only ideas from public descriptions.

### Realism and data verification rule (for all Stage 5 cards)

1. **Maximum realism.** Format, phraseology, timings and logic — as in real operations. Message samples are taken from real sources (ICAO Doc 4444 and the CPDLC message set, ARINC 620/623, AIP, real ACARS printouts). The source of a sample is given in a comment to the scenario or table.
2. **Maximum data verification.** Codes, frequencies, level rules, squawk ranges, FIR boundaries, stations, satellites, schedules, API fields — only from a verified source. The source, verification date and license are recorded in `06-integrations.md` (or next to the table). Do not guess: a place without a source is marked `[VERIFY]` and checked before use.
3. **Assumptions — only with approval.** A simplification, approximation, heuristic or synthetic data is allowed, but **before implementation** it must be listed to the user: what we assume, why there is no real data, how it differs from reality, whether the pilot will notice. Without an explicit "yes" the assumption is not implemented. Approved assumptions are recorded in the card (subsection "Assumptions") with the date.
4. **Tables under tests.** Every rule table (forbidden squawks, levels, ranges) is covered by tests on real examples. External API responses — trimmed real fixtures. External data goes into the flight recording (replay is deterministic).
5. **`[AT RELEASE]`** (user decision 2026-10-09): a question that does not require a human check in the simulator and does not hinder personal use (licenses, unavailable AIPs, real OFPs for cross-checking) is closed before the public release. For now the accepted decision or assumption applies. The summary list — `08`, "Deferred until the public release". The `[VERIFY]` mark for a human check in the simulator stays open.
6. Invariants are not relaxed: numbers not from the LLM (8), everything through the formatter (9), keys only with the user or on the backend (2), respect for the Hoppie network (6).

### Priority 1

#### T21 — "App → bridge" command channel
Bridge protocol v2: besides telemetry, the app sends commands (whitelist of types, validation, confirmation or error). Listens only on 127.0.0.1. The core emits a new output type "simulator command", alongside "send message". This is the basis for T22 and T34.
**Done when:** the fake source accepts and confirms commands, protocol tests in `bridge:test` and in `apps/desktop/test` are green, v1 clients do not break.

#### T22 — Custom messages on the VDGS via GSX
Output of airline text on the VDGS board (gate, flight number, greeting, scenario messages: new step `vdgs:`). Per the FSDreamTeam documentation: mechanism (L-variables, file or GSX Pro API), length and character set, GSX version (closed by the analysis of 2026-10-09, below; character set — human check). Without GSX the function is silently disabled. Depends on T21.
**Human check:** MSFS 2024 + GSX Pro, an airport with a GSX VDGS, arrival at the stand: the text appears on the board and does not interfere with parking.

**Analysis (2026-10-09).** The source is the FSDreamTeam manual "VGDS Custom Messages System" of 2025-07-29, shipped with GSX Pro (`couatl\GSX\SDK\Samples\VGDS\vgds_developer_manual.pdf`), details in `06`, "GSX VDGS". The mechanism is JSON files in `%APPDATA%\Virtuali\GSX\VGDS\`, which GSX rereads by itself. There are no L-variables and no API, the bridge is not needed. Limits: small 4×2 (at the stand), narrow 6×6, wide 9×7, x 10×7 (the last line belongs to GSX, see "Correction" below). The character set is not described in the source. When GSX shows the message and whether it interrupts the parking guidance is also not described — this is a human check.

**User decision (2026-10-09):** the file is written by the app's main process (`apps/desktop/src/main/vdgs.ts`), not by the bridge. From T21 only the core output type `CoreOutput.commands` (command `vdgs`) is used.

**Assumptions (approved by the user 2026-10-09):**
1. The message is filtered only by the arrival airport (or diversion), with no gate filter: the gate names from the OFP/OSM/`ops.arr_gate` may not match GSX's `gate_name`/`gate_number`.
2. `valid_from`/`valid_until` are not used (the simulator time may differ from real time). The app owns a single file `acars-sim.json`, writes it atomically and deletes it at the end of the flight, on exit and at app start.
3. GSX is considered installed if the folder `%APPDATA%\Virtuali\GSX` exists. No folder — the function is silently disabled.
4. The `vdgs:` step: `narrow` is mandatory, `wide` and `x` without their own lines take `narrow`, `small` is output only if set. A line that does not fit is an error (a literal — at scenario load, a template — `SCENARIO_ERROR` at run time), no truncation.
5. Characters: only A–Z, 0–9, space and `- / . :`. Values (flight, gate, airport) are substituted by the core. GSX's own SimBrief variables are not used (invariant 8).

**Correction (approved by the user 2026-11-05):** per the VGDS editor manual (`06`, "GSX VDGS", second source) the last line of the narrow/wide/x board is GSX's scrolling line. Line limits — narrow 5, wide 6, x 6 (were 6/7/7, the gate number in `vdgs_arrival` landed on this line). `vdgs_arrival`: on narrow without the "FROM" line. The `flex` type is not added.

#### T23 — XATC: station, CPDLC logon, FIR tracking
Simulation of an ATC station through Hoppie: pilot logon to the station, a reply with the current ATC unit. On crossing an FIR boundary the logon message is updated (hand-off, like CDA/NDA in real CPDLC). FIR boundaries — external data in the core input, the point-in-polygon check in the core.
**Open questions (before code; closed by the decisions of 2026-10-09 below, VATSpy license — CC BY-SA 4.0):** the station callsign on the shared Hoppie network (unique per user? Hoppie rules `[VERIFY]`, coordination with Hoppie from `02`); packet type `cpdlc` or `telex`; which aircraft support CPDLC through Hoppie; behavior when a live controller of this sector is online on VATSIM (do not conflict). Source of FIR boundaries (VATSpy data project or other) — license `[VERIFY]`.
**Human check:** FBW A32NX and PMDG 777, logon to the station, a flight through 2+ FIRs: messages arrive at the boundaries.

**Analysis (2026-10-09).** Details — `06`, "CPDLC through Hoppie" and "FIR boundaries". Hoppie has no rules about station callsigns. Live VATSIM stations log on under 4-letter codes (EDGG, EDYJ, LONC). Logon and hand-off at Hoppie are `cpdlc` packets only (`/data2/<min>/<mrn>/<ra>/<text>`), aircraft do not process telex this way. The phrases are taken from the FBW A32NX code and from captured network packets: `REQUEST LOGON` → `LOGON ACCEPTED`, `CURRENT ATC UNIT@_@<code>@_@<name> CTL`, `HANDOVER @<code>`, `LOGOFF`. FIR boundaries — VATSpy Data Project (CC BY-SA 4.0). Which aircraft support CPDLC through Hoppie: FBW A32NX (by code). iniBuilds A350/A380 send `REQUEST LOGON` (verified in T09). The rest — human check.

**User decisions (2026-10-09):**
1. Station callsign — `X` + the last three letters of the FIR (EDGG → XDGG). There are no ICAO indices on X: the station does not intercept logons of other people's pilots and does not interfere with live controllers. At the FIR boundary — a real hand-off (`HANDOVER @XDWW`), the aircraft logs on to the next station itself.
2. ATC packets — `cpdlc`, airline messages stay `telex`. For CPDLC text the formatter has its own profile: without `/` (FBW reads the text as the sixth `/`-separated field), `@` and `_` are allowed.
3. If a center (CTR/FSS) covering the FIR is online on VATSIM, the station yields: to a connected aircraft — the notification `VATSIM ATC ONLINE@_@CONTACT <callsign> @<frequency>@` and `LOGOFF`, to a logon — `UNABLE` with the same text. In the next FIR or after the controller leaves, the station accepts logons again. The setting "Yield to live VATSIM ATC", on by default.

**Assumptions (approved by the user 2026-10-09):**
1. FIR boundaries — from the VATSpy `Boundaries.geojson` (VATSIM data, an approximation of the real FIRs). The file is downloaded by the app and cached for 7 days; the repository holds only a trimmed fixture for tests.
2. FIRs only (VATSpy 4-letter areas), no UIRs and no sectors. Where areas overlap, the areas that serve as the FIR of some airport (`[Airports]` of VATSpy.dat) come first: overlay areas without airports (`EGVV` Swanwick Military, `EDXX` FIS only) do not override the FIR. Among the remaining ones the larger is chosen (London TMA `EGTL` inside `EGTT`). In Australia VATSpy has only sectors (YGUN "Melbourne (Gundagai)"), the station there is per sector.
3. Logon is accepted in any FIR. The real CPDLC coverage (in Europe — only above FL285, etc.) is not modeled.

**Implementation.** Core: `packages/core/src/atc/` (`cpdlc.ts` — packets and profile, `fir.ts` — point in polygon, callsign and unit name, `xatc.ts` — station). Core option `xatc: { enabled, standAside }`, output `CoreOutput.listen` — the callsigns of stations that the transport polls before the first send (the station waits for a logon). Incoming messages with `to` = a station `X…` go to the station, the rest to the airline. ATC messages do not wait for the interval between telex messages. Data: `packages/data-sources/src/vatspy.ts` (FIRs along the route, VATSIM centers by VATSpy prefixes, including UIRs). App: the setting "XATC station" (off by default, live data needed), an XATC line in the status, events in the feed. Other CPDLC requests until T25 get `MESSAGE NOT SUPPORTED BY THIS ATS UNIT` (a phrase of live Hoppie stations). At the stand (ON_BLOCK) the station sends `LOGOFF` to the connected aircraft and stops being polled.

#### T24 — XATC: PDC on request, squawk by regional rules
A PDC (DCL) request from the pilot, a reply in the real format (ARINC 623 / ED-85A, American PDC — `[VERIFY]` samples): runway, SID, initial altitude, departure frequency, ATIS, squawk. Squawk — by regional rules (ORCAM and analogues), with a ban list: emergency and special codes per ICAO Annex 10 Vol IV, Doc 4444 and regional AIPs (7500/7600/7700, 2000, 7000, 1200, 1000, 7400, etc. — the full list only from sources). Data that is not in the OFP (initial altitude, frequency) — only from a source or as an approved assumption.
**Done when:** tests of the ban list and of ranges by region on real examples; the PDC for 3+ regions (EU, US, Asia) matches real samples in format.

**Analysis (2026-10-09).** Details — `06`, "DCL/PDC" and "Squawk tables". The pilot's request in Hoppie — telex `REQUEST PREDEP CLEARANCE <callsign> <type> TO <dest.> AT <dep> STAND <x> ATIS <letter>` (FBW `DclMessage.ts`, TopSky "RCD"). Europe and Asia — ARINC 623 DCL (RCD → FSM → CLD → CDA/WILCO → FSM). Samples: Singapore AIP eSUP 2018/013, Malaysia AIP SUP 02/25 and real DCLs decoded from the ACARS air (EDDF 2022/2023, EFHK/EGLL/LSZH 2025, WSSS/VHHH/VTBS/RKSI 2023). No official European AIP with a sample was found, so for Europe — real DCLs and the TopSky manual. USA — PDC through the airline host, with no pilot reply: fields per FAA JO 7110.113F, layout — a real Delta PDC (KMSP, 2025-12-29). Squawk tables: EUROCONTROL CAL v4.13, FAA JO 7110.66H (NBCAP), ICAO APAC eANP Vol II (Table ATM II-APAC-2). Real squawks fall within the computed blocks: EFHK 0437, EGLL 3403/4622, EDDF 0122, LSZH 3016, KMSP 3631, KPHL 2747/1772, WSSS 2214, VHHH 5132, VTBS 0747, RKSI 7135.

**User decisions (2026-10-09):**
1. The DCL station — `X` + the departure airport index (EDDF → XDDF), as in TopSky (the DCL station is the airport index). Polled on the ground until takeoff, until the exchange is finished.
2. Europe, Asia and other regions — ARINC 623 DCL in `cpdlc` packets (on the wire — as with Hoppie stations: values in `@…@`). USA — PDC telex from the same station, layout as Delta's: `PDC <flight> <IATA> <IATA>` / `**** PREDEPARTURE  CLEARANCE ****` / `<callsign> DEPARTING <ICAO>  TRANSPONDER <code>` / `SKED DEP TIME <hhmm>   EQUIP  <type>` / `FILED FLT LEVEL <level>` / `-CLEARED AS FILED-` / route / `CLEARED <SID> DEPARTURE <fix> TRSN` / `CLB VIA SID` / `EXP <level> 10 MIN AFT DP,DPFRQ <frequency>` / `XPCT RWY <runway>`. Without "EXC MAINT …" (the SID altitude is unknown) and without the equipment suffix. No SID — `UNABLE REVERT TO VOICE`.
3. Squawk: EUR — CAL (destination lines, civil, ENR; authority = country prefix, airport or departure FIR; direction; protection and via by destination and route FIRs; CCAMS pool). USA — NBCAP (ARTCC = the airport's FIR per VATSpy, domestic flight — destination in the same ARTCC, primary blocks first). APAC — FIR blocks (I/D), country per VATSpy `[Countries]`, restriction footnotes 11–16 are not used, Australia and New Zealand domestic — any code except international blocks. Outside the tables — a random code. Codes ending in 00 and special codes (ban list) are never issued.
4. Code 1000 (Mode S) is not assigned yet — it is only in the ban list. The Mode S rule (a real EDDF DCL issues `SQUAWK 1000`) — a separate `[AT RELEASE]` task.
5. The tables live in the repository as TS data with a link to the source, edition and date. NBCAP — public domain. The reuse terms of CAL (EUROCONTROL) and eANP (© ICAO) — `[AT RELEASE]` (`08`, decision 9).

**Assumptions (approved by the user 2026-10-09):**
1. The FSM "RCD RECEIVED" goes out immediately, CLD (and PDC in the US) — after 60–180 s, deterministic from the seed. The Singapore and Malaysia AIPs say "within 5 minutes".
2. A request is accepted at any time on the ground before takeoff (in the Singapore and Malaysia AIPs — no earlier than 20 min before TOBT).
3. If the pilot does not reply WILCO within 5 min, nothing is sent: there is no cancellation text in the sources.
4. The PDC number — from the seed, 001–999. The ADT/STARTUP lines are not written: no data. There is no initial altitude: it is not in the OFP.
5. NEXT FREQ (DCL) — live DEL, then GND from VATSIM; otherwise OurAirports `DEL`/`CLD`/`GND`; otherwise no line. DPFRQ (US) — live APP from VATSIM, otherwise OurAirports `DEP`/`APP`. OurAirports is community data (public domain), accuracy not guaranteed (for EDDF the real DCL gives 121.905, OurAirports has no such frequency).
6. The FIR names of the APAC table are matched to airport FIRs per VATSpy.dat `[Airports]` (China: ZBPE etc.; Japan: ACC areas RJTG/RJBG/RJDG; Australia: VATSpy sectors "Brisbane (…)" / "Melbourne (…)").

**Implementation.** Core: `packages/core/src/atc/dcl.ts` (request, FSM/CLD/CDA, US PDC, rejections), `packages/core/src/atc/squawk/` (`squawk.ts` — code selection and ban list, `eur-cal.ts`, `faa-nbcap.ts`, `icao-apac.ts` — generated tables). The DCL station works inside the XATC station (setting "XATC station"). Data: country and FIR of departure and destination from VATSpy, departure airport frequencies from OurAirports (`packages/data-sources/src/ourairports.ts`). In the spirit of the T23 decision "do not conflict with live controllers": if DEL/GND/TWR of the departure airport is online on VATSIM and the "Yield" setting is on, the request is rejected (FSM `RCD REJECTED / REVERT TO VOICE PROCEDURES`).

#### T25 — XATC: level, speed, Direct To requests, level logic
Replies to pilot requests: level, speed, Direct To — in CPDLC phraseology (CLIMB TO, UNABLE, DUE TO …). Level logic: the semicircular rule and the table of cruising levels (ICAO Annex 2, App. 3), RVSM FL290–410, exceptions by state (AIP; metric levels — `[AT RELEASE]`), MORA/MEA of the sector (from the SimBrief navlog: the `mora` field exists, checked below), aircraft ceiling from the OFP. Direct To — only to a route point ahead. A refusal "due to traffic" — an assumption, only with approval.
**Done when:** the table of levels and exceptions is under tests; an odd level in an "even" zone is rejected with the correct reason.

**Analysis (2026-10-09).**
- Pilot requests (FBW A32NX `CpdlcMessageElements.ts`, GPL-3.0, format only; on the network values are without `@`): DM6 `REQUEST <level>`, DM9 `REQUEST CLIMB TO <level>`, DM10 `REQUEST DESCEND TO <level>`, DM18 `REQUEST <speed>`, DM22 `REQUEST DIRECT TO <point>`. Level — `FL350`, `11000FT` or `8900M`; speed — `M.78` (FANS-A: M.61–M.92) or `280` / `280KT` (70–350 kt). FANS-A replies: UM0 `UNABLE`, UM20 `CLIMB TO <level>`, UM23 `DESCEND TO <level>`, UM74 `PROCEED DIRECT TO <point>`, UM106 `MAINTAIN <speed>` (all WU, except UNABLE), UM169 — free text (reasons `DUE TO …`). FBW's FANS-A set has no UM166/UM167 (`DUE TO TRAFFIC`, `DUE TO AIRSPACE RESTRICTION` — ATN B1, Doc 4444 Annex 5), their text goes as UM169.
- Table of levels: SERA, Appendix 3 (Reg. (EU) 923/2012; matches ICAO Annex 2, App. 3, table a, feet, RVSM): 000–179° — FL10, 30 … 290, 310 … 410, 450, 490; 180–359° — FL20, 40 … 280, 300 … 400, 430, 470, 510. The track angle is magnetic.
- Exceptions verified against the AIP: Spain (AIP ENR 1.7-3, AMDT 403/26 of 19-FEB-26) and Portugal (ENR 1.7.5, effective 01 OCT 2026) — 090–269° odd, 270–089° even, the same level grid. France — the same, but only for IFR in the UTA (AIC A 06/20, from 21 May 2020; table — AIP France ENR 1.3.5; the lower limit of the UTA and the text of ENR 1.3.5 — `[AT RELEASE]`). New Zealand (CAA NZ Part 91, amd. 12) — 270–089° odd, 090–269° even (mirroring Spain). Italy — the same as Spain, per the ENAV document on Free Route above FL335; the text of Italy's ENR 1.7 was not obtained — `[AT RELEASE]`.
- Metric levels (China, DPRK; Mongolia and the CIS countries moved to feet in different years): official AIP tables were not obtained, there are only secondary sources and the ICAO working paper A36-WP/176 (the Chinese-Russian RVSM table in meters) — `[AT RELEASE]`.
- SimBrief: every navlog point has `mora` (ft), `fir`, `fir_units`, `fir_valid_levels` (the levels permitted on the leg taking into account direction and route restrictions: on M750 in RJJJ — from FL270), `fir_crossing`, `track_mag`. In `general` — `stepclimb_string` (`RCTP/0390` = point/level in hundreds of feet; the format of several steps on a real OFP with a step climb — `[AT RELEASE]`). There is no aircraft ceiling in the OFP (`aircraft` has only the type, engines, equipment). The RVSM indicator is the letter `W` in `aircraft.equip` (FPL field 10a, Doc 4444, App. 2).
- Telemetry gives IAS, but not Mach and not magnetic heading: the magnetic track angle is taken from `track_mag` of the current navlog leg.
- Ceiling and VMO/MMO — EASA TCDS (verified 2026-10-09): A.064 A318–A321 iss. 62 (39 800 ft with mod. 30748, VMO 350 kt / MMO 0.82), A.004 A330 iss. 69 (41 450 ft), A.151 A350 iss. 33 (-900 43 100 ft, -1000 41 450 ft), IM.A.120 B737 iss. 29 (NG and MAX 41 000 ft), IM.A.003 B777 iss. 20.0 (43 100 ft), IM.A.115 B787 iss. 30 (-8/-9 43 100 ft, -10 41 100 ft, VMO 350 / MMO 0.90), IM.A.196 B747 iss. 19 (-400 45 100 ft, -8 43 100 ft, VMO 365 / MMO 0.90). A.110 A380 iss. 17 — "see AFM", no numbers.

**User decisions (2026-10-09):**
1. Permitted levels: parity — our own table (SERA App. 3 and the ES/PT/FR-UTA/NZ exceptions, other FIRs — per ICAO); route restrictions — SimBrief `fir_valid_levels` when the aircraft is on a navlog leg (if the intersection with our own table is empty — only our own table).
2. Metric areas (China, DPRK) — the ICAO feet table (assumption: in China the real levels are metric).
3. A refusal "due to traffic" (`UNABLE@_@DUE TO TRAFFIC`) — 10% of permissible level requests, from the seed, not twice in a row; a repeat of the same request after 5+ min is approved.

**Assumptions (approved by the user 2026-10-09):**
1. Ceiling and VMO/MMO — from the TCDS (list above; for A318–A321 — 39 800 ft, the most common modification 30748). A type without data — no check. Weight-dependent ceiling is not modeled. Below FL100 a speed > 250 kt is rejected everywhere (14 CFR 91.117; in SERA — not in all classes).
2. Direct To — only to a route point ahead, in the current FIR, not beyond the first STAR point; otherwise `UNABLE`.
3. The reply — in 20–60 s from the seed. An aircraft without `W` in the equipment — `UNABLE` on FL290–410.
4. Below the MORA of the current leg — `UNABLE` with no reason.
5. Italy — per ICAO until the text of Italy's AIP ENR 1.7 is obtained (`[AT RELEASE]`). France — the exception above FL195 (lower limit of the UTA `[AT RELEASE]`). New Zealand — FIR NZZC only.

**Implementation.** Core: `packages/core/src/atc/requests.ts` (request parsing and decision), `levels.ts` (table and state rules), `performance.ts` (TCDS), `route.ts` (route leg, STAR entry point). OFP: `OfpFix.trackMag/moraFt/fir/validLevelsFt`, `OfpAircraft.equip`. The XATC station replies only to the station the aircraft is logged on to (otherwise, as before, `MESSAGE NOT SUPPORTED`); the decision is made on receipt, the reply — after 20–60 s (a queue in `XatcState.req`, survives state saving). The `ATC_REQUEST` event — in the app feed and the replay report. If there is no telemetry — `UNABLE`. The magnetic track angle — from `track_mag` of the leg the aircraft is on; a leg without `track_mag` — no parity check.
#### T26 — XATC: random Direct To, step climb, initial descent altitude
Up to 3 random Direct To per flight (seeded Rng, only waypoints ahead on the route; the constraints are the approved assumptions below). Automatic step climb clearance at the OFP steps (`ALT_CHANGE` event, steps field `general.stepclimb_string`, below). Descent clearance with a logical initial altitude instead of the generic "descend via STAR": based on route, phase, MORA, and the destination transition level. The altitude selection algorithm is agreed as an assumption.
**Human check:** messages arrive on time in Fenix/FBW/PMDG and are accepted by the CPDLC page.

**Analysis (2026-10-09).** Available: the core's XATC station (T23) and the FANS-A phrases from T25 (`PROCEED DIRECT TO`, `CLIMB TO`, `DESCEND TO`; FBW also knows `WHEN ABLE PROCEED DIRECT TO`, `AT PILOTS DISCRETION`). Steps — `general.stepclimb_string` and `altitude_feet` changes in the navlog during the `CRZ` leg. For the initial descent altitude there are `mora` per waypoint, the destination `transLevelFt`, and navlog altitudes during the `DSC` leg (the SimBrief profile, not STAR constraints). The sources contain no real constraints on random Direct To: the choice of points is an assumption.

**Assumptions (approved by the user 2026-10-09):**
1. Random Direct To: 0–3 per flight from the seed; only in cruise and no closer than 50 NM to TOD; a point in the current FIR, 50–250 NM ahead, not SID/STAR, shortcut ≥ 2 NM; `PROCEED DIRECT TO @X@` (WU). *Replaced by the user's decision of 2026-10-10 (T38, "Along the way", item 4): the FIR exit point at first contact, 50 % per FIR, ≥ 5 NM; this assumption applies until that is implemented.*
2. Step climb: 2 min before the step point — `CLIMB TO @FLxxx@` (WU); not sent if the pilot has already requested that level or the level is not valid per the T25 table.
3. Descent: 20 NM before TOD. The initial altitude is the highest of: the SimBrief navlog altitude at the first STAR waypoint; MORA of the remaining route + 1000 ft; rounded up to 1000 ft. Above the destination transition level — `DESCEND TO @FL120@`, below it — `DESCEND TO @6000FT@`.

**Implementation.** Core: `packages/core/src/atc/auto.ts` (random Direct To points, point selection, steps, descent altitude), called from `XatcStation.autoClearances` (only for a logged-on aircraft with telemetry, CRUISE/DESCENT phases; state — `XatcState.auto`). OFP: `Ofp.stepClimbs` from `general.stepclimb_string` (`FIX/0410`, the first pair is the departure airport). Direct To points — 0–3 random distances along the route in [TOC, TOD − 50 NM]; when one is passed, a point is selected by the rules; if none is suitable, there is no Direct To. Points passed by more than 30 NM (start mid-flight) are discarded. A step passed by more than 20 NM is discarded. Descent is not sent if a lower level is already cleared or the computed level is not at least 1000 ft below the current altitude. If the destination has neither a transition level nor a transition altitude, the FL/feet boundary — see the decision below. The `ATC_AUTO` event is in the feed and the replay report.

**User decision (2026-10-09):** random Direct To can be switched on and off: core option `xatc.randomDirects` (on by default, written to the flight recording header), in the app — the "Random Direct To" setting. Step climb and descent do not depend on it.

**User decision (2026-10-09, option "b"):** if the OFP has neither a transition level nor a transition altitude for the destination, the FL/feet boundary is 18 000 ft only in the US and Canada (destination ICAO `K…`, `PA…`, `PH…`, `C…`), and a flight level (`FL080`) everywhere else. The Canadian Standard Pressure Region (flight levels at all altitudes) and other US territories (`PG…` etc.) are not distinguished — `[AT RELEASE]`. Code — `defaultTransitionFt` in `packages/core/src/atc/auto.ts`.

#### T27 — XATC: oceanic clearance
Request and issue of an oceanic clearance if the route enters an oceanic area (NAT, PACOTS, etc.). Format — from real samples (Shanwick/Gander/New York, data-link OCL). Track, entry point, time, level, Mach — from the OFP and current tracks. Request window — per the area's rules (all closed by the analysis of 2026-10-09, below).

**Analysis (2026-10-09).** Details — `06`, "NAT oceanic clearance". Per NAT OPS Bulletin 2023_001 rev 6 (25 Feb 2026), only Shanwick issues a clearance in the NAT; Gander, Santa Maria, Bodø accept the RCL without a clearance, New York and Reykjavik do not require an RCL. CLX format, errors and windows — NAT OPS Bulletin 2020_001 rev 01. The request in Hoppie is a FlyByWire CPDLC packet `OCEANIC REQUEST …` to the station the aircraft is logged on to. Tracks — natTrak (from NOTAMs). No clearance procedures were found for PACOTS and other areas.

**User decisions (2026-10-09):**
1. NAT only.
2. Response by the first oceanic area after the OEP: Shanwick — `RCL RECEIVED`, a CLX after 2–10 min, the pilot's ROGER = CLA → `CLA RECEIVED CLEARANCE CONFIRMED END OF MESSAGE`, no CLA for 10 min — cancellation; Gander, Santa Maria, Bodø — `RCL RECEIVED BY <ANSP>`; Reykjavik and New York — `RCL NOT REQUIRED BY <BIRD|KZWY>` (the exact text is unknown).
3. Responses are a telex from the station the RCL came to (the real CLX is ACARS, containing `/`).

**Assumptions (approved by the user 2026-10-09):**
1. The request window is based on our estimate of the time to the OEP (route distance / ground speed; without speed — the OFP average speed); the pilot's time goes only into `FM <OEP>/<ETO>`.
2. The CLX route — the OFP coordinate points after the OEP and the next named point (exit), as in the Shanwick example. `NAT <letter>` if the points match the active natTrak track (track validity — at the time of crossing 30W), otherwise `RANDOM ROUTE`. No reroutes.
3. Level: the requested one if the track has it (off a track — per the T25 table on the first oceanic leg); otherwise the nearest valid one not above MAX (on a tie — the lower) and `ATC/ LEVEL CHANGE`; if there is none — `RCL/CLA RECEIVED NEGOTIATION REQUIRED CONTACT SHANWICK BY VOICE`. Mach — as requested. No random rejections.
4. Entry point not on the route ahead, wrong callsign, Mach or level — `RCL REJECTED` with the reason from the bulletin.

**Implementation.** Core: `packages/core/src/atc/oceanic.ts` (`OclService`: RCL parsing, area, windows, CLX, CLA, reclearance with `ATC/ … RECLEARANCE n`), called from `XatcStation` (RCL and ROGER to any XATC station; state — `XatcState.ocl`). Tracks — `ExtDataUpdate.natTracks`, adapter `packages/data-sources/src/nattrak.ts` (source `nattracks`, only with an XATC station, once an hour). The `ATC_OCL` event is in the app feed and the replay report. The CLX number (`CLRNCE nnn`) is from the seed, like the PDC number in T24. On a route without a NAT area, an RCL gets `MESSAGE NOT SUPPORTED BY THIS ATS UNIT`, as before. Tests — on a synthetic EGLL–KJFK OFP using the real track E; a real transatlantic OFP (the `fir`, `tracks` fields, the type of coordinate points) — `[AT RELEASE]`.

#### T28 — Rocket launches near the route
TheSpaceDevs Launch Library adapter (version and limits — checked, below; a cache is mandatory), a scenario in `external/`: a launch within 300 NM of the route and in the flight time window. Real data only; no launch — no message. The data is written to the flight recording.
**Done when:** a fixture of a real response, a distance and window test, degradation without network.

**Analysis (2026-10-09).** Details — `06`, "External data". Current version — Launch Library **2.3.0** (`ll.thespacedevs.com/2.3.0/`), without a key 15 requests per hour per IP (the thespacedevs.com/llapi page and `/2.3.0/api-throttle/`). Pad coordinates are only in `mode=normal`. Statuses and T-0 precision — the reference lists `/2.3.0/config/launch_statuses/` and `/net_precisions/`. The data license is not stated on the site — `[AT RELEASE]`. There is no real format for such an airline message.

**User decision (2026-10-09):** launches with status Go **and those that have already happened** are reported (in flight, success, payload deployed, failure, partial failure).

**Assumptions (approved by the user 2026-10-09):**
1. A request at flight start and then hourly, 30-min cache, a 15-min pause after an error. The request window — from one hour before flight start to "now + OFP block time + 1 h" (the service does not know the remaining time; extra data does no harm). Time — real UTC. Without network — no message.
2. Go — only with T-0 precision of an hour or better; TBD, TBC, Hold are not taken.
3. One message per flight, in cruise: the pad is ≤ 300 NM from the route ahead; for Go — the launch window is not closed and T-0 is before the ETA, for a launch that happened — T-0 is after takeoff. If several — the nearest along the route.
4. Text — our own template (`SPACE LAUNCH NEAR ROUTE` / rocket / mission / pad / `NET hhmmZ WINDOW hhmm-hhmmZ` or `LAUNCHED hhmmZ <status>` / distances / `EXPECT AIRSPACE RESTRICTIONS CHECK NOTAM`, signature `SOURCE THESPACEDEVS`).

**Implementation.** Adapter — `packages/data-sources/src/launches.ts`, source `launches` in `ExternalDataService`. Core: `SpaceLaunch` and `ExtDataUpdate.launches` (`core/src/ext/data.ts`, pads farther than 300 NM are discarded on receipt, data in the flight recording), `ext.launch_*` variables (`scenario/context.ts`, `05`). Scenarios — `packages/scenarios/external/space_launch.yaml` (Go) and `space_launch_done.yaml`, mutually exclusive.

#### T29 — Special Events (sports match results, etc.)
Messages about real events (for example, results of major football matches). Source — an API with documented terms of use (`[VERIFY]` the license and whether a key is needed). The key — only user-entered or backend (invariant 2). Facts only from the API.

**User choice (2026-10-09).** Politics, elections, crime, and disasters with casualties are not covered.
- *Sports* (final results completed during the flight): football — World Cup, Euros, European cups (Champions League, Europa League, Conference League); NHL; Formula 1; WEC; Grand Slam tournaments; NBA; Olympic Games; Tour de France.
- *Aviation*: space weather (HF, GNSS, radiation on polar routes); aurora on the route; an eclipse visible from the route; volcanic ash; GNSS jamming zones; ATC strikes; airshows; "this day in aviation history".
- *Other*: a holiday in the destination country; a clock change on the day of the flight; New Year in flight; an earthquake near the destination airport; major events in the destination city.

**Source analysis (2026-10-09, responses verified by requests).**

| Event | Source | Key | Terms | Conclusion |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| Space weather | NOAA SWPC `services.swpc.noaa.gov/products/noaa-scales.json` (R/S/G scales now and forecast), `/products/alerts.json` (ALERT/WARNING texts) | no | US government data; the page has no separate license text `[AT RELEASE]` | ✅ There is no public feed of ICAO SWX Advisory (FNXX01 KWNP) — they go through SADIS/WIFS channels. The message is based on NOAA scales and alerts |
| Aurora | SWPC `/json/ovation_aurora_latest.json` (1°×1° grid, probability 0–100, forecast ~1 h) and `/products/noaa-planetary-k-index.json` | no | as above | ✅ |
| Eclipse | NASA "Five Millennium Canon", Besselian elements catalog (CSV) | no | "Permission is freely granted to reproduce…" with credit to the source | ✅ Computed in the core, no network; the table is in the repository |
| Volcanic ash | aviationweather.gov `/api/data/isigmet?format=json` — international SIGMETs, `hazard: "VA"`, polygon, base/top | no | already in use (T13), limit 100 requests/min | ✅ A real aviation product; better than VAAC bulletins (there is no public feed from London and Toulouse) |
| GNSS jamming zones | gpsjam.org — no API and no terms of use | — | — | ❌ No live source. An option — static regions from EASA SIB 2022-02 (with revisions) — an assumption |
| ATC strikes | EUROCONTROL NOP — web pages only (Headline News), no feed; the primary source is NOTAMs (FAA NOTAM API — needs a key) | — | — | ❌ No source without a key |
| Airshows | no API | — | — | ⚠️ Only our own reference of dates from official sites, updated once a year — an assumption |
| Aviation history | no API | — | — | ⚠️ Our own reference: fact + date + source — the content is to be agreed |
| Holidays | Nager.Date `date.nager.at/api/v3/PublicHolidays/{year}/{country}` | no | "may be used in private and non-commercial projects", for commercial use — sponsorship | ✅ For personal use; commercial — `[AT RELEASE]` |
| Clock change | Needs the airport's time zone identifier (IANA). The OFP has only the `timezone` offset (`9`) — it has no transition rules | — | — | ⚠️ Needs an "airport → IANA zone" source (candidates: OpenFlights airports.dat, ODbL, not updated for a long time; timezone-boundary-builder, ODbL) — not verified |
| New Year in flight | UTC and the `timezone` offsets of departure/destination from the OFP | — | — | ⚠️ Midnight in the zones flown over needs the same time zone boundaries; without them — only UTC and the local time of departure/destination (an assumption) |
| Earthquakes | USGS `earthquake.usgs.gov/earthquakes/feed/v1.0/summary/4.5_day.geojson` (updated once a minute) | no | USGS — `[AT RELEASE]` (usually public domain, text not checked) | ✅ |
| City events | no free source without a key found | — | — | ❌ |
| Formula 1 | Jolpica (successor of Ergast) `api.jolpi.ca/ergast/f1/current/last/results.json` | no | non-commercial use, data CC BY-NC-SA 4.0 | ✅ For personal use |
| WEC | FIA publishes classifications only as PDF; no JSON API found | — | — | ❌ No source |
| Football, NHL, NBA, tennis | ESPN `site.api.espn.com/apis/site/v2/sports/…/scoreboard` — works without a key, but unofficial | no | Disney/ESPN terms (item 2.B) **prohibit** automated access | ❌ Not used |
| NHL | `api-web.nhle.com/v1/score/now` — the league's official domain, no key, no documentation and no terms | no | terms not published | ⚠️ Assumption |
| Football, NBA, tennis, Tour de France | TheSportsDB, free key `123` (public, not a secret) | — | the free key is "for your own projects", publishing in app stores — paid only; 30 requests/min; on the free key the queries are cut down (past league events — 1 record, season — 15); the data is entered by community editors | ⚠️ Coverage incomplete, accuracy not guaranteed |
| football-data.org | World Cup, Euros, Champions League | yes | free tier with a key | ❌ 403 without a key |
| Olympics | no free official medal-table API found (Sportradar is commercial) | — | — | ❌ |

**User decisions (2026-10-09):**
1. Excluded (no source): WEC, Olympics, ATC strikes, city events.
2. Excluded: football (World Cup, Euros, European cups), NBA, Grand Slam, Tour de France — no reliable source without a key (ESPN prohibits automated access, TheSportsDB on the free key is cut down and filled by the community).
3. NHL — `api-web.nhle.com` (the league's official domain, no documentation): acceptable.
4. GNSS jamming — excluded.
5. Airshows and "this day in aviation history" — the references are compiled during T29: each fact or date with a source (the airshow's official site, the primary source of the event) and a verification date, the data file in the repository; the finished list is shown to the user in the T29 report.
6. Clock change and New Year in flight — excluded.

**Final T29 scope:** sports — Formula 1 (Jolpica), NHL; aviation — space weather, aurora, eclipse, volcanic ash (SIGMET VA), airshows, aviation history; other — a holiday in the destination country, an earthquake near the destination airport.

**Assumptions (approved by the user 2026-10-09; 7 and 8 — as worded by the user):**
1. No more than one T29 message per flight (except space weather and ash — they concern safety and go separately), only in cruise, no closer than 30 min to TOD. Each group (sports, aviation, other) is switched on in the settings.
2. Time — real UTC (as in T28); data — in the flight recording.
3. Text — our own template, signature `OPS` (there is no real format for airline "news"), in upper case, diacritics transliterated (Ü → U, É → E, ß → SS).
4. Space weather: a message at NOAA scale G3+, S2+ or R3+ (current or in the 24-hour forecast); the correspondence to ICAO MOD/SEV levels is not 1:1 — we write the NOAA scales. Radiation — only on routes above 60° latitude.
5. Aurora: probability ≥ 30 % at a route point ahead, at night (sun below the horizon).
6. Eclipse: within the flight window a route point falls into the band of a partial (phase ≥ 0.5) or total eclipse, with the sun (moon) above the horizon.
7. Ash: a SIGMET VA polygon closer than 300 NM to the route ahead (as with `natural_event`). Not linked to `natural_event`: both messages may arrive.
8. Earthquake: M ≥ 5.0 within 100 km of the destination airport in the last 24 h. Not linked to `natural_event`: both messages may arrive.
9. Holiday: only nationwide (`global: true`) in the destination country on the arrival day by local time.

**API check during implementation (2026-10-09).** `noaa-scales.json`: key `0` — current R/S/G scales, `-1` — the maximum for the past day, `1`–`3` — forecast for today and two days, in the forecast only G has a scale (R has probabilities `MinorProb`/`MajorProb`, S — `Prob`). NHL `/v1/score/now` — a 307 redirect to `/v1/score/<date>` (the client forbids redirects), there is no match end time. Jolpica: race end = start + the winner's `Time.millis`. SIGMET VAs also come as forecast polygons (a series with `F`), geometry `AREA` and `AREAS`. Besselian elements — the NASA CSV `eclipse_besselian_from_mysqldump2.csv` ("Permission is freely granted to reproduce… acknowledgment of the source").

**Implementation assumptions (approved by the user 2026-10-09):**
1. Space weather: R and S — by the current scale (`0`), G — by the current scale or the forecast for today and tomorrow (`1`, `2`); probabilities are not used.
2. Aurora: only route points the aircraft will reach within 60 min (by ground speed); night — the center of the sun is below the geometric horizon at the point at the moment of passing.
3. Eclipse: an element table for 2025–2035 in the repository (generated, with the source); route points every 10 NM, passing time by ground speed, without it — by the OFP plan.
4. Ash: SIGMET VAs in effect now (observation and forecast); levels are not filtered (base/top are in the text); the message is for the nearest volcano.
5. Destination country for the holiday — OurAirports `airports.csv` (public domain, 12.7 MB, 30-day cache); the arrival date — local, by the OFP `timezone`.
6. NHL: a match finished during the flight if it was not final in this flight's update but is `FINAL`/`OFF` in the update after takeoff. Regular season and playoffs only; up to 8 matches in a message.
7. Airshows: the destination airport within 50 km of the show site, the local arrival date within the show days; the 2026–2027 reference from official sites.
8. Aviation history: a fact with the day and month of the current UTC date; if several — selected from the seed.
9. The "Sports", "Aviation", "Other" groups — switches in the app, on by default; a disabled group disables its scenarios and requests to its sources.
10. Requests: NOAA (scales, aurora) — once every 30 min, SIGMET — 15 min, USGS — 30 min, Jolpica — 30 min, NHL — 15 min, Nager and OurAirports — once per flight. Signature `OPS`, the source — the last line of the body.

**Implementation.** Adapters — `packages/data-sources/src/{spacewx,sigmet,quakes,holidays,sport}.ts`, sources `spacewx`, `aurora`, `ash`, `quakes`, `holidays`, `f1`, `nhl` in `ExternalDataService` (`SPECIAL_GROUPS`, only for the groups from `startFlight(…, { special })`). Core: types and astronomy — `core/src/ext/special.ts` (sun altitude, local eclipse circumstances from Besselian elements; checked against the NASA catalog: 2026-08-12 and 2027-08-02 — magnitude and sun altitude at the point of greatest phase), `ExtDataUpdate` fields and route binding — `ext/data.ts`, `ext.*` variables — `scenario/special-vars.ts` (`05`). Core tables: `ext/eclipses.ts` (24 eclipses 2025–2035, generator `tools/eclipse-table/generate.mjs`), `ext/airshows.ts` (7 shows), `ext/aviation-history.ts` (25 facts). Scenarios — 10 files in `packages/scenarios/external/` (`05`). App — the "Special events" settings (three groups), `apps/desktop/src/main/special.ts`. Tests — `core/test/special.test.ts`, `data-sources/test/special.test.ts`, `scenarios/test/special.test.ts`, `desktop/test/controller.test.ts`.

**Airshow reference** (dates — organizers' pages, place — the aerodrome from OurAirports, checked 2026-10-09): Airshow China, Zhuhai, 7–13.12.2026 (airshow.com.cn; aggregators say 10–15.11 — not used); Aero India, Yelahanka, 8–12.02.2027 (aeroindia.gov.in); Avalon, 23–28.02.2027 (airshow.com.au); Paris Air Show, Le Bourget, 14–20.06.2027 (siae.fr); RIAT, Fairford, 16–18.07.2027 (airtattoo.com); EAA AirVenture, Oshkosh, 26.07–01.08.2027 (eaa.org); Dubai Airshow, DWC, 15–19.11.2027 (dubaiairshow.aero). Not included: past 2026 shows, Farnborough (the next show's date is not published), Seoul ADEX 2027 (start date only), Bahrain (no dates on the site); ILA 2028 (17–21.05.2028) — outside the 2026–2027 range.

**Aviation history reference** (checked 2026-10-09): Smithsonian NASM — Wright Flyer 17.12.1903, Bell X-1 14.10.1947, Lindbergh's takeoff 20.05.1927; ICAO — Chicago Convention 07.12.1944, ICAO 04.04.1947; Boeing "Select Products in Boeing History" — first flights of the 707 (20.12.1957), 727 (09.02.1963), 737 (09.04.1967), 747 (09.02.1969), 767 (26.09.1981), 757 (19.02.1982), 777 (12.06.1994), 787 (15.12.2009), 747-8I (20.03.2011), DC-3 (17.12.1935), DC-8 (30.05.1958), DC-9 (25.02.1965), DC-10 (29.08.1970); the H-4 flight (02.11.1947), the start of the Douglas World Cruisers (17.03.1924), airmail Model 40A (01.07.1927), the first 787 for ANA (26.09.2011); Airbus — first flights of the A300B1 (28.10.1972), A320 (22.02.1987), A380 (27.04.2005). The A350 (14.06.2013) is not included: the date is only in an Airbus presentation, not in a press release.

#### T30 — CONNEX schedule: airline connecting flights
**Reopened 2026-10-15 (user decision):** AeroDataBox, the key is entered by the user. The cancellation of 2026-10-09 (option "d") was based on "a key in the client is prohibited", but invariant 2 allows a key entered by the user and stored in the OS store (`apps/desktop/src/main/secrets.ts`).

Statement: before arrival — a list of real connecting flights of the same airline from the destination: flight, direction, time, gate. The source is AeroDataBox (RapidAPI). A real flight replaces the synthetic one in `short_connection`/`connection_*` (T42 item 12).

**Source (checked 2026-10-15).** AeroDataBox, OpenAPI specification v1.15.4.0 (`doc.aerodatabox.com/docs/openapi-rapidapi-v1.json`): FIDS `GET /flights/airports/icao/{ICAO}/{fromLocal}/{toLocal}` — Tier 2, 2 units; RapidAPI — `https://aerodatabox.p.rapidapi.com/`, headers `X-RapidAPI-Key`, `X-RapidAPI-Host: aerodatabox.p.rapidapi.com`. Plans (`aerodatabox.com/pricing`): Basic on RapidAPI — free forever, 400 units/month (≈200 FIDS requests), 1 request/s, FIDS window ≤ 12 h, schedule up to 365 days ahead; over the quota requests are rejected (no overage charges). Flight fields: `number`, `airline.icao`, `codeshareStatus` (`IsOperator`/`IsCodeshared`/`Unknown`), `status`, `isCargo`, `movement.airport.icao`, `movement.scheduledTime.utc`, `movement.terminal`, `movement.gate`, `movement.quality`. Terms (`aerodatabox.com/terms`, rev. 2026-10-11): item 5.3 — commercial use only via a paid channel; item 5.4 — on the free channel AeroDataBox attribution is mandatory; item 5.5 — data storage no longer than 7 days (item 5.8: identifiers — flight number, airport code — indefinitely); items 6.1.2, 6.1.5 — the key is not passed to third parties; item 5.2(a) — a ban on real navigation and flight operations (a simulator is not affected); item 2.4 — the marketplace terms govern payment only.

**Assumptions (approved by the user 2026-10-15, unchanged):**
- **C1. User's key.** The user signs up on RapidAPI and enters their own key; the key is stored in `SecretStore` (`safeStorage`) and goes only in the header to `aerodatabox.p.rapidapi.com`. We do not receive the key and do not resell data: the AeroDataBox client is the user, the app is their program. The terms do not describe this scenario directly; the interpretation — allowed (the key is not passed to anyone, only the owner sees the data).
- **C2. Delivery via Hoppie.** A message with a connection (flight number, direction, STD, gate) goes through the Hoppie server to the user's own aircraft — considered use of the data by the user, not distribution to third parties.
- **C3. Non-commercial use.** An explanation in the settings: a key on the free plan is for personal non-commercial use only (item 5.3).
- **C4. Attribution.** The last line of the body of every message with this data is `SRC AERODATABOX`; in the settings next to the key field — a link to aerodatabox.com.
- **C5. One request per flight** at flight start (as soon as the OFP is known). The window — local time of the destination airport from STA+30 min to STA+4 h; `direction=Departure`, `withCargo=false`, `withPrivate=false`, `withCancelled=false`, `withLeg=false`. Local time — from `ofp.destination.utcOffsetH`, the date — from the OFP (not the current one). Retry — once, after a pause, on 5xx or timeout; on 429 and 401 — no requests until the end of the flight. No gate update before TOD.
- **C6. Flight selection.** Candidates — departures of the same airline (`airline.icao == ofp.airlineIcao`); if a flight exists both as operating and as codeshare, the operating one (`IsOperator`). How `airline` and `codeshareStatus` are actually filled for codeshare flights — **[VERIFY]** on a live response (human check). The flight for `irr.connect_flight` — by seed among the candidates of the first 2 h after STA+30; if empty there — among the whole window.
- **C7. The passenger count is synthetic:** `irr.connect_pax` is still from `ops.transfer_pax` (no API has the real load).
- **C8. Variables and scenario.** `irr.connect_dest` (where the connecting flight goes), `irr.connect_gate` (gate or terminal, if known). A new scenario `connex_list`: at 120 NM before TOD — up to 4 departures of the airline with direction, STD and gate. `short_connection` and `connection_*` use the real flight.
- **C9. Determinism and storage.** The needed response fields go into the core input `CoreInput.data` and the flight recording (replay is deterministic). At app start, this data is purged from recordings older than 7 days, flight numbers and airport codes remain (item 5.8); replay of such a recording goes via the fallback, `--check` warns. There is no disk cache — in memory per flight only.
- **C10. Fallback.** Without a key, on error, on a 204 response, without candidates, without `airlineIcao` or `utcOffsetH` — as before: `ONWARD FLT` from OpenFlights or nothing. The test flag `syntheticConnections` remains.
- **C11. RapidAPI only.** API.Market (the free plan there is a trial) and direct access (from $19/month) — `[AT RELEASE]`; the base address and the key header are changed in one place of the adapter.

### Priority 2

#### T31 — Data link simulation: VHF, satellites, SATCOM, delay
The core chooses a ground VHF station in line of sight or (with SATCOM in the aircraft profile) a satellite and sets the delivery delay. The VHF station database — from verifiable sources (`[VERIFY]`: an open authoritative SITA/ARINC list may not exist — then only with approval). Satellites: Inmarsat (GEO), Iridium (CelesTrak TLE + SGP4, the library license `[VERIFY]`). Delays — from real data. TLE and the database — in the flight recording.

**Assumptions (approved by the user 2026-10-15, unchanged; per `docs/_drafts/T31-T32-research.md`, A1–A11):**
- A1–A2: a VHF "station" — the nearest OurAirports airport within the radio horizon (`1.23·√h_ft` NM, ceiling ~200 NM); marked "notional station". Check the formula against a source before implementation.
- A3: an Inmarsat GEO satellite — by longitude and elevation angle (> 5°); positions 54°W, 15.5°W, 64.5°E, 178°E, 98°W, 25°E, 143.5°E.
- A4, A6, A10: Iridium, TLE and SGP4 **moved out of T31** to a separate card (CelesTrak/Space-Track terms and the ACARS-over-Iridium protocol not clarified). When it comes up: SGP4 (`satellite.js`, MIT) outside the core, positions in `CoreInput.data`; do not commit or distribute TLE.
- A5: VHF delays 2–10 s, Inmarsat 5–30 s — configurable constants without a source, deterministic jitter from `Rng`.
- A8: GES code — from the L-band table in the research; for the ambiguous `02` — the first in the table.

**Done (2026-10-15).** `packages/core/src/datalink/link.ts` (pure TS): `chooseLink` — VHF (the nearest airport from the route corridor within `min(200, 1.23·√h_ft)` NM; on the ground 20 NM), otherwise with `datalink.satcom` the Inmarsat satellite with the highest elevation angle ≥ 5° (7 positions of A3), otherwise no link; `LinkChannel` — a queue: a message waits for a link, then for the VHF 2–10 s / SATCOM 5–30 s delay (A5, `DELAY_MS` constants, deterministic jitter from `Rng` `<seed>/#link`, FIFO), and leaves with a `link` tag (`kind`, `station`, `delayMs`, `waitedMs`); without telemetry messages pass as before. `CoreOptions.datalink`, `CoreState.link` (persisted), `AcarsCore.linkView` (the current link, how many are waiting/in transit — for the T32 window). Stations — `ExtDataUpdate.vhf` (in the flight recording, `ExtState.vhf`); adapter `fetchVhfStations` (OurAirports `airports.csv`), source `vhf` in `ExternalDataService` (`startFlight(…, { datalink: true })`). App: the "Data link simulation" and "Aircraft has SATCOM" settings (off / on by default), `header.datalink` in the recording, replay.
**Clarification of A2 (approved by the user 2026-10-15):** the formula `1.23·√h` gives 0 on the ground, so on the ground the VHF radius is a constant 20 NM (otherwise there is no link at the gate). Stations — only large and medium airports with scheduled service, in a 300 NM corridor around the planned track; outside the corridor (for example, a diversion far to the side) SATCOM or waiting remains. The radio horizon formula `1.23·√h` is a well-known approximation, I did not look for a primary source to cite.
**Human check:** switch on "Data link simulation" in the settings, fly a flight with live data: messages arrive 2–30 s later than with the setting off; over the ocean without SATCOM messages do not arrive until a station appears.
**Not included (per A4/A6/A10):** Iridium, TLE and SGP4 — a separate future card.
#### T32 — AES/GES headers and the data link status window
Dynamic header based on real samples (`AES:… GES:… .<reg>`), aircraft ICAO hex (entered in the profile, or a source `[VERIFY]`), a wrapping rule for narrow displays. Window in the app: station or satellite, transmission time. Depends on T31.

**Assumptions (approved by the user 2026-10-15, unchanged):**
- A7: the AES/GES header is a decoder format, not a cockpit one; it is shown in the status window, and in the message text only by option, off by default.
- A9: ICAO hex is entered in the aircraft profile; without it no header is output. Open databases (OpenSky, ADS-B Exchange) are not used until `[AT RELEASE]`.
- A11: the header is wrapped by tokens (`AES:…` / `GES:… 2` / `.REG`); a token longer than a line gets the formatter's hard wrap; the header goes through the formatter (invariant 9).

**Done (2026-10-15).** Core: `aesHeaderTokens` (`AES:<hex> GES:<code> 2 .<reg>` — the format of decoder samples), `gesCode` (table A8: AOR-W 43, AMER 05, APAC 50, EMEA 90; AOR-E, IOR and POR have no code — so no header, we do not invent a code), `linkHeaderLines` / `withLinkHeader` in `format/formatter.ts` (wrapping by tokens, hard wrap of a long token, repeated pagination; messages are still created only by the formatter). `OutboundMessage.link.header`; `AcarsCore.linkView` now returns `header` and `last` (when the last message went out and how long the link held it). `CoreOptions.datalink`: `icaoHex`, `headerInText` (off by default; the header only on SATCOM, not on VHF and not for CPDLC). App: settings "Aircraft ICAO address (6 hex digits)" (empty = no header; open databases are not used) and "Include AES/GES header in message text"; a "Data link" row in the status panel (VHF station and distance / SATCOM satellite and elevation / no link, the header, how much is waiting and in flight, the time and delay of the last message); the record's `header.datalink` stores `icaoHex` and `headerInText`.
**Human check:** in settings enter the ICAO address of your aircraft and turn the link on; over the ocean / Pacific the status panel shows a SATCOM row with the satellite and the header `AES:… GES:… 2 .REG`; with the "in text" option the first lines of the message on the CDU are the header, wrapped by tokens on a narrow display.
**T31/T32 clarifications (approved by the user 2026-10-15):** on the ground the VHF radius is 20 NM; stations are large and medium airports with scheduled flights within a 300 NM corridor; the satellite's first GES code from the table, no header for AOR-E/IOR/POR; one ICAO address for all aircraft; formula `1.23·√h` without verification against the primary source.
**Known limits:** the ICAO address is one for all aircraft (not per registration); AOR-E, IOR and POR satellites have no header until a source of GES codes is found.


#### T33 — Fire at the destination airport
Irregular scenario: a fire near the runway, effect on visibility, a recommendation to prepare a diversion, continuation after ~20 min. Conditions (runway from the OFP/METAR, wind) are real.
**Done (2026-10-15).** `irregular/dest_fire.yaml`: 120 NM before the destination, probability 0.02, only if the planned runway and the destination METAR wind are known. Telex: a fire at the runway, the smoke drift bearing and the wind from the METAR, "VIS REDUCED IN SMOKE", prepare a diversion to the alternate; reply HOLD (default, `flight.hold: 20`, issued by XATC) or DIVERT; after 20 min — "UNDER CONTROL, ARRIVALS RESUMED".
**Assumptions (approved by the user 2026-10-15):** 1) the visibility figure is not given in the text — "reduced by smoke" without metres, the actual visibility is visible to the pilot in the METAR/simulator; 2) the side of the runway where the fire is is not stated (no source); 3) 20 minutes and probability 0.02 — from the card and by analogy with `drone_activity`; 4) the "fire" is a random event, not data of a real incident (like the other irregular scenarios).
**Rework (user request 2026-10-16): random point and a fire before departure.** Step `smoke: { start: orig | dest, spread_nm: [0.2, 2.5] }` (`05`, T34): the point is a random bearing and distance from the middle of the planned runway, the place in the text is `run.fire_at` (`FIRE REPORTED / 1.2NM NW OF RWY 24R`, ≤ 0.5 NM — `NEAR RWY 24R`). `dest_fire` moved to a random point. New `irregular/dep_fire.yaml`: trigger — planned OUT − 30 min, then a random moment 0–45 min, while the aircraft is on the stand or taxiing (PREFLIGHT–TAXI_OUT; during the takeoff roll the scenario does not run); "DEPARTURES SUSPENDED", updates every 20 min, after 20–90 min "UNDER CONTROL, DEPARTURES RESUMED"; smoke at the departure airfield (the bridge creates it immediately — the aircraft is closer than 40 NM), removed on the all-clear or goes away after departure (> 60 NM); excludes `atc_flow_delay`, `deicing_holdover`.
**Assumptions (approved by the user 2026-10-16):** 1) a radius of 0.2–2.5 NM from the middle of the planned runway — "on the airport grounds or next to it"; land/water is not checked (no data), at coastal airports the point may end up in the water; 2) a fire before departure — on the stand and while taxiing, from −30 min before the planned departure until the takeoff roll; 3) a delay of 20–90 min, probability 0.02 (changeable in the app). My clarification: the start window is 0–45 min after the trigger (up to OUT + 15 min — typical taxi), the flight delay (`flight.delay_min`) is not set by the scenario — the pilot reports it himself (`DELAY`).
**Fixes after the human check (2026-10-16):** 1) "Run now" waited for a random moment of 0–45 min — now on a manual start the fire happens immediately (`run.forced`); 2) the manual-start telex stood in the general queue behind the flight-start messages (one per `minGapMs`) — the first telex of a manual start goes ahead, like a reply to the pilot; 3) at LEBL the point "1.4NM S OF RWY 24L" ended up in the sea, no smoke visible. **User decision (2026-10-16), replaces assumption 1:** the point along the planned runway — up to 0.8 NM from the middle along the axis in both directions and 0.1–0.3 NM sideways, the side random; no new data, at coastal runways water remains possible but rare. Text: `FIRE REPORTED / SE SIDE OF RWY 24L`. The runway direction is true, from the OurAirports threshold coordinates. 4) A smoke object created by the bridge a few seconds after the flight loaded stands in the sim but the effect does not start on it; the same object created later does smoke (checked by a human 2026-10-16, MSFS 2024, LEBL; EX1 is not needed — the ordinary `AICreateSimulatedObject` works). A 60 s wait after loading (user decision 2026-10-16) did not help: an object created after it also has no smoke, and a recreated one has smoke (checked by a human the same day). The smoke is not received by the first object of the session, not an early one. **Chosen by default (the user did not choose an option, the recommended one was taken, 2026-10-16):** the first object of a session or flight is created by the bridge immediately and recreated once after 15 s (`SmokeController.RespawnAfter`); the 60 s wait was removed. Also: a new `smoke_start` with a different point moves the already created object.
**Human check:** MSFS 2024, the smoke package in Community, the bridge running; restart the app; at LEBL on the stand "Run now" `dep_fire` — a telex within ~1 min, a smoke column at runway 24L on the side given in the telex, no farther than ~0.3 NM from the axis; the same with `dest_fire` closer than 40 NM to the destination.


#### T34 — Effects in the simulator: a smoke column accounting for wind
Per T33 the bridge creates an object with a smoke effect at the runway (SimConnect, a package for Community). Wind comes from the simulator. The performance impact is limited. Depends on T21, T33.
**Human check:** MSFS 2024, the T33 scenario: the smoke is visible from afar, drifts with the wind, FPS does not drop noticeably.
**User decision (2026-10-15):** our own SimObject package for Community with a smoke FX, spawn via `AICreateSimulatedObject_EX1`, runway coordinates from OurAirports, wind and drift — the effects engine, human check in MSFS.
**Done (2026-10-15).** Scenario step `smoke: start|stop` (core → a `smoke` command with the airport and runway, into the record and replay); `dest_fire` turns the smoke on with the first telex and puts it out after "UNDER CONTROL" (on DIVERT the smoke stays). The app (`controller.ts`) takes the runway midpoint from the OurAirports `runways.csv` (`ExternalDataService.runwayPoint`, 30-day cache; no runway or network — a line in the feed) and sends the bridge `smoke_start {lat, lon}`; `smoke_stop` — by the scenario, on flight stop and on exit. Bridge: commands in `CommandCatalog`, `SmokeController` (create on approach ≤ 40 NM, remove > 60 NM, after 90 min, on stop; in a new SimConnect session create again), `SimConnectSource.ApplySmoke`. The package is a draft `bridge/msfs/package/` (README there).
**Deviation from the decision:** `AICreateSimulatedObject_EX1` is absent from the local SimConnect wrapper (`SimConnectLibs/sdk2024`, checked 2026-10-15; `SimConnect.dll` exports only `AICreateSimulatedObject`), and the ordinary call works only with non-modular objects. So the object is a non-modular `aircraft.cfg` container and the ordinary function is called; with the official SDK (T19) the call is replaced with `_EX1` in one place (`ApplySmoke`).
**Assumptions (approved by the user 2026-10-15):** 1) the smoke stands at the middle of the planned runway (which side is burning is unknown, as in T33); 2) the object is created on approach ≤ 40 NM (farther the scene is not loaded — not checked), removed > 60 NM; 3) it lives no more than 90 min; 4) `Altitude = 0`, `OnGround = 1` (the SDK does not describe their relation); 5) the look of the smoke has to be made in the Visual Effects Editor — `FX_GUID` in `ACARS_Smoke.xml` is still zero.
**Human check (clarification):** see `bridge/msfs/package/README.md`, "To finish": create the effect, build the package, put it in Community, run `dest_fire` closer than 40 NM to the airfield — the smoke at the runway is visible from afar, leans with the wind, FPS does not sag.
**User decision (2026-10-16): smoke without wind.** The `WindDirection` node in the effect (MSFS 2024 1.9.18) gave about zero in the human check: with a ground wind of 135–150 kt (the "Custom settings" weather) the column stayed vertical, although the same speed path with a constant vector did tilt the smoke; the `Raw`/`UseCFD` options and connecting into the Update block did not help or did not connect. The user gave up the drift: the column is vertical in any wind. The `dest_fire` telex still writes `SMOKE DRIFTING <bearing>` from the METAR — that is text, the smoke in the simulator does not repeat it. The fallback (the object rotates with the wind from the METAR, the effect in local axes) is not being done.
**Effect (set up by a human 2026-10-16, based on the SDK SimpleFX sample "EngineSmoke", second iteration from the original sample):** Emitter `Rate 8`, `Capacity 800`, `MaxDistanceEmission 80000` (the field limit is not checked); Init `Lifetime 90`, `Size 9`; Update: size = (9 + 90 × curve(age) 0→1) × random 0.75–1.25, speed = spread ±0.9 × curve 0.3→5 + `GravityVector`, scale −16 × curve 1→0.25 × random 0.75–1.25 + wind × 0.305 × curve 0.15→1 (the wind contribution ≈ 0, see above); colour — curve 0.02→0.11 × random 0.7–1.2, opacity — curve 0→0.9 (2 %)→0.85→0.6→0. Result: `EngineSmoke.xml`, `InstanceId {88CDD86E-…}` (the SDK sample GUID). Observations of the first iteration: the column height barely grew with the climb speed in Init, only with the lifetime; stopping an effect instance with the Stop button in the debugger once crashed MSFS.
**Object (2026-10-16):** the MSFS 2024 `aircraft.cfg` container showed in the SimObject Spawner but was not created; the working variant is `sim.cfg` with `category=StaticObject` in `SimObjects\Landmarks` (modelled on the SDK Windsock sample), the model is one empty node without geometry (a hand-written triangle with a `data:` buffer did not compile). The smoke on the object broke off when the camera moved away — `DistanceToNotAnimate=80000` was added, after that no pauses. The `ASOBO_GT_FX` template is in `Asobo\Generic\FX.xml` of the SDK 2024.
**Why the smoke did not come from Community (found 2026-10-17):** the effect and all its nodes carried the SDK sample GUID (`{88CDD86E-…}`, name `EngineSmokeSample`). From Community the object was created (checked via SimConnect: it stands on the ground at the runway), but there was no effect; with the project open in DevMode the smoke came at once on an already standing object. After replacing all the effect's GUIDs with our own (73 of them, effect `{8C8B2356-…}`, name `AcarsSmokeColumn`, `FX_GUID` in `ACARS_Smoke.xml` — the same) and rebuilding, the smoke from Community is visible without DevMode (human check, LEBL, `dep_fire` "Run now"). Probably the same GUID exists in the stock MSFS packages (they are encrypted, cannot be checked directly). Rule: for any effect based on an SDK sample — new GUIDs for all nodes. Particle speed is in Update (version 19:55; moving it into Init was not checked and was rolled back). The effect path in the package is `VisualEffectLibs\<company>\<group>` (Asobo, DevSupport 15309); the draft `bridge/msfs/package` now has `VisualEffectLibs\ACARS\` — one level, to be fixed when building the package.
**License `[VERIFY]` before distribution:** MSFS SDK EULA (11/2019), item 1 b: SDK samples (sample content) are for internal use only, external distribution is prohibited. The `vfx_smoke.png` texture and the graph are taken from SimpleFX — such a package must not be put in the repository/release; our own texture is needed (and our own effect with new GUIDs).

#### T35 — Editor for custom messages
UI: the send condition (engine triggers) and drag-and-drop of data fields (`ofp.*`, `wx.*`, `ext.*`) → YAML validated against the schema, through the formatter. Based on T20.

#### T36 — CTAF in the VATSIM message
For US airports without an online controller — CTAF from a frequency source (FAA NASR or OurAirports, license and verification date `[VERIFY]`).

#### T37 — Funny messages
A set of humorous scenarios with low probability. No real people, no insults, not in critical phases (sterile cockpit).

#### T38 — `drone_activity` probability by destination country
A multiplier on the `probability` of the `irregular/drone_activity` scenario by the destination airport's country (levels A–D below). Result = base (from the file or a manual "%") × multiplier, not more than 1. Only for `drone_activity`; other scenarios do not use the field. In settings ("Scenarios", under the "%" field of `drone_activity`) — a table of countries and multipliers: change, add, delete; empty — values from the file. Differences from the file and the flight's resulting probability go into the record header, replay applies them.
**Done when:** tests of the levels on real airports (UUEE, EDDM, EKCH, OMDB, KJFK, RJBB), country unknown, a manual "%" together with multipliers, replay with a changed table; a settings test in `apps/desktop/test`.

**Analysis (2026-10-09).** The live version (real closures at the time of the flight) was **rejected by the user**: no source key for the whole world. FAA NAS Status — US only. FAA NOTAM API — the `api.faa.gov` portal → MyAccess/Login.gov, an external account got HTTP 401 on `am-gateway.apic4e.faa.gov`. ICAO API Data Service — an account is needed. SkyLink (RapidAPI) — an intermediary, coverage outside the US not confirmed. SWIFT (`portal.swim.faa.gov`) — a Solace JMS stream, inconvenient for a desktop. GDELT — noise. NOTAMs from the SimBrief OFP (`origin/destination/alternate.notam[]` with `notam_qcode`) — a snapshot at the time of the OFP; not used (possibly a separate card).
