There is no statistic of "closures due to drones per flight" comparable by method in any country, so only the **order** of the levels is derived from the data.

Data (verified 2026-10-09):
- **RU** — at least 217 airport stoppages due to the threat of UAV attacks for January–May 2025 (58 in 2023, 91 in 2024), in 2026 restrictions at at least one airport about every two days. There are no official Rosaviatsia statistics, media counts: "Novaya Gazeta Europe" (via meduza.io, 2025-05-14), meduza.io (2026-07-03), novayagazeta.ru (2026-01-26).
- **DE** — DFS: 225 drone disruptions in 2025 (161 in 2024); closures of MUC (2 and 3 Oct 2025), BER (31 Oct 2025, 23 Sep 2026), BRE (2 Nov 2025). airliners.de, aerotelegraph.com, meduza.io/en (2026-09-23), Wikipedia "2025 European drone sightings".
- **DK** — CPH closed (22 Sep 2025, ~4 h), AAL (24 Sep), BLL (25 Sep); 107 illegal flights near airports in 2025. **NO** — OSL (22 Sep 2025). **BE** — BRU (6 Nov 2025), LGG (9 Nov 2025). Wikipedia, euronews.com (2025-11-20).
- **LT** — Vilnius closed 20 Aug 2025, 13 Sep 2026, 3 Oct 2026. lrt.lt, swedenherald.com, dailypost-uk.com.
- **AE, QA, KW, BH** — airspace closures due to drone and missile strikes: 28 Feb, March (UAE ~2 h), 3 and 23 June 2026. No reports after June. gulfnews.com, cnbc.com (2026-03-17), blog.wego.com (June 2026).
- **US** — FAA "Drone Sightings Near Airports" (faa.gov): ~100 sightings a month, 601 in Q2 2026 (dronelife.com); closures are rare. **GB** — individual UK Airprox Board reports, no annual statistics found.
- Asia — no recent cases found (Changi — 2019).

**Assumptions (approved by the user 2026-10-09):**
1. The multipliers are game numbers; from the data only the order of the levels. The real per-flight frequency is orders of magnitude lower:

   | Level | Countries (ISO 3166-1) | Multiplier | Result at 3% |
   |---|---|---|---|
   | A — almost daily | RU | ×5 | 15% |
   | B — recurring closures 2025–26 | DE, DK, LT, BE, NO, AE, QA, KW, BH | ×2 | 6% |
   | C — frequent sightings, closures rare | US, GB, NL, PL, SE, FI, LV, EE | ×1 | 3% |
   | D — rest of the world | `*` | ×0.3 | 0.9% |

2. The country is the ISO code of the destination airport from OurAirports (the T29 map), not the ICAO prefix.
3. NL, PL, SE, FI, LV, EE — in level C without figures of their own (neighbouring B and sighting news).
4. The Gulf countries (AE, QA, KW, BH) — in `drone_activity`, level B (option 1 of two; a separate scenario `security_airspace_closure` was rejected). SA, IQ, JO, OM, IL, IR, UA are not included (no verified sources / no civil flights).
5. The scenario text stays the same for RU and the Gulf (the reason is the threat of an attack, not a sighting; for the pilot the message is the same).
6. The table is a snapshot as of 2026-10, reviewed manually, with the verification date next to the table.
7. Settings: a table of countries and multipliers for `drone_activity`, editable by the user; empty — from the file; differences — in the record header.

**Open questions:** destination country unknown (not in OurAirports, no network) — the `*` multiplier is proposed; on a diversion — the country of the original destination (the probability roll is before the diversion).

**Incidentally — a bug from the test flight (user decision 2026-10-10: fix it in the nearest task).** The interface crashes on an XATC handover (HANDOVER).
Found in a test flight on 2026-10-10 (Fenix A320, EFHK–ENGM): at the FIR change the app window went empty, in the console `TypeError: Cannot read properties of undefined (reading 'replace')` in `translator` (`i18n.ts`) from `Entry` (`FeedList.tsx`). Cause: `Entry` treats the `from`/`to`/`phase` parameters of any system line as flight phases and translates them as `phase.<value>`, while `atc_handover` (`session.ts`) passes station callsigns (`XFIN`) in `from`/`to` → there is no key `phase.XFIN`. The entry stays in the feed, so View → Reload crashes again. The main process and the flight keep working.
1. Translate as a phase only the parameters of a `phase` line (or rename the `atc_handover` parameters, e.g. `fromStation`/`toStation`).
2. `translator`: an unknown key does not crash the interface (the key as text + `console.error`).
3. A React error boundary around the feed and the tabs: an error in one entry does not blank the window.
4. Main: a `render-process-gone` / `unresponsive` handler — a log entry, reload of the window.
**Done when:** a feed render test for every `SystemCode` with parameters as `session.ts` sends them (including `atc_handover`); a `translator` test for an unknown key.
**Human check:** a flight through 2 FIRs with a logon to XATC: on the handover the line "XATC: handover XFIN → X…", the window does not go empty.

**Incidentally — the second bug of the same flight.** All three random Direct Tos (T26) came to the same fix `TEKVA`, the pilot answered WILCO each time. Cause (from the code): `autoClearances` → `directCandidate` (`atc/auto.ts`, `atc/xatc.ts`) does not remember issued fixes and does not account for the aircraft already flying direct to the fix: the route position (`routePosition`) is a projection onto the OFP line, and while the fix is 50–250 NM ahead it again gives a "shortcut" ≥ 2 NM.
1. Remember the issued Direct To (the fix and its index in the navlog) in the station state (and in the record for replay). While it is not passed — no new random Direct Tos; a trigger point that falls in that time is discarded (as stale, `DIRECT_STALE_NM`).
2. Candidates for the next Direct To — only fixes after the last issued one; fixes between the position and the issued fix are considered skipped.
3. The same fix is never issued again.
4. **Not a random point but logic (user request 2026-10-10; approved by the user 2026-10-10: logic, 50 % probability per FIR, 5 NM threshold — replaces assumption 1 of T26).** The source for real practice — `[VERIFY]` before code (if the practice turns out different — return to the user) (ICAO Doc 4444, EUROCONTROL Free Route Airspace, ATC manuals); T26 records that no restrictions were found in the sources, so everything below is assumptions.
   - **Where:** the exit point from the current FIR along the OFP route (the last route fix in the FIR — the controller takes the aircraft to his own boundary). If it is farther than 250 NM — the farthest route fix in the FIR within 250 NM. Not SID/STAR, not beyond TOD − 50 NM.
   - **When:** not at random route points but at the first contact — after the logon or HANDOVER to the FIR station, if there is more than 50 NM to TOD and the aircraft is in CRUISE (or 1–5 min from the seed after that). No more than one random Direct To per FIR.
   - **Whether to issue:** a shortcut ≥ 5 NM (was 2), the per-FIR probability from the seed — 50 %. Randomness only in "whether to issue" and in the delay, the choice of point is deterministic.
   - **Do not issue:** the aircraft is already flying to an issued fix (items 1–2); the exit point coincides with the next route fix (nothing to shortcut); a live VATSIM controller in the FIR (the station yields, as now).
   - Out of scope (no data source): restricted areas and active TSAs on the shortcut path, traffic density.
**Done when:** a test on telemetry that after `PROCEED DIRECT TO` flies straight to the fix (a synthetic OFP route flight does not reproduce this case): three triggers → no more than one Direct To per fix, the next ones only beyond the issued one; the record of this flight (EFHK–ENGM, `<userData>/recordings`) through `pnpm replay run` gives `DIRECT TO TEKVA` once (`--check` on it will expectedly show a discrepancy with the live run — that is the fix).
**Human check:** a flight with a logon to XATC and "Random Direct To" on: all shortcuts to different fixes, each next one farther than the previous.

**Incidentally — the third bug of the same flight.** The Fenix A320 answers an uplink `/data2/11/5/N/STDBY` (not `STANDBY`). The station does not recognise the answer (`xatc.ts`, the filter `^(WILCO|UNABLE|ROGER|STANDBY|AFFIRM|NEGATIVE)`), takes it for a request and sends `MESSAGE NOT SUPPORTED BY THIS ATS UNIT` (12:35:37, twice — the Fenix sent STDBY in two packets). Fix: accept `STDBY` as STANDBY (and check against FBW/TopSky whether there are other reply abbreviations `[VERIFY]`); a packet with `mrn` on our uplink must not get `MESSAGE NOT SUPPORTED` at all. **Done when:** a test for `STDBY` and for an unknown reply with `mrn` — no station reply; the record `reference/recordings/20261010-FIN4E-EFHK-ENGM-tekva.jsonl` without `MESSAGE NOT SUPPORTED`.

## Stage 6 — Scenario improvements (plan 2026-10-09)

Based on the review of all 63 scenarios and XATC (2026-10-09). The realism rule of stage 5 applies to the whole stage: items marked **R** — first the source and a description of the assumption to the user, code after approval; record what is approved in the card. Game numbers (wait durations, thresholds, probabilities) — as a list for approval before code.

Order and dependencies: **T39 → T40 → T41** (the base for the rest) → T43, T44 → T46 → T47 → T48 → T45. T42 (research of sources) can run in parallel with T41–T44.

#### T39 — Engine: `wait: until` and scenario groups
1. Step `wait: { until: "<condition>", timeout_min }`: the condition is checked on every tick (the same language as `conditions`); `timeout_min` is flight time, like `reply`. The following `branch` separates the outcomes `MET` / `TIMEOUT` (the keys are checked at load, like `reply`). The wait state is serialized.
2. Field `group: <name>` and in the engine a `max_per_flight` limit per group (groups are described in the scenario package). The eight T29 "news" scenarios are moved to `group: special_news` instead of mutual `excludes`; behaviour does not change (the same snapshots).
3. Documentation: `05` (model, execution), JSON Schema.
**Done when:** tests of `until` (met, timeout, pause, ×4, telemetry loss, serialization in the middle of a wait), a group test (one message per flight, `force` bypasses the limit like `excludes`), the `external` snapshots did not change.

#### T40 — Aircraft type in scenarios: `aircraft.family`
1. Variables from `ofp.aircraft.icao` by a table: `aircraft.family` (A320, A330, A350, A380, B737, B747, B777, B787, E-JET, CRJ, ATR, DH8, other — `null`), `aircraft.maker` (`AIRBUS`, `BOEING`, …), `aircraft.body` (`NARROW` / `WIDE`), `aircraft.alert_sys` (`ECAM` / `EICAS`).
2. Generators: `{seat}` — seat letters by `body`; `{hold}` — cargo position: compartments (bulk) for narrow-bodies, ULD positions for wide-bodies.
3. **R:** type codes — ICAO Doc 8643 (Aircraft Type Designators); compartments — the manufacturers' "Aircraft Characteristics for Airport Planning" documents (Airbus AC, Boeing ACAPS) `[VERIFY]` availability and terms of use. Seat letters (A–F / A–K without I) — an assumption: the layout depends on the airline.
   **Assumptions approved by the user 2026-10-14** (items 1–5 of the proposal: a family table by ICAO code; `body`; `alert_sys` — Airbus `ECAM`, Boeing/Embraer/CRJ `EICAS`, ATR/DH8 `null`; `{seat}` — narrow A–F, wide A–K without I, regional E-JET/CRJ/ATR/DH8 — A, C, D, F, rows 1–45; `{hold}` — see the amendment below).
   **Source verification 2026-10-14:**
   - ICAO Doc 8643: the page exists (icao.int/publications/DOC8643), the list is in a dynamic search, the text of the terms of use (`/terms-and-conditions`, `/copyright-notice`) was not read, no data export was obtained. **The family table could not be checked against the Doc 8643 list** — the type codes were taken from knowledge of ICAO designators; `[VERIFY]` on real OFPs.
   - Airbus "Aircraft Characteristics — Airport and Maintenance Planning" (aircraft.airbus.com): A320 Rev. 44 (June 2024, section 2-6-0), A330 Rev. 32 (July 2023, section 2-6-1). Compartment names confirmed: A320 — `FWD`, `AFT`, `BULK` (3 + 4 containers / bulk); A330 — `LOWER DECK FWD / AFT / BULK CARGO COMPARTMENT`; loading combinations — LD3 and pallets 88×125 / 96×125 inches. License: the document is Airbus property, reproduction without written permission is prohibited → we take only compartment names (facts), we do not copy text and drawings.
   - Boeing ACAPS: 737NG D6-58325-7 Rev C (October 2025, item 2.6) — compartments `FORWARD` / `AFT` / bulk (confirmed). 777 (D6-58329-2 Rev G, December 2024) could not be downloaded (the Boeing server returns HTML instead of a PDF) — **not checked**.
   - **User decision 2026-10-14:** `{hold}` — compartments only (`FWD HOLD`, `AFT HOLD`, `BULK HOLD`), for wide-bodies the ULD type (`LD3`, `PALLET`) is added to `FWD`/`AFT` without a position number; regional types — only `FWD`/`AFT` (an assumption, not checked), an unknown type — `CARGO HOLD`. The family table is made **without verification against Doc 8643** — this is marked in the code (`scenario/aircraft.ts`) and in the report.
   - **Implemented** (`core/src/scenario/aircraft.ts`, tests `core/test/aircraft.test.ts`): `aircraft.family|maker|body|alert_sys`, `{seat}`: regional `ACDF`, narrow `A–F`, wide `A–K` without I, unknown type `A–F`; `{hold}`. Snapshots did not change.
   - **Conclusion for `{hold}`:** AC/ACAPS describe compartments and container types but **not the ULD position codes** (like `24L`) — those come from the airline's loading manuals / IATA AHM, there is no open source. The approved item 5 in the "ULD positions" part cannot be done without inventing. Proposal for re-approval: `{hold}` for all types — only the compartment per the sources (`FWD HOLD`, `AFT HOLD`, `BULK HOLD`); for wide-bodies additionally the ULD type from AC (`LD3`, `PALLET`) without a position number.
**Done when:** a table test on types from real OFPs (A20N, B38M, B77W, A359, E190, AT76), an unknown type → `null` and the scenarios use the generic text.

#### T41 — Irregular: errors and promises in the texts (group A)
From the review table, without new sources:
1. `atc_flow_delay` — condition `'slot_ctot' in flight.sent`.
2. `drone_activity` (HOLD), `runway_closure_dest` (HOLD) — continuation via `wait` (T39): "ARRIVALS RESUMED" / "RWY … REOPENED" or "EXPECT RWY …" (another runway from the airport data, if there is one; otherwise without a number).
3. `altn_wx_deterioration` (OK) — the promised update before TOD (`wait: until` on `flight.dist_to_tod_nm`); with live data — the alternate's METAR/TAF, otherwise a generic text.
4. `irr.connect_pax` — a share of `ops.transfer_pax` (core, `irregular-data.ts`); `connection_made` → three outcomes: OK / HELD / MISSED (thresholds — for approval).
5. `medical_onboard` — a message at ON_BLOCK (medics at the door), "DOCTOR ON BOARD NO" raises the insistence of the recommendation, `reply.arg` (symptoms/place) in the reply.
6. `unruly_passenger` (RESTRAIN) — a message at ON_BLOCK (police at the door).
7. `crew_duty_limit` (NO) — if there is no OUT by `irr.latest_out`: "CREW CHANGE", a new delay (`wait: until`).
8. `gate_reassignment` — a line about the baggage belt.
9. `vip_onboard` — seats via `{seat}` T40.
10. Airline memory flags — a lifetime (N days, for approval), expired ones are deleted at flight start.
**Assumptions approved by the user 2026-10-14:** item 4 — `connection_made` ≥ 40 min before STD, new `connection_held` 15–40 (the flight waits), `connection_missed` < 15; the share of `irr.connect_pax` — 20–50 % of `ops.transfer_pax` (my decision, within the card). Item 7 — crew change: the delay is random 40–90 min, step 5 (`irr.crew_change_min`); the new delay only in the text (`set` cannot change `est_*`). Item 10 — flag lifetime: MEL 10 days, no-fly and discretion 30, rush bags 3. My decisions: HOLD waits a fixed 15 min (text "EXPECT HOLDING 15 MIN"), the continuation goes out while not yet LANDED; there is no runway from the airport data (only the planned one) — "EXPECT RWY …" was not done; the VIP seat — a new generator `{seat:1-2}`.
**Implemented:** `core/src/scenario/{flags-expiry,irregular-data,context,template,engine}.ts`, scenarios `irregular/*.yaml` (+ `connection_held`), tests `scenarios/test/irregular.test.ts` ("continuations", "airline memory lifetime"), `core/test/flags.test.ts`. The "if" idiom on `wait: until` with `timeout_min: 0.02` — in `05`.
**Done when:** `irregular.test.ts` — every reply variant and the new continuations on 3 flights × 3 profiles; determinism; snapshots updated deliberately.
#### T42 — Irregular: realism (group B) **R**
Each item: source with verification date and license → assumptions to the user → code.
1. `dg_notoc` — UN3481 instead of UN3480 (UN3480 is forbidden on passenger aircraft), IMP codes (RLI, ICE, RFL, RIS), dry ice in kg, position via `{hold}` T40. Source: ICAO Technical Instructions / IATA DGR (paid) — find open excerpts (ICAO, FAA PHMSA) `[VERIFY]`.
2. `tech_defect_mel` — defect tables per family (T40), category per MMEL, `ECAM`/`EICAS` per `aircraft.alert_sys`, consequences: level restriction (tie-in with the XATC level table), APU INOP → GPU/ASU at the destination. Source: FAA MMEL (public) `[VERIFY]` availability for A320, B737, B777, A350.
3. `deicing_holdover` — only with precipitation/frost risk per METAR (SN, FZRA, FZDZ, BR, etc. at t ≤ threshold); holdover from the FAA Holdover Time Guidelines tables by fluid type, OAT and precipitation.
4. `curfew_dest` — table of real night restrictions (15–30 airports) from the AIP; with no entry for the airport, the scenario does not run.
5. `tankering` — tied to country/region: there is no open source of prices, only an assumption.
6. Connection thresholds `connection_made` — real MCTs are paid (OAG): fixed thresholds as an assumption.
**Source check 2026-10-14 (search; I did not read the document texts, no code — waiting for approval of the assumptions):**
- item 1: UN3480 ban on ICAO passenger aircraft since 2016-04-01 (cargo aircraft only), UN3481 (PI 966/967) is not banned — confirmed by ICAO DGP WG/17 WP.044 and industry summaries; IMP codes `RLI` (lithium-ion, section I), `ELI` (section II) — from a single airline source (SAS Cargo, 2019), `ICE`/`RFL`/`RIS` **not confirmed**; PHMSA references (49 CFR 173.185) were not obtained in the search. There is no open full IMP table (IATA DGR is paid).
- item 2: FAA MMELs are public on FSIMS (final) and Opspecs (drafts); found: A320 Rev 30 (draft, FAA), 737 CL/NG Rev 63 (Boeing draft, 2026-02-12), 777-9 Rev 0 (draft), 787 Rev 19 (2025-05-20). **A350 and 777-200/300 not found**; A330/A380 not searched. This is an MMEL, not an airline MEL; the item texts must be read before use.
- item 3: FAA Holdover Time Guidelines — seasons 2025-26 (PDF) and 2026-27 (PDF, Type IV generic table No. 20) exist; numeric values not extracted (the search returned only the table of contents). The FAA conditions are a public document, not legally binding.
- items 4, 5, 6: not researched. Item 6 (connection thresholds) closed in T41 (≥40 / 15–40 / <15, assumption approved). Item 5: there is no open source of prices.
**Implemented 2026-10-14 (assumptions accepted by the user; the rest is `[VERIFY]`, not done):**
- item 1 `dg_notoc`: UN3481 instead of UN3480, `IMP RLI` (section I) / `ELI` (section II) — per SAS Cargo 2019, `[VERIFY]` against IATA DGR; dry ice `UN1845` 25/50/100 kg net (weights are an assumption), IMP `ICE`/`RFL`/`RIS` **not added** (not confirmed); position — `{hold}` T40; `CAO NO`.
- item 2 `tech_defect_mel`: FAA MMEL items, category C (10 days). **A320** — FAA MMEL A318/A319/A320/A321 **Rev 30, draft (date XX)**, downloaded 2026-10-14: 49-10-01 APU, 30-42-01 Window Heat Computers (one — when there is no icing), 21-51-01 Pack Flow Control Valves. **B737** — MMEL 737 CL/NG Rev 63 dated 2026-04-03 (Rev 63a, 2026-05-27 on the Boeing page), downloaded 2026-10-14: 49-01-01A APU, 21-01-01 Air Conditioning Packs (one — up to FL250 except ETOPS), 30-01-02 Wing Anti-Ice Valves. MMEL texts are not copied: only numbers, titles and the fact from the remarks (category C = 10 days is the general FAA definition, not checked in the downloaded files `[VERIFY]`). The other types keep the earlier generic items, **without a source** `[VERIFY]` (A330/A350/B777/B787 — MMELs not found or not read). `aircraft.alert_sys` for B737 is now `null` (the 737 has no ECAM/EICAS): text "CONFIRM INDICATION ON ECAM" for A320, "CREW ALERTING" for the 737. The FL250 level restriction is text only, with no link to the XATC level table.
- item 3 `deicing_holdover` — `[VERIFY]` (FAA 2026-27 numbers not read), item 4 `curfew_dest` — done 2026-10-25 (see below), item 5 `tankering` — `[VERIFY]` (no open price source), item 6 — closed in T41.
**Item 4 `curfew_dest` — assumptions approved by the user 2026-10-25** (trigger: the message "NOISE CURFEW AT EGCC, LATEST LANDING 1500Z": Manchester has no ban, only QC quotas 23:00–07:00):
1. The table holds only airports where landings in the window are forbidden; airports with only quotas/noise limits (EGCC, EGLL, EHAM) are excluded. The list contains only what is confirmed by the AIP (AD 2.20/2.21) or by law/airport rules.
2. Where late arrivals have an allowance after the ban starts (ZRH etc.), `LATEST LANDING` is the end of the allowance.
3. The scenario runs only if the planned landing is before the ban starts and the estimated one (with delay) is within 20 min of it or later; a flight scheduled to land inside the ban gets no message.
4. Only facts (hours, scope) are taken from the AIP; the text is not copied.
5. The `rng.int(20, 60)` draw in `irregular-data.ts` is kept (the result is unused) so that the Rng order and flight recordings do not change.
6. Day-of-week windows are supported; seasonal and quota exceptions are not modeled — such airports are either not included or are simplified with a note in the record.
**Implemented 2026-10-25** (table approved by the user): `core/src/ext/curfews.ts` — 13 airports, each with a source, link and verification date 2026-10-25: YSSY, YPAD 23:00; EDDF, EDDH 23:00 + 60 min allowance; LSZH 23:00 + 30; LSGG 00:00; EGLC 22:00 + 30, on Saturday 12:30 + 30; LFPO 23:30; NZWN 00:00 + 30, international arrivals 01:00 + 30; CYTZ, KSNA 23:00; RJOO 21:00; RJFF 22:00. Simplifications: EGLC has no bank holidays or 25 December, NZWN has no +60 min shift on holidays; an NZWN international flight is one whose departure ICAO prefix is not `NZ`. Not included: quotas only — EGCC, EGLL, EHAM, EDDM; no official source — EDDS, EDDL, BER, RKSS; no ban or not confirmed — LIML, YBCG. `irr.curfew` = `latestLanding(ofp)`; scenario `curfew_dest` has the condition `irr.curfew != null`. Tests: `core/test/curfews.test.ts`, `scenarios/test/irregular.test.ts` (flight moved to RJOO). Update the table once a year.

**Done when:** tables have the source and verification date next to them; tests on real types and airports.

#### T43 — Base set
1. `slot_ctot` — **first check** whether the slot shifts `est_*` and `flight.delay_min` (judging by the code, it seems not). If not — shift them by the same mechanism as `DELAY`.
2. New `slot_missed`: OFF later than CTOT + tolerance → "SLOT MISSED, NEW CTOT". **R:** window −5/+10 min — EUROCONTROL NM ATFCM Users Manual `[VERIFY]`.
3. `fuel_slip` — on fuel stabilization (`fuelStable`, `04`), not strictly at STD−12; UPLIFT lines (FOB after − FOB before refueling) and DIFF against the plan.
4. `takeoff_data` / `landing_data` — SimBrief TLR data (V-speeds, flex, flaps) if present in the OFP `[VERIFY]` API fields; without TLR — as now.
5. `final_loadsheet` — DOW/OEW and crew composition from the OFP; no CG — do not invent it.
6. `departure_report` / `block_report` — IATA delay codes when `delay_min > 0`, the reason from `DELAY <min> <reason>`. **R:** code list — EUROCONTROL CODA `[VERIFY]` the license.
7. `arrival_info` — for a stand without a jet bridge (OSM) "REMOTE STAND, BUS AND STAIRS" instead of "GPU AND PCA AVBL".
8. `welcome_turnaround` — with a delay, "QUICK TURN, TARGET … MIN".
9. `next_leg` — unchanged (no airline schedule data), noted.
10. **Crew names by airline** (`{name:crew}` / `{name:ops}`, `core/src/scenario/names.ts`; user request 2026-10-10 from a test flight). Currently the surname region is taken from the ICAO prefix of the departure airport, and only 10 regions exist; everything else is `en`. A Finnair FIN4E flight from EFHK got English-language surnames in `crew_info` (`EF` is not in the table). Proposal (for approval, realism rule):
    - **Airline country** — from the airline's ICAO code in the OFP (`FIN` → FI) via an open reference (OpenFlights `airlines.dat`, ODbL, or similar). **User decision (2026-10-10):** check the reference's license and currency before use (source, license, verification date — in the card); if unsuitable — another source for approval. If not in the reference — the departure airport's country from OurAirports (as in T29), not the ICAO prefix.
    - **More regions:** the most frequent surnames from national statistics (Statistics Finland, etc.) with source and verification date, transliterated to ASCII A–Z; first the countries of frequent routes (FI, SE, NO, DK, NL, PL, TR, AE, QA, IN, US, GB, BR, etc. — list for approval).
    - **Multinational airlines** (Emirates, Qatar Airways, Etihad, etc.): each crew member has their own nationality from a mix; the mix weights are an assumption (there are no open statistics on crew composition `[VERIFY]`). **User decision (2026-10-10):** the list of airlines and the mix weights — from an analysis on the internet **before writing code** (reports and airline sites on the number of crew nationalities, press, job postings; with source and verification date), the result goes to the user for approval. For ordinary airlines — mostly the airline's country, **10%** other nationalities (approved by the user 2026-10-10).
    - Deterministic from the seed; format unchanged (`K. VIRTANEN`). List order is part of the replay contract: a new generator version (version number in the recording header), old recordings are run by the old one.
    **Done when:** tests: FIN from EFHK → Finnish surnames; FIN from ENGM → Finnish (by airline, not by airport); UAE from OMDB → mixed crew; unknown airline → airport country; replay of an old recording (`reference/recordings/20261010-FIN4E-EFHK-ENGM-tekva.jsonl`) gives the same names as before.
11. **Invented gate** (found in a test flight 2026-10-14, destination RJCH: gate `C40` was issued, which does not exist at the airport). Cause per the code: `rebuildData` (`engine.ts`) takes the gate from OSM stands (`pickStand`); if there are no stands (`ext.stands === null` — live data is off, Overpass did not respond, or the airport has no `ref` tags), the `ops-data.ts` stub remains: a random letter A–E and a number 1–40. The stub ends up in `gate_assignment`, `arrival_info`, `REQ GATE`, `short_connection` (`TRANSFER STAFF AT GATE`), `gate_reassignment` (`reassignedGate` takes the same number ± a step). **R:** the gate comes only from a verified source (OSM `aeroway=gate`/`parking_position`, link to the query and verification date in the record); there must be no invented numbers. Proposal (for approval): no real gate → `gate_assignment` is not sent, in `arrival_info` and `REQ GATE` — "GATE WILL BE ADVISED" (text for approval), `gate_reassignment` and `short_connection` without the gate line; a note "gate: no source" in the recording header. Check separately: why there are no stands for RJCH (empty Overpass response, `standRefs` filter, 4 km radius) and whether the `ref` tag of `parking_position` is suitable as a "gate" (at airports without jet bridges it is a stand number, not a gate — then write `STAND`, not `GATE`). Related to item 7 (stand without a jet bridge) and T44 item 7 (gate change takes gates from the same source).
12. **`short_connection` — only for a real flight** (found in the same flight: `ANA330` at RJCH, no such flight exists from that airport). Currently the flight is synthetic: `irregular-data.ts` takes the flight's airline prefix and a random number 100–989, STD 50–95 min after arrival. This contradicts the realism rule. Condition: the message is sent only if the flight is confirmed by a source — the airline operates a flight from this airport (number, direction, time). There is no free schedule API without a key (decision on T30, 2026-10-09), so by default — without a confirmed flight the scenario does not run (`conditions` on `irr.connect_flight != null`, `irr.connect_flight` = `null` without a source). Sources for approval, **R** `[VERIFY]` license and currency: (a) an open route database (OpenFlights `routes.dat` — ODbL license, data long not updated: only "the airline flies from this airport", without numbers and times); (b) a schedule entered by the user in settings (key/file on the user's side, as in T16); (c) live VATSIM/online data. Which option — the user decides; option (a) allows only generic text without a flight number ("ONWARD FLT") if the number is not confirmed. Affects `connection_made`/`connection_missed` (they use the same `irr.connect_*`) and `arrival_info` (`ops.next_flight` — item 9). Combine with T41 item 4 (`irr.connect_pax`) and T42 item 6 (connection thresholds).
**Done (2026-10-14):** item 1 — the slot shifts the delay (`set: flight.delay_min`), the slot's CTOT does not move with the delay it caused; a pilot who is not waiting for CTOT sees documents later (approved as the "as in the card" option); item 2 — `slot_missed` (the −5/+10 window is confirmed in the Ireland AIP ENR 1.9 of 18.05.2023, a secondary source; the NM manual was not read; it does not invent a new CTOT); item 3 — `fuel_slip` on stabilization (`flight.fuel_stable`, UPLIFT, DIFF); item 7 — `STAND`/"REMOTE STAND, BUS AND STAIRS" for a stand without `aeroway=gate`; item 8 — quick turn; item 11 — gate only from OSM (`ops.gate_*`; the note in the recording header was not made; why RJCH has no stands — not investigated); item 12 — by default `short_connection` does not run without a confirmed flight, the flight source is not chosen.
**Done (2026-10-15):** item 4 — TLR from a real OFP (`reference/ofp/raw`, A35K RCTP–RJBB): `takeoff_data` has flaps, thrust (`FLEX 71`), V1/VR/V2, `landing_data` has flaps and VREF — only for the runways in the OFP plan; without TLR — as before; new fixture `rctp-rjbb-a35k-kgs-tlr.json` (`anonymize-ofp.mjs --with-perf`, crew names replaced by roles). Boeing (`D-TO`, assumed temperature) — `[VERIFY]` on a real OFP. Item 5 — `OEW` (as in the OFP; we do not substitute `DOW` — the OFP does not say they are the same) and `CREW 2/16` (captain + 2 pilots / cabin crew with a purser) from the OFP `crew`, without the section — no line; `ops.cabin_crew` in `crew_info` — the same headcount. Crew names from the OFP are not used (related to item 10).
Item 6 (user decision 2026-10-15: CODA, non-commercial use) — IATA AHM730 codes per the EUROCONTROL CODA Digest "Delays to Air Transport in Europe", September 2012, annex F (eurocontrol.int/archive_download/all/node/9022, verified 2026-10-15; copying with attribution to EUROCONTROL, non-commercial, unmodified — we take only the code numbers). The format is the `DL` element of an MVT message (AHM780): `DL81/0025`, two codes `DL64/72/0015/0020` — per OAG "MVT, MVA & DIV Message Types and Examples" (verified 2026-10-15). `core/src/scenario/delay-codes.ts`: the delay is composed of parts — late start `63`, slot (`set: flight.delay_min`) `81`, `DELAY <min> <reason>` — the code as the first word (`DELAY 20 41 …`) or by keywords (TECH→41, FUEL→36, PAX/BOARDING→15, BAGS→18, CATERING→37, CLEANING→35, LOADING/CARGO→32, LOADSHEET/DOCS→31, CREW→63, PUSHBACK/TUG/GPU→39, WX→71, DEICE/SNOW→75, ATC/SLOT/FLOW→81, SECURITY→85, CUSTOMS→86, GATE/STAND→87, ROTATION/INBOUND→93), otherwise `99`; actual delay = OUT − STD, parts from larger to smaller, the unexplained remainder — `99`, no more than two codes in the line. **Assumptions to confirm:** the keyword table, `63` for late start, `99` for the remainder. The `DL…` line is last in `departure_report` and `block_report`, only when OUT is later than STD.
Item 12 (user decision 2026-10-15: OpenFlights) — a table from `routes.dat` (ODbL, routes are public domain, not updated since 2014): if the flight's airline operated its own departures from the destination airport → `irr.connect_flight` = `ONWARD FLT` (no number), otherwise there is no connection. `ANA330` from RJCH is no longer issued: per 2014 data ANA flew from RJCH, the text is `ONWARD FLT`. The connection STD is still an assumption (50–95 min after STA) — to be confirmed.
Item 10 (approved by the user 2026-10-15) — name generator version 2 (`core/src/scenario/names.ts`, core option `namesVersion`, in the recording header; without the field — version 1, old recordings give the same names as before — verified on FIN4E): the region is by the airline's country (OpenFlights `airlines.dat`, ODbL, see item 12), otherwise the departure airport's country by VATSpy (live data; this is the same ICAO prefix table — OurAirports is not passed into the core, a deviation from the proposal), otherwise the earlier ICAO prefix; each name with probability 1/10 comes from another region (approved 2026-10-10). New regions with source and verification date 2026-10-15: FI — DVV Sukunimitilasto 2026-08-04 (CC BY 4.0), SE — SCB (population 2021-12-31, 10 surnames unordered after the first), NO — SSB "Etternavn brukt av 200 eller flere" (year not stated), DK — Danmarks Statistik 2016-01-01 (only 5 surnames), NL — Meertens 2003 via Onze Taal (secondary), PL — PESEL 2026-01-20 (dane.gov.pl, CC0; male forms), US — 2010 census (Census Bureau). No open official lists found: TR, GB, BR, IN, AE, QA — they stay on the earlier rules. Multinational airlines: Emirates (UAE), Etihad (ETD), Qatar Airways (QTR) — each name from any region with equal probability (**assumption to confirm**: there are no open-source shares by country). The old regions (en, de, fr, es, it, ru, jp, tw, cn, kr) have no recorded source `[VERIFY]`.

Low-cost subsidiaries after 2017 and QTR/CPA/THY among the hubs — confirmed by the user 2026-10-15.

#### T44 — Pilot commands
1. `cmd_eta` — from the ground speed in telemetry and the remaining distance (without telemetry — as now).
2. `cmd_divert` — ETA and distance to the diversion airport in the reply; new `divert_arrival` (ON_BLOCK with `flight.divert`: refueling, passengers, plan); memory flag `recovery` — the next flight from the diversion airport (message before departure).
3. `MEDICAL <text>` — the pilot reports a medical case themselves: the same chain as `medical_onboard` (CONTINUE/DIVERT, medics on arrival), the pilot's text in the reply; a random `medical_onboard` does not run in the flight after this.
4. `REQ ATIS <ICAO>` (without an airport — the destination): VATSIM ATIS text (`atis[].text_atis` in the v3 feed — the `vatsim.ts` adapter currently takes only the letter) → otherwise "NO ATIS AVBL" + METAR. **R:** real D-ATIS — only unofficial sources (US), `[VERIFY]` the license, not used by default.
5. `RDY` — the crew is ready before the slot: with CTOT issued and before OUT the reply is "READY MSG SENT" and after a seed-based time — an improved CTOT or "NO IMPROVEMENT". **R:** analogue of the CASA REA message, EUROCONTROL NM ATFCM Users Manual `[VERIFY]`; improvement probability — an assumption.
6. `cmd_help` — lines about XATC (logon `X`+FIR, DCL `X`+airport) and the new commands.
7. Request for a different gate (added 2026-10-10 from a test flight: currently `REQ GATE` with extra words silently returns the same gate). For example `REQ GATE CHANGE [<gate>] [<reason>]` — exact syntax per source. OPS reply: a new gate (the requested one if "free", otherwise another) or a refusal ("UNABLE ALL GATES OCCUPIED" etc.); on agreement — `set ops.arr_gate`, then arrival info and `REQ GATE` name the new one, as after `gate_reassignment`. Not after ON_BLOCK; with `flight.divert` — as `cmd_req_gate_divert`. Gates come from the same source as `irr.new_gate` (OSM stands with live data). **R:** the format of the request and OPS replies — from real AOC/ACARS samples `[VERIFY]`; agreement probability and gate choice — assumptions for approval.
**Done (2026-10-14):** item 1 — `cmd_eta_gs` (ETA = now + remainder/GS, `flight.eta_live`; without GS — the earlier `cmd_eta`); item 2 — `cmd_divert_eta` (distance and ETA only for OFP airports: the core has no coordinates of other airports), `divert_arrival`, flags `recovery_*` (3-day term — an assumption) and `recovery_followup`; item 3 — `MEDICAL <text>` (`cmd_medical`; a random `medical_onboard` does not run after it); item 4 — `REQ ATIS [ICAO]` from VATSIM `text_atis` (checked on a feed fixture), otherwise "NO ATIS AVBL" + METAR; D-ATIS is not used; item 5 — `RDY` (window per the REA rule: Ireland AIP ENR 1.9 items 2.5.3–2.5.4; 40% chance, 5–15 min gain — assumptions; the improved CTOT is only in the text, `est_*` are not reduced); item 6 — `cmd_help`; item 7 — `REQ GATE CHANGE [gate] [reason]` (70% agreement, `set ops.arr_gate`; texts and 70% — assumptions).
**Done when:** parser of the new commands (`pilot.ts`), `commands.test.ts` — each variant on 3 flights × 3 profiles, `UNABLE` on wrong arguments and in the wrong phase; for item 7 — agreement and refusal are deterministic from the seed, after the gate change `REQ GATE` and arrival info name the new one.

#### T45 — External data and VDGS
1. T29 "News" → `group` (T39).
2. `space_launch_done` on FAILURE / PARTIAL FAILURE — text about the debris zone. **R:** FAA Debris Response Area `[VERIFY]`.
3. `volcanic_ash`, `space_weather` — interactive: "REPLY REROUTE OR CONTINUE"; REROUTE → avoidance via XATC (T48). For S2+ on a polar route — a proposal of a lower level / non-polar route. **R:** airline practice and FAA/ICAO on polar routes `[VERIFY]`.
4. New `atis_update`: during descent the destination ATIS letter changed (VATSIM) → the new letter and METAR.
5. `title_ru` / `description` / `description_ru` for `base/`, `commands/` and the T13 scenarios in `external/`.
6. `vdgs_arrival` — on diversion/holding a red line; departure board — if GSX supports it `[VERIFY]` the VGDS manual.
**Done (2026-10-15):** item 1 — already in T39 (all eight "news" items are on `group: special_news`). Item 4 — `external/atis_update`: at 150 NM from the destination, if VATSIM online ATIS exists, the letter is remembered in the temporary flag `flags.atis_seen` (deleted after the end); `wait: until` (up to 60 min) for a letter change in CRUISE/DESCENT/APPROACH → "NEW INFO <new> REPLACES <old>" + METAR. If ATIS is not on the air at 150 NM — the scenario does not run (we do not count the letter "appearing" as a change). VATSIM only, D-ATIS is not used (as `cmd_req_atis`). Item 5 — `title_ru`/`description`/`description_ru` on all scenarios (`base`, `commands`, `external`, `irregular`, `sim`), the `index.test.ts` test for presence. Item 6, the diversion part — `sim/vdgs_divert`: on LANDED with `flight.divert`, the diversion airport board shows the flight, a red line `DIVERTED`, the departure airport; **without a gate** (the stand at the diversion airport is unknown). Red lines are a VGDS capability (`06`). The wording assumption ("DIVERTED") — to be confirmed.
**Not done, waiting for a user decision (R):**
- **item 2 done (approved by the user 2026-10-15):** on FAILURE / PARTIAL FAILURE `space_launch_done` adds "DEBRIS RESPONSE AREA MAY BE ACTIVE / CHECK NOTAM" (the same for any launch site: the scenario does not know the site's country; an FAA zone may or may not exist — hence the text "may"); on success the earlier text. Through the "if" idiom (`wait: until`, TIMEOUT) the message goes out 2 s later — the snapshot was updated deliberately. Item 3 — after T48 (user decision 2026-10-15). Original entry: `space_launch_done`: FAA (faa.gov/space/airspace_integration/debris_response_areas, verified 2026-10-15) — a Debris Response Area is activated by the FAA after a confirmed rocket failure, may require leaving the zone/forbid entry, hold aircraft on the ground; we have no live source of zones (boundaries are in NOTAMs). Proposal: on FAILURE / PARTIAL FAILURE add the line "DEBRIS RESPONSE AREA MAY BE ACTIVATED BY FAA / CHECK NOTAM" only for launches whose site and route are in the FAA (US) area of responsibility — otherwise the generic text "CHECK NOTAM". We do not claim "a zone was created" without data.
- item 3 `volcanic_ash`/`space_weather` — "REPLY REROUTE OR CONTINUE": the REROUTE action depends on T48 (avoidance via XATC, not done yet); for S2+ on a polar route — a source on polar route practice (FAA/ICAO) was not found. Do after T48.
- item 6, the holding part: the VDGS board is shown only at a stand (after landing), there is nowhere to show a red line while holding; departure boards in the VGDS manual not checked `[VERIFY]`.
**Done when:** `external.test.ts` with data and without, snapshots; UI texts for all scenarios (presence test).

#### T46 — New irregular scenarios
1. `hard_landing_check` — touchdown vertical speed from the bridge: SimVar `PLANE TOUCHDOWN NORMAL VELOCITY` `[VERIFY]` in the MSFS SDK; a telemetry field (`03`, C# bridge, `bridge:test`). Above the threshold — maintenance: "REPLY CONFIRM", CONFIRM → a flag on the aircraft, an inspection before the next flight (as `mel_carry_over`). **R:** thresholds — manufacturer AMMs (Airbus 05-51-11, Boeing) `[VERIFY]`; ~1 Hz telemetry is not good enough for this, without the SimVar the scenario does not run.
2. `fuel_monitoring` — FOB from telemetry against the plan at a navlog point: SimBrief navlog field `fuel_plan_onboard` `[VERIFY]` → `OfpFix.planFobKg` (T02, parser). Below the plan by more than the threshold — dispatcher: "FUEL BELOW PLAN, REPLY REASON" (options for approval), then a recommendation. The threshold is an assumption.
3. `ped_fire_cabin` — lithium battery fire in the cabin in cruise: CONTINUE / DIVERT, continuation via `wait: until`. **R:** FAA SAFO 09013 (PED firefighting) and ICAO/EASA materials `[VERIFY]`; probability — for approval.
**Source check 2026-10-15 (no code — waiting for approval of the assumptions):**
- item 1: the SimVar `PLANE TOUCHDOWN NORMAL VELOCITY` exists in the MSFS SDK (docs.flightsimulator.com, 2024: "velocity normal to the ground at the last touchdown", feet per second, not writable). The C# bridge does not request it now — a new index in `SimVars.cs` and a `touchdownFps` field are needed (null until the first landing; extending the `TelemetrySnapshot` v1 schema → `03`). The value is "since the last touchdown" — the scenario must take it on LANDED. AMM Airbus 05-51-11 / Boeing not opened, **thresholds in them not checked**. An open reference figure is the design descent velocity per the regulations: 10 ft/s at design landing weight and 6 ft/s at design takeoff weight (14 CFR / CS-25.473, EASA Easy Access Rules) — these are design loads, not an inspection criterion.
- item 2: the `fuel_plan_onboard` field exists in the SimBrief navlog (checked on fixtures, an integer in OFP units) — into the parser `OfpFix.planFobKg` with lbs → kg conversion.
- item 3: FAA SAFO 09013 (23.06.2009, "Fighting Fires Caused by Lithium Type Batteries in PEDs"): water/halon/substitute, do not use fire-resistant containment bags; in 2017 the FAA references the SAFO together with AC 120-80A (the current revision 120-80B not checked). The SAFO status (canceled/superseded) is not confirmed `[VERIFY]`.
**Assumptions for approval (proposal):** (a) `hard_landing_check`: threshold 10 ft/s (≈600 ft/min); (b) `fuel_monitoring`: threshold 5% of the plan at the point or 500 kg (whichever is greater), checked once per flight in CRUISE, REASON reply options — `WX`, `ATC`, `LEAK`, `NORMAL`; (c) `ped_fire_cabin`: probability 1% per flight in CRUISE.
**Assumptions approved by the user 2026-10-15:** hard landing — more than 350 ft/min (the bridge supplies ft/s × 60); `fuel_monitoring` — 5% of plan or 500 kg (whichever is greater), once per flight, replies WX/ATC/LEAK/NORMAL (NORMAL by default); `ped_fire_cabin` chance — configurable, default 0.5% (`probability: 0.005`, changeable in the app, like the other irregular scenarios).
**Implemented (2026-10-15):** the `touchdownFpm` field in telemetry (optional in TS; C# bridge: `SimVars.Touchdown`, `PLANE TOUCHDOWN NORMAL VELOCITY`, feet per second, `Math.Abs`×60; `reference/telemetry.sample.json`, `03`), `OfpFix.planFobKg` (parser, lbs → kg), variables `flight.touchdown_fpm`, `flight.fuel_below_plan_kg`, `flight.fuel_plan_tol_kg` (`05`), scenarios `irregular/{hard_landing_check,hard_landing_followup,fuel_monitoring,ped_fire_cabin}.yaml`, the flag family `hardland` (3 days — **my assumption, to be confirmed**), `withTouchdown` for synthetic flights. The inspection text ("NO DAMAGE FOUND") and the fuel cause hints are game content, with no AMM figures. The SimVar sign (down = positive?) — `[VERIFY by human]`, the bridge takes the absolute value.
**Done when:** each reply option on 3 flights × 3 profiles; for `hard_landing_check` — a bridge test and a flight without the field (the scenario does not run).
**Human check:** MSFS 2024, Fenix A320: hard landing → maintenance message; a flight with under-fueling → `fuel_monitoring`.

#### T47 — XATC: execution monitoring and new requests
1. Level monitoring: after WILCO on `CLIMB/DESCEND TO` — if within N min the aircraft has not started the change or has left the assigned level → `CONFIRM ASSIGNED LEVEL` (FANS-A UM). **R:** UM numbers and phrases — per the T25 sources `[VERIFY]`; N — an assumption.
2. Currently "MESSAGE NOT SUPPORTED": `REQUEST WEATHER DEVIATION` → `CLEARED TO DEVIATE UP TO … OF ROUTE`; `REQUEST OFFSET`; `WHEN CAN WE EXPECT HIGHER`; `REQUEST VOICE CONTACT` → frequency (VATSIM/OurAirports). **R:** packet format as in FBW `[VERIFY]`.
3. `MAYDAY` / `PAN` (DM56/DM55) → XATC reply (`ROGER MAYDAY`) and start of the airline chain (diversion to the nearest airport, medics/fire services).
4. DCL with CTOT issued — the slot in the clearance. **R:** DCL format ARINC 623 / EUROCONTROL `[VERIFY]`, whether such a line exists.
**Done when:** CPDLC packet tests, level monitoring on a synthetic profile, MAYDAY → event and scenario.
**Assumptions approved by the user 2026-10-15** (sources: L3Harris "CPDLC message types", FAA CPDLC uplink/downlink message set, Eurocontrol "Message sets supported by each ANSP", verified 2026-10-15; FBW/PMDG packet texts not checked `[VERIFY]`): reply `ROGER MAYDAY` / `ROGER PAN` (GOLD v1.0; FANS-1/A has the attribute `N`, so as free text, immediately); level monitoring — 5 min after WILCO, text UM135 `CONFIRM ASSIGNED ALTITUDE` (the L3Harris list says "ALTITUDE", not "LEVEL"); route deviation up to 10 NM (DM27 → UM82, a larger request — 10 NM). **Not done:** `REQUEST OFFSET`, `WHEN CAN WE EXPECT HIGHER`, slot in DCL (no source), `REQUEST VOICE CONTACT` (needs a frequency: only live VATSIM, and with a live controller the station yields).
**Done (2026-10-15):** `atc/xatc.ts` — `ATC_LEVEL_CHECK` (after WILCO on our CLIMB/DESCEND TO; "not started" after 5 min or "left" by >300 ft after reaching the level; one question per clearance; monitoring is reset on logon loss), `ATC_EMERGENCY` (MAYDAY/PAN are accepted even without a logon: we do not reject an emergency message); `atc/requests.ts` — `deviation`; `core.ts` — after MAYDAY/PAN on the next tick `emergency_mayday` / `emergency_pan` is force-started (`irregular/`, `probability: 0` — they do not run on their own; the DIVERT/CONTINUE offer, a 5-min wait — my assumption, MAYDAY defaults to DIVERT, PAN to CONTINUE; DIVERT → `flight.divert`, then T48). Tests: `core/test/xatc-control.test.ts`, `scenarios/test/t47.test.ts`.
**Level monitoring fix (2026-10-15, branch `task/T47-level-watch-fix`):** (1) level clearances the station issues itself under T48 replace the monitored one: the descent on diversion (`DESCEND TO`) waits for its own WILCO and is monitored like a requested CLIMB/DESCEND; `HOLD AT … MAINTAIN` cancels monitoring and is not itself monitored (the aircraft is already at that level, and the descent after holding is by voice). Previously, a descent after the station's own `DESCEND TO` was counted as leaving the old level → a false UM135. (2) The transition level in `HOLD … MAINTAIN` and in the diversion descent is as in the T26 descent: `trans_level` → `trans_alt` → default (decision 2026-10-09, option b), for a diversion — per the diversion airport; previously 18 000 ft was hardcoded and always "FT". (3) **Assumption (approved by the user 2026-10-15):** level monitoring runs only in the CLIMB / CRUISE / DESCENT phases; in APPROACH and later (GO_AROUND, LANDED…) it and the WILCO wait are cancelled — from approach on, levels are given by the approach controller by voice. The T26 descent (to the STAR entry level) and the diversion descent (10 000 ft) are the station's "last" level: on reaching it monitoring ends, there is no "left" check after them (further descent is by voice), "not started within 5 min" remains. Not closed by this rule: if the T26 descent was not issued (no STAR entry level / MORA or lower is already cleared) and a climb level is under monitoring, the pilot's descent at TOD in the DESCENT phase will give UM135; and a level issued at the pilot's request already in the DESCENT phase is checked for "left" until APPROACH. Tests: `core/test/xatc-control.test.ts`, `core/test/xatc-link.test.ts`.
**Human check:** FBW A32NX and PMDG 777: the new requests are sent from the CPDLC page, the replies are received.
#### T48 — Airline and XATC link
1. **Diversion** (`DIVERT`, `set flight.divert` from any scenario): XATC issues `PROCEED DIRECT TO <airport>` and a descent; automatic Direct To / step climb / descent along the old route stop.
2. **Holding** (HOLD branches of `drone_activity`, `runway_closure_dest`): `HOLD AT <fix>` (a STAR fix or the first approach fix) and the expected time (EFC); the end of the hold follows the T41 continuation.
3. **Deviation** (REROUTE in `volcanic_ash`, `space_weather`, T45): `CLEARED TO DEVIATE` / Direct To around the area, or a different level.
4. Without an XATC logon (or with a live VATSIM controller), only the airline message, as now.
**R:** the phrases `HOLD AT`, `EXPECT FURTHER CLEARANCE` (FANS-A UM) `[VERIFY]`.
**Done when:** end-to-end "scenario → XATC" tests on a synthetic flight; replay is deterministic.
**Done (2026-10-15):** item 1 — `atc/xatc.ts` `airlineLink`: on `flight.divert` (pilot command or scenario), after 20–60 s (seed), `PROCEED DIRECT TO <airport>` (UM74), then `DESCEND TO 10000FT` once the airport is closer than 3 NM per 1000 ft + 20 NM (assumption, the numbers are mine; an airport outside the OFP gets no descent, there are no coordinates); automatic Direct To / step climb / descent along the old route stop. Item 2 — new target `set: { flight.hold: <min> }` (airborne; `null` ends it), in `drone_activity` and `runway_closure_dest` (HOLD branch): `HOLD AT <fix> AS PUBLISHED MAINTAIN <level>` (UM92) and `EXPECT FURTHER CLEARANCE AT <hhmm>` (UM93) — both elements confirmed against the L3Harris FANS list (checked 2026-10-15); the fix is the first one ahead of the STAR entry (otherwise the next one in the nav log), the level is the current one rounded to 1000 ft; the end of the hold is `PROCEED DIRECT TO <next fix>`. Item 4 — without a logon (or with a live controller the station yields), only the airline message works. The `link` state in `XatcState`, `divert`/`hold` are inputs of `XatcStation.tick`. Tests: `core/test/xatc-link.test.ts`, `scenarios/test/t48.test.ts`.
**Not done — item 3 (deviation, REROUTE in `volcanic_ash`/`space_weather`):** an ash cloud and a geomagnetic storm have no geometry (area, coordinates), so the "deviation" would have to be invented; the same blocks item 3 of the T45 card. A source of areas is needed (SIGMET VA — outlines, TheSpaceDevs/SWPC — indices only) or a decision from the user.
**Human check:** FBW A32NX, XATC logon, `DIVERT` in cruise → a Direct To the alternate arrives on the CPDLC page.

#### T49 — XATC: UI hints when a message went to the wrong station
Found in a test flight on 2026-10-10 (Fenix A320, EFHK–ENGM): the request `REQUEST PREDEP CLEARANCE …` went by telex to the FIR station `XFIN` instead of the DCL station `XFHK`. The FIR station accepts only `cpdlc` and silently dropped the telex (`xatc.ts`, `receive`). Neither the feed nor the status showed why there was no reply.
1. Core: instead of silently dropping, emit an event (for example `ATC_MISROUTED`: station, packet type, what it was, where it should go). Cases: telex to a FIR station; a PDC request to a FIR station or to a different airport; telex/cpdlc to a DCL station after takeoff or after the exchange has ended; a message to an `X…` station the core does not currently serve. Refusal of a logon to the wrong station already exists (`atc_logon_refused`) — check that the feed makes clear where to log on.
2. App: a feed line with a hint, for example "XFIN accepts CPDLC only (logon, requests). Send the PDC request by telex to XFHK". In "Status", on the XATC row, what to send where: "CPDLC logon → XFIN · PDC (telex) → XFHK".
3. Nothing new goes to the network: the hint is in the app only. Whether to answer the pilot in the cockpit (free text from the station) is an open question, only if a real sample of such a reply turns up.
4. Aircraft without CPDLC. **User decision (2026-10-10):** XATC stays only for aircraft with CPDLC through Hoppie; we do not accept telex at FIR stations and we do not build an ATC console in the app. In the app — a hint: with XATC enabled and an aircraft without CPDLC — "This aircraft has no CPDLC: only the PDC request (telex to X…) is available, no logon and no ATC requests". The aircraft is identified by the telemetry `aircraftTitle`; the list of aircraft without CPDLC comes only from a human check or the add-on documentation, with a date `[VERIFY]`. Unknown aircraft — no hint.
   Correction the same day: the decision was made on a wrong conclusion that the Fenix A320 has no CPDLC (ATC COMM opened AOC MENU first). The Fenix does have an ATC MENU (ATSU DLK: REQUEST, MSG RECORD, CONNECTION, REPORTS). Checked by a human the same day (MSFS 2024, Fenix A320, EFHK, PUSHBACK): ATC MENU → CONNECTION → NOTIFY to `XFIN` → a `cpdlc` packet `/data2/4//Y/REQUEST LOGON`, the station answered `LOGON ACCEPTED` and `CURRENT ATC UNIT@_@XFIN@_@HELSINKI CTL`, status "linked to XFIN". The Fenix is not in the list of aircraft without CPDLC; there are no known aircraft without CPDLC yet. Requests (REQUEST) and WILCO from the Fenix — `[VERIFY]`.
5. Messages do not arrive at the gate. Same flight: after landing the FMS resets INIT (according to the user, on all aircraft), FLT NBR disappears, the aircraft stops picking up telex; block_report and welcome_turnaround (`sent`) arrived only after FLT NBR was entered again (`06`, Hoppie, "Verified by a human" 2026-10-10). **User decision (2026-10-10, option 1 of three):** a hint only; the send time and addressee of the messages do not change. On OOOI IN — a system line in the feed (and in "Status" until the flight is closed): "The aircraft clears INIT after landing: re-enter FLT NBR {callsign} to receive messages at the gate". For all aircraft. Rejected: sending the turnaround messages earlier (on ON/TAXI_IN) and addressing them to the next flight.
**Done when:** core tests for each case in item 1; replay is deterministic; en/ru translations; the item 5 line on OOOI IN (controller test); the item 4 hint for an aircraft from the list and its absence for an unknown one.
**Done (2026-10-15):** item 1 — the `ATC_MISROUTED` event (`atc/dcl.ts`, `atc/xatc.ts`): telex to a FIR station (PDC request → the airport station is named while the exchange is open, otherwise "PDC unavailable"; other telex), logon to a PDC station, other opening messages and telex to a PDC station; replies with `mrn` do not count. Nothing goes to the network. Stations the core does not poll (a PDC station after takeoff, a foreign `X…`) do not get the message — the transport does not listen to them, so no hint is possible (we did not poll them just for a hint: extra Hoppie polling). `ATC_LOGON` carries `expected` when logging on to a wrong station. Item 2 — feed lines (`atc_misrouted_*`, `atc_logon_wrong`, en/ru) and under "Status" "CPDLC logon → XFIN · PDC (telex) → XFHK" (`shared/atc-hints.ts`). Item 4 — the mechanism and the "no CPDLC" hint exist, the `NO_CPDLC_AIRCRAFT` list is empty (none known, the Fenix has CPDLC), an unknown aircraft gets no hint. Item 5 — the system line `flt_nbr_hint` on OOOI IN and a line under "Status" on ON_BLOCK. Tests: `atc.test.ts` ("wrong XATC station"), `atc-hints.test.ts`, `controller.test.ts`.
**Human check:** after parking at the gate — the FLT NBR hint; after entering FLT NBR, block_report and welcome_turnaround arrive. Fenix A320 or A350: PDC to a FIR station → hint in the feed; PDC to the DCL station → clearance as before. Fenix A320: logon via ATC MENU → CONNECTION to `X`+FIR — does the status show "linked to X…"? Open question: does the Fenix show a `cpdlc` packet with the CLD clearance (if not, PDC for the Fenix will have to be sent by telex, as in the US; a separate decision).

#### T50 — Irregular: random hold duration and choice of diversion airport **R**
Found during a scenario review on 2026-10-15. (1) In `drone_activity`, `runway_closure_dest`, `dest_fire` the hold is fixed (15/15/20 min): the message says "EXPECT HOLDING 15 MIN", and exactly 15 min after the HOLD reply the all-clear arrives — predictable. (2) "Nearest suitable" (`flight.nearest_altn`: `medical_onboard`, `MEDICAL`, `emergency_mayday`, `emergency_pan`, `unruly_passenger`, `ped_fire_cabin`) is chosen only from the departure airport and the destination alternates (`context.ts`, `irrVariables`): over the ocean the "nearest" turns out to be an airport 1000+ NM away, although the OFP may contain en-route alternates.

**Part A — hold duration. User decision (2026-10-15):**
- `drone_activity` — from 30 min to 3 h;
- `runway_closure_dest` — from 30 min to closure of the airport indefinitely;
- `dest_fire` — from 20 min to 2 h.

Proposals 1–6 were **approved by the user on 2026-10-15** as they stand:
1. Distribution — uniform by the minute from the seed (`Rng`); the value is chosen when the scenario fires and is written to the state (replay is deterministic). The pilot does not know the true duration.
2. `runway_closure_dest` — duration by the drawn reason (`pick`): FOD INSPECTION and BIRD STRIKE CHECK — 30–60 min; DISABLED ACFT — 60–180 min or (in 30% of cases of this reason) the airport is closed until further notice. In total, "closed indefinitely" is about 10% of firings. The numbers are game values, approved.
3. Indefinite closure: at the first update (item 4) — `{dest} AD CLSD UFN`, "DIVERT {altn}", `set flight.divert: {ofp.altn}` without a choice (nothing to wait for). Text approved.
4. Instead of "EXPECT HOLDING N MIN" — "NO ESTIMATE, NEXT UPDATE {hhmm}Z" and updates every 20 min of flight time: "ARRIVALS STILL SUSPENDED, NEXT UPDATE …, REPLY HOLD OR DIVERT" (the pilot can leave for the alternate at any update, HOLD by default). XATC (T48): `EXPECT FURTHER CLEARANCE AT` = the time of the next update, a new UM93 at each update. The all-clear (`ARRIVALS RESUMED`, etc.) — when the drawn duration has expired, not on the update grid.
5. **Fuel** (most flights cannot manage a 3 h hold). Minimum to leave: `fuel.alternate_burn + fuel.reserve` from the OFP (the fields exist in the reference OFP, `parse.ts`: `alternateBurnKg`, `reserveKg`). When `tm.fuel_kg` ≤ minimum + 10% — "MIN DIVERSION FUEL, RECOMMEND DIVERT {altn}", reply DIVERT / HOLD, by default after 5 min — DIVERT. Without fuel in the telemetry — the hold reserve by plan: `(extra + contingency) / avg_fuel_flow` (`avg_fuel_flow` exists in the OFP, units `[VERIFY]`); then the same message. The 10% threshold and 5 min are game values, approved.
6. The hold also ends when `flight.divert != null` (the pilot's `DIVERT <ICAO>` command or another scenario) — currently `wait until` waits only for landing; landing during the hold — no all-clear, as now.

**Part B — choice of diversion airport. Proposals 1–6 approved by the user on 2026-10-15 as they stand:**
1. Candidates from the OFP: en-route alternates (`enroute_altn`, ERA), suitable ETOPS airports (`etops`), the takeoff alternate (`takeoff_altn`), destination alternates (`alternate`), the departure airport; the destination is excluded. The fields `enroute_altn`/`etops`/`takeoff_altn` exist in the SimBrief response but are empty in the reference OFP (RCTP–RJBB A35K) — the structure of the entries is `[VERIFY]` on a real OFP with ERA/ETOPS (for example a transatlantic A359/B77W, "ETOPS" and "ERA" in the SimBrief planner) and to be added as a trimmed fixture. These are dispatcher plans: the aerodromes are already matched to the aircraft type.
2. Fallback if the nearest candidate from the OFP is farther than 250 NM (≈ 30–35 min of flight; the threshold is a game value, approved): OurAirports `airports.csv` (`large_airport`/`medium_airport` with scheduled service — like the T31 VHF stations, a 300 NM corridor from the route, 30-day cache) + `runways.csv`: the longest open hard-surface runway not shorter than required for the aircraft family (T40 `aircraft.family`) and not narrower than required. The "family → runway length/width" table is from the manufacturers' Airport Planning (ACAP) documents, landing distance at MLW, sea level, **R** `[VERIFY]` against Airbus/Boeing documents with a check date. For the A380 — additionally width 45 m and `large_airport` (OurAirports has no aerodrome code F — an assumption). No family in the table → only candidates from the OFP, as now. General aviation aerodromes will not qualify this way.
3. Which rule applies where:
   - MAYDAY, `ped_fire_cabin` — the nearest of all candidates (items 1 and 2), time matters more;
   - PAN, medical (`medical_onboard`, `MEDICAL`), `unruly_passenger` — the nearest from the OFP (item 1); the item 2 fallback — only if the OFP candidates are beyond the threshold;
   - `drone_activity`, `dest_fire`, `runway_closure_dest`, `curfew_dest`, `altn_wx_deterioration` (LOW) — the destination alternate from the OFP, as now (that is what it is planned for).
4. Distance — straight line from the current position (as now). The item 2 fallback airport has coordinates → XATC (T48) issues a descent too, not only a Direct To.
5. The fallback candidates go into `CoreInput.data` (the flight recording), the core stays pure.
6. A candidate with a live METAR below minima is not excluded (approved 2026-10-15); a minima filter is a separate decision if it turns out to be needed.

**Assumptions (approved by the user on 2026-10-15):** uniform duration distribution from the seed; runway-closure durations by reason (30–60 / 60–180 min, 30% of DISABLED ACFT — UFN); UFN closure → diversion without a choice; updates every 20 min with a new UM93; fuel minimum `alternate_burn + reserve` + 10%, DIVERT by default after 5 min, without fuel in the telemetry — the reserve by plan; the 250 NM threshold for the OurAirports fallback; the runway table from ACAP (source and check date — at implementation, `[VERIFY]` marks remain); A380 — width 45 m and `large_airport`. Message texts — as in the card. The `[VERIFY]` marks (structure of `enroute_altn`/`etops`/`takeoff_altn`, units of `avg_fuel_flow`, the ACAP table) are checked before use.
**Done when:** tests: duration within bounds and deterministic by seed; updates and UM93 on the grid; UFN closure → diversion; fuel minimum → DIVERT recommendation (with fuel and without); the pilot's `DIVERT` interrupts the hold; airport choice by the Part B item 3 rules on an OFP fixture with ERA/ETOPS and on an OurAirports fixture (including the A380 not going to a short runway); scenario snapshots updated deliberately; replay is deterministic; en/ru translations for the scenario descriptions.
**Done (2026-10-15), Part A:** scenario language — `draw` (random `run.*` from the seed), `loop` (`until` / `stop` / `every_min`, `loop.min`, outcomes `MET` / `STOP`), `break`, `if` + `steps`, `wait: reply` with `timeout_round` (a round boundary, the pilot's reply does not shift the grid), the template `|plusmin:N` (`05`, "Random values and loops"); `drone_activity` 30 min – 3 h, `dest_fire` 20 min – 2 h, `runway_closure_dest` by reason (FOD / BIRD STRIKE 30–60, DISABLED ACFT 60–180, 3 out of 30 — UFN → diversion at the first update); updates every 20 min ("ARRIVALS STILL SUSPENDED …, NEXT UPDATE hhmmZ"), the all-clear when the drawn time is up; at each update XATC sends a new UM93 (`atc/xatc.ts`); the scenario `hold_fuel_watch` (fuel ≤ `alternate_burn + reserve` + 10% → "MIN DIVERSION FUEL", DIVERT by default after 5 min); variables `flight.holding`, `flight.hold_fuel_low`. Tests: `core/test/loop-draw.test.ts`, `scenarios/test/t50.test.ts`.
**Deviations from the Part A card (accepted by the user on 2026-10-15 as implemented):** (1) the reply to "MIN DIVERSION FUEL" is `DIVERT` / `STAY`, not `HOLD`: the engine gives the word to the oldest pending question, and `HOLD` would have taken over the hold loop while the flight left on timeout; (2) the reserve by plan without fuel in the telemetry is computed from the average cruise burn `enroute_burn / est_time_enroute`, not from `avg_fuel_flow`: the reference OFP has no such field (the card wrongly said "exists in the OFP"), units not verified; (3) the "NEXT UPDATE" time is clock time (`flight.now`) + 20 min, the update grid is flight time: at ×N they diverge; (4) a HOLD reply between updates is just an acknowledgement, with no new message.
**Done (2026-10-15), Part B — mechanism:** `scenario/divert.ts` (`pickDivert`: OFP candidates, fallback beyond 250 NM, the "nearest of all" rule for MAYDAY and `ped_fire_cabin` — variables `flight.nearest_any` / `_nm`; PAN, medical, unruly passenger — `flight.nearest_altn`); the OurAirports fallback (`data-sources/src/ourairports.ts`: `fetchDivertAirports`, 300 NM corridor, longest open hard-surface runway, the `airports.csv` and `runways.csv` fields checked 2026-10-15; the `divert` source in `ExternalDataService`, 30-day cache); `CoreInput.data.divertApts` (flight recording); XATC builds the Direct To and descent for the fallback airport too. Tests: `core/test/divert.test.ts`, `data-sources/test/divert-airports.test.ts`, `scenarios/test/t50.test.ts`.
**Runway table (user decision 2026-10-15):** `RUNWAY_NEEDS` (`divert.ts`) is filled in for A320, A330, A350, A380, B737, B747, B777, B787. Length — the landing distance (landing field length) of the family's "longest" variant: dry runway, sea level (ISA), maximum landing weight, for Boeing — flaps 30; taken from the manufacturer's document chart and rounded up to 50 m. Sources (downloaded and read 2026-10-15): Airbus AC A321 Rev. 35, A330 Rev. 34, A350 Rev. 17, A380 Rev. 20 (aircraft.airbus.com, section 3-4); Boeing ACAP D6-38A004 Rev K (737 MAX), D6-58325-7 Rev C (737NG), D6-58326-3 Rev E (747-8), D6-58329-2 Rev G (777-200LR/-300ER/F), D6-58333 Rev Q (787) (boeing.com/commercial/airports/plan-manuals, section 3.4). For Airbus the value was read from the PDF vector curves, for Boeing — by pixels of a raster chart (±10 m). The curves in the documents are "for information", the assumption approved. Result, m: A320 1750 (A321-200), A330 1950 (-900), A350 2150 (-1000), A380 1950, B737 1800 (737-10), B747 2250 (747-8F), B777 1900 (777-300ER), B787 1900 (787-10). Width — a game rule (Annex 14 is not openly available): widebodies 45 m, narrowbodies 30 m; the A380 — also `large_airport`. Regional families (E-Jet, CRJ, ATR, DH8) and the 777X have no entry: the documents were not checked — for them only OFP airports. Tests: `core/test/divert.test.ts` (including the A380 not going to a short, narrow runway or to a `medium_airport`).
**ETOPS from the OFP (2026-10-15):** the `etops` structure was checked against the user's real OFP (RJTT–KLAX A35K, rule 370, generated 2026-10-15): an object `rule` (min), `entry` / `exit` (`icao_code`, `pos_lat_apt` / `pos_long_apt` — airport, `pos_lat_fix` / `pos_long_fix` — fix), `suitable_airport[]` (`icao_code`, `name`, `pos_lat`, `pos_long`, the window `suitability_start` / `_end`, METAR/TAF/NOTAM), `equal_time_point[]`, `critical_point`; without ETOPS — `etops: []`. Parsing — `Ofp.etops` (`ofp/parse.ts`: rule, entry, exit, suitable airports); diversion candidates — departure, alternates, then ETOPS (entry, suitable, exit), destination excluded; an ETOPS airport is "known" for `DIVERT <ICAO>` (T51). Fixture `core/test/fixtures/ofp/rjtt-klax-a35k-etops.json` (`tools/anonymize-ofp.mjs --with-etops`). Tests: `core/test/ofp.test.ts`, `core/test/divert.test.ts` (over the Pacific at the ETP — RJCM, without ETOPS — the departure airport).
**Part B — NOT done, waiting for data:** `enroute_altn` (ERA) and `takeoff_altn` are not parsed: in the OFP with ETOPS both are empty arrays (the request's `api_params` has no ERA parameter — ERA was not enabled in the planner), the structure of the elements is not verified. An OFP with ERA and a takeoff alternate is needed, then — parsing and a fixture.
**Human check:** FBW A32NX, XATC logon, a hold from `drone_activity` → `HOLD AT` and a new `EXPECT FURTHER CLEARANCE AT` arrive on CPDLC at every update; a transatlantic with ERA in the OFP, `MEDICAL` in mid-ocean → `ROGER DIVERT` to the en-route alternate, not to the departure airport.

#### T51 — Irregular: random moment in cruise and the airport in the `DIVERT <ICAO>` reply **R**
Found during a review on 2026-10-15. (1) The trigger `on: phase, phase: CRUISE` is an edge — "in the phase and conditions true" (`engine.ts`, `triggered`): the scenario starts on the first cruise tick, i.e. about a minute after levelling off (CLIMB → CRUISE, 60 s of stability, `04`). `medical_onboard`, `unruly_passenger`, `ped_fire_cabin`, `tech_defect_mel` never happen in the middle or at the end of cruise. (2) A reply to a scenario question — the first word is the option, the rest is `reply.arg`: `DIVERT EGLL` leads to `flight.nearest_altn`, and `EGLL` ends up in `CREW INFO`; the airport can be chosen only by a separate `DIVERT <ICAO>` command after the reply.

**Part A — random moment in cruise. Proposals 1–6 approved by the user on 2026-10-15 as they stand:**
1. Scope: `medical_onboard`, `unruly_passenger`, `ped_fire_cabin`, `tech_defect_mel`. T29 news (`special_news`), external events, `curfew_dest`, `fuel_monitoring`, `emergency_*` — unchanged (they are tied to data or to a condition, not to chance).
2. Engine mechanism: a new trigger field `window: { after_min: 10, until: "<end condition>" }` for `on: phase`. On the edge (entering cruise, conditions true) — a `probability` roll as now (seed of the same scenario, the per-flight frequency does not change); on success a fraction `f` ∈ [0, 1) is chosen (seed) and the scenario is **deferred**, not started.
3. The moment — by the **fraction of the remaining path**, not by the clock: on entry `T0 = flight.minutes_to_tod` is remembered; start when `flight.minutes_to_tod ≤ T0 − 10 − f × (T0 − 10 − threshold)`, where the threshold comes from the scenario's condition (30 min for medical and unruly passenger, 20 for fire, 30 for MEL). So the moment does not depend on pauses, ×N or wind and always falls in the window. Uniform in the window [levelling off + 10 min; TOD − threshold]. The "+10 min" (the seat belt sign goes off, service begins) is a game number.
4. At the start moment the scenario's conditions, `cooldown` and `excludes` are checked again: if a diversion has begun in the meantime or a mutually exclusive scenario has fired — skip (`SCENARIO_SKIPPED`, reason as now). Mutual exclusion is kept: whichever has the earlier moment comes first.
5. A window shorter than 10 min (a short cruise) — the scenario does not run, as now when `minutes_to_tod` < the threshold.
6. The deferred start is part of the engine state (serialized), the recording and replay are deterministic. "Run now" works as before (without the window).

**Part B — `DIVERT <ICAO>` in the reply. Proposals 1–6 approved by the user on 2026-10-15 as they stand:**
1. Scope — all DIVERT branches: `medical_onboard`, `cmd_medical`, `unruly_passenger`, `ped_fire_cabin`, `emergency_mayday`, `emergency_pan`, `drone_activity`, `dest_fire`, `runway_closure_dest`.
2. A new launch variable `reply.icao`: the first word of `reply.arg`, if it is a **known** airport — from the OFP (departure, alternates, ERA/ETOPS per T50) or from OurAirports `airports.csv` (already loaded for T29). A lookup against the directory is needed because 4-letter words (`SICK`, `PAIN`, `CALM`) would otherwise be taken for an ICAO code. Without a loaded directory — only OFP airports. Not the destination and only airborne — as with the `DIVERT` command. The rest after the ICAO is `reply.arg` for `CREW INFO`.
3. Branches: `set: { flight.divert: "{reply.icao|or:<as now>}" }` — without an ICAO the behaviour is as before (nearest per T50 or the OFP alternate); a timeout with `default: DIVERT` (fire, MAYDAY) — also the previous choice.
4. The suitability of the airport named by the pilot is not checked — the captain's decision (the same in reality). The distance in the reply — if the coordinates are known (OFP or `airports.csv`), otherwise the line is not printed.
5. An unknown ICAO (`DIVERT ZZZZ`) — diversion to the default choice and the line "ZZZZ UNKNOWN, DIVERT <airport>" in the reply. Text approved.
6. XATC (T48): Direct To the chosen airport; descent — when it has coordinates.

Order: after T50 (the same scenario files and default airport choice).
**Assumptions (approved by the user on 2026-10-15):** Part A scope — `medical_onboard`, `unruly_passenger`, `ped_fire_cabin`, `tech_defect_mel`; the `probability` roll on entering cruise, the moment — uniform by path fraction in the window [levelling off + 10 min; TOD − the scenario's threshold]; at the start moment a re-check of conditions, `cooldown`, `excludes`; window < 10 min — the scenario does not run. Part B — `reply.icao` only for a known airport (OFP or OurAirports `airports.csv`), not the destination, only airborne; without an ICAO and on timeout — the default choice; the suitability of the pilot's airport is not checked (captain's decision); an unknown ICAO — the default choice and the line "<ICAO> UNKNOWN, DIVERT <airport>".
**Done when:** engine tests: the window (the moment within bounds, deterministic by seed, pause and ×N do not shift the fraction, re-check of conditions and `excludes` at the start moment, serialization in the middle of waiting, a short cruise); scenario tests: `DIVERT EGLL PAX STABLE` → `flight.divert = EGLL`, `CREW INFO PAX STABLE`; `DIVERT SICK` → the nearest, `SICK` in the text; an unknown ICAO; the destination is rejected; snapshots updated deliberately; replay is deterministic.
**Done (2026-10-15):** Part A — the field `trigger.window: { after_min, before_tod_min }` for `on: phase` (`types.ts` `StartWindow`, `engine.ts` `defer` / `dueWindows`, the `pending` state is serialized): the `probability` roll on the edge, then the fraction `f` from the seed and waiting for the moment `minutes_to_tod ≤ T0 − after_min − f × (T0 − after_min − before_tod_min)`; at the start moment the conditions, cooldown, excludes are checked again (`SCENARIO_SKIPPED` with reasons `conditions` / `excluded`; `window` — the window closed or cruise ended); "run now" without the window. The window is applied to `medical_onboard`, `unruly_passenger` (up to TOD − 30), `ped_fire_cabin` (− 20), `tech_defect_mel` (− 30; the condition `flight.minutes_to_tod ≥ 30` was added). Part B — `reply.icao` (a known airport: OFP, the T50 fallback, or found in `airports.csv`), `reply.unknown`, `flight.divert_nm`; the format `|else:<variable>` and optional lines `~…` in templates; the core asks for the airport's location (`DATA_REQUEST` `place`, waits `dataWaitMs`), the app answers `places` (`ExternalDataService.requestPlace`, the whole `airports.csv` once per session); the DIVERT branches of `medical_onboard`, `cmd_medical`, `unruly_passenger`, `ped_fire_cabin`, `emergency_mayday`, `emergency_pan`, `drone_activity`, `dest_fire`, `runway_closure_dest`; XATC builds a Direct To the named airport. Tests: `core/test/window.test.ts`, `core/test/loop-draw.test.ts`, `scenarios/test/t51.test.ts`, `data-sources/test/divert-airports.test.ts`.
**T51 decisions (accepted by the user on 2026-10-15 as implemented):** (1) An "unknown ICAO" cannot be told apart from a word like SICK by the directory (both are absent from the file): implemented so — a word that is not found stays text (`CREW INFO: SICK`), and the line "ZZZZ UNKNOWN, DIVERT <airport>" is printed only for `ZZZZ` (the code for "aerodrome not in the list" in flight plans). If another criterion is needed — tell me; (2) the directory is the whole `airports.csv` (large/medium/small with a 4-letter code), not only large/medium: the pilot may name a small airport; (3) the `window` is given by the numbers `after_min` / `before_tod_min`, not by an "end condition"; (4) "window shorter than 10 min" is understood as: less than `after_min + before_tod_min` minutes remain from entering cruise.
**Human check:** a long flight (≥ 3 h of cruise), medical "%" set to 100 in settings → the message arrives in the middle of cruise, not at the TOC; the reply `DIVERT <ICAO of an alternate not from the OFP>` → that airport in the reply, and on CPDLC (XATC logon) — a Direct To it.

#### T52 — PMDG 737 (MSFS 2024): display profile and CPDLC check
User request 2026-10-15: the PMDG 737 for MSFS 2024 has gained CPDLC through Hoppie. The official PMDG changelog could not be read (forum.pmdg.com is closed behind a Cloudflare check, 2026-10-15). From user reports (PMDG forum, AVSIM, early 2026): CPDLC/Hoppie exists in the 737 for MSFS 2024; the Hoppie code must be entered manually and the Hoppie network selected in the aircraft settings; the CPDLC sound disappears after a few messages (an aircraft bug). CPDLC fixes in the 737 via Hoppie were already in the MSFS 2020 version (PMDG news 11 Jul 2024). CDU width, the comma, pages, telex reception, DCL and auto-logon after HANDOVER — not described anywhere, `[VERIFY]` by a human.

The XATC station (T23–T27) speaks the common Hoppie `/data2/…` format and does not depend on the aircraft — no separate code for the 737 is needed until the check shows differences.
1. Built-in profile `b73x`: ICAO `B73*` and MAX (`B37M`, `B38M`, `B39M`, `B3XM` — as in `aircraft.ts`), title `\b(B?737)`. Width — base (24), characters — default.
2. Human check (below); results go to `06-integrations.md`, as for the Fenix and iniBuilds. Based on them — the `b73x` width and characters, and the CPDLC differences (reply phrases, logon, HANDOVER) — a separate station change in this same card or a new one.

**Assumptions (approved by the user on 2026-10-15):** the `b73x` width = 24 (base, like the CDU) until a human check; character set — `DEFAULT_ALLOWED_CHARS`.
**Done when:** the profile is built in, selected by the ICAO of the 737 NG/MAX and by the title `PMDG 737-800`; `pnpm check` is green.
**Human check:** MSFS 2024, PMDG 737-800, Hoppie code entered manually and the Hoppie network selected.
1) Telex: `pnpm hoppie:telex --to <callsign> --profile b73x` → where it is shown (ACARS → RECEIVED MSGS?), the header, whether the lines fit exactly 24 characters and the `---` rules, whether the comma is visible, how many lines per page, whether there is an indication (FMC/ACARS light, sound).
2) Pilot reply: free text from ACARS to the OPS station → did a `telex` arrive in the app feed.
3) CPDLC: ATC LOGON to `X`+FIR (the "XATC station" setting) → `LOGON ACCEPTED`, CURRENT ATC UNIT on the CDU; `REQUEST FL…` → `CLIMB TO`; WILCO / STANDBY / UNABLE replies — how they went out (text in the feed).
4) PDC: a clearance request to `X`+departure airport → the clearance display, WILCO/UNABLE buttons.
5) FIR boundary: `HANDOVER` → LOGOFF from the old station and whether it logs on to the new one by itself.
