6) If a 737-700 is available (April 2026) — repeat items 1 and 3 briefly.

#### T53 — Next flight: airline hub and route from the hub **R**
Found 2026-10-15: the next flight (`ops.next_flight`, scenarios `next_leg`, `aircraft_change`, `welcome_turnaround`) is always "back to the departure airport, number + 1" — also after a flight into the hub. An overnight stay (the last flight of the day) — **we do not do it** (user decision 2026-10-15): there is no schedule data, only assumptions.

1. The airline's hub — the airport with the largest number of its own direct routes in OpenFlights `routes.dat` (the same source and licence as in T43; data from 2014). A tie for first place — no hub (we do not pick anything at random). The `OPENFLIGHTS_HUB` table is generated by `tools/openflights/generate.mjs`.
2. The "Home hub (ICAO)" setting in the app overrides OpenFlights — for virtual airlines and changed hubs. One for all flights; empty — OpenFlights.
3. A flight **into the hub** (destination = hub, departure ≠ hub): the next flight is from the hub along a real airline route (`OPENFLIGHTS_HUB_ROUTES`), seeded-randomly, except the airport the aircraft came from. Only routes flown by **this aircraft type**: the type's ICAO from the OFP → IATA via OpenFlights `planes.dat`, compared with the route's `equipment` field. No suitable routes (a type not in the 2014 data, for example A20N/B38M; a hub from the setting that is not in the airline's data; the airline is not in OpenFlights) — back to the departure airport, as before.
4. The number of such a flight is seeded-random, the same length as the current one (not equal to it). The return flight — as before, number + 1.
5. All other cases (from the hub, between two non-hubs, hub unknown) — back to the departure airport, number + 1, as before.
6. New variables `ops.next_orig`, `ops.next_dest` — the airport pair in scenarios instead of `{ofp.dest}-{ofp.orig}`. Randomness — a separate Rng stream `${seed}/#next`: the order of `#ops` draws does not change.
7. The core option `nextLegVersion` (like `namesVersion`): 2 — per this card (default and in the app), 1 — as before; in the recording header; recordings without the field are replayed as 1. The hub from the setting — the option `hub`, also in the header.

**Assumptions (approved by the user on 2026-10-15):** the hub — by the number of OpenFlights routes plus the "Home hub (ICAO)" setting; after a flight into the hub — a seeded-random airline route from the hub, filtered by aircraft type (2014 `equipment` and `planes.dat`), otherwise back; the number — seeded-random of the same length; non-hub → non-hub — back, as before.
**Done when:** core tests: the hub from OpenFlights and from the setting; a flight into the hub → a route from the hub for the right type, deterministic by seed, not to the departure airport; a type without routes → back; a flight from the hub and non-hub → back with number + 1; `nextLegVersion: 1` → as before; scenarios use `ops.next_orig`/`ops.next_dest`; replay of old recordings does not change; `pnpm check` is green.
**Human check:** the app, the "Home hub (ICAO)" setting empty; SimBrief AFL OFP (A320) flight URSS–UUEE → in `NEXT LEG`/`WELCOME` the next flight is `UUEE-<airport>` with a different number, not `UUEE-URSS`; OFP UUEE–URSS → `URSS-UUEE`, number + 1; "Home hub (ICAO)" = `URSS` and flight UUEE–URSS → the return flight (`URSS-UUEE`), since the AFL data has no routes from URSS.
#### T54 — Simulator effects: emergency services meet the arriving aircraft **R**
User request 2026-10-15. In an in-flight emergency the landing airport meets the aircraft with fire trucks (and an ambulance): the vehicles wait at positions beside the runway, after touchdown they follow the aircraft, and after it stops they surround it. All automatic via scenarios, no manual buttons. The idea comes from the MSFS mod Emergency Response On Demand v1.97 (TechN9ne225, analysed 2026-10-15: SimConnect `AICreateSimulatedObject` + `SetDataOnSimObject` `AI WAYPOINT LIST`, runway threshold via `RequestFacilityData`, manual start from an HTML panel). The mod has no licence and no source code, so only ideas and timings are taken, no code (same rule: no code from other apps is copied). Depends on T21, T34 (same way of creating objects), T50 (diversion airport).
**User decision (2026-10-15):** option A — own implementation in the bridge (not an integration with the mod's HTTP API and not static vehicles only).

**Core and scenarios**
1. Scenario step `rescue: { level: full | standby | medical }` and `rescue: stop`. The core issues a `rescue` command with the airport (`flight.divert`, otherwise `ofp.dest`) and the level; recorded and replayed like `smoke`.
2. Triggers (approved 2026-10-15): `emergency_mayday`, `ped_fire_cabin` → `full`; `emergency_pan` → `standby` (vehicles stand at their positions and do not follow the aircraft); `medical_onboard` and the `MEDICAL` command (`cmd_medical`) → `medical` — an ambulance at the stand after on-block (item 6); `hard_landing_check`, `dest_fire` — no reception.
3. When the level is raised (PAN, then MAYDAY) the higher one applies. `rescue: stop` — on cancelling the alert, stopping the flight and on exit.

**App (`controller.ts`)**
4. To the bridge — `rescue_start {level, rwy: {thrLat, thrLon, hdg, lengthM}, count, titles}`. Runway threshold and heading — OurAirports `runways.csv` (fields `le_*`/`he_*`, cache as in T34). Landing runway — the planned one from the OFP (`destination.plan_rwy`, `[VERIFY]` SimBrief field), without it — the longest. Landed on another runway — the vehicles drive to the stopping place.
5. Settings: "Rescue services in the simulator" (on/off), SimObject title lists (fire trucks, ambulance) — editable. Siren — a WAV played by the app (sound on the PC, not in 3D), off by default.
6. Default titles — `ASO_Firetruck02`, `Truck Fire Long 02`, `Truck Fire Short`, `Truck Fire Short 02`, `SUV_NorthAm` (from the mod's INI); presence in MSFS 2024 and 2020 `[VERIFY]` by a human. Ambulance object not found — `[VERIFY]` by a human (Dev Mode → Tools → SimObject Spawner). No object — the `medical` level gives only a message, no vehicle.

**Bridge (`RescueController`: logic without SimConnect, under tests, like `SmokeController`)**
7. States: `Idle` → `Staged` (create when approaching within ≤ 15 NM of the airport, vehicles beside the runway) → `Trailing` (`full` only: after touchdown, `SIM ON GROUND`, they follow the aircraft) → `Encircle` (aircraft stopped) → `Done` (remove 10 min after stopping, on `rescue_stop`, when the aircraft is farther than 30 NM — go-around, at the end of the SimConnect session; in a new session — create again if the request is active).
8. Geometry and timings — from the mod: 45 m from the runway centreline, 70 m between vehicles, 100 m from the threshold; following 25 m behind the aircraft; "stopped" — < 1 kt for longer than 2 s; circle of 50 m radius; route update every 350 ms.
9. Movement — `SetDataOnSimObject` with `AI WAYPOINT LIST`; presence of `SIMCONNECT_DATA_WAYPOINT` in the local wrapper (`SimConnectLibs/sdk2024`) `[VERIFY]`. Creation/removal — `AICreateSimulatedObject` / `AIRemoveObject` (the mod uses the plain call, not `_EX1`, with these titles — probably works for us too, `[VERIFY]` by a human). Object not created — a line in the feed, the other vehicles continue.

**Realism (data)**
10. Number of vehicles: aerodrome rescue and firefighting category — by aircraft (`aircraft.family` T40: fuselage length and width, ICAO Annex 14, Vol. I, Table 9-1), minimum vehicles by category: 1–5 → 1, 6–7 → 2, 8–10 → 3 — so far only from a secondary source (KCAA presentation, ICAO ESAF 2022, referring to Doc 9137), `[VERIFY]` against Annex 14 / Doc 9137 Part 1 with a check date before use. Plus a SUV for the fire commander.
11. Arrival time (ICAO guideline — 3 min to the middle of the farthest runway, `[VERIFY]`) is not modelled: the vehicles are at their positions in advance.

**Assumptions (approved by the user 2026-10-15):** triggers of item 2; vehicles drive in a straight line between points (grass, buildings), not along taxiways; they stand by the runway in advance rather than leaving a depot; siren on the PC, not from the vehicle; thresholds of 15/30 NM and 10 min are game values; geometry and timings of item 8 are from the mod, not from regulations; SUV as the fire commander's vehicle; without an ambulance object `medical` is only a message. The `[VERIFY]` marks (field `plan_rwy`, `SIMCONNECT_DATA_WAYPOINT`, object titles, category table) are checked before use.
**Done when:** `RescueController` tests (state transitions, positions at the threshold, following, circle, creation/removal thresholds, re-creation in a new session) in `bridge:test`; tests of the `rescue` step in the core and replay; `controller.ts` tests (runway choice, "longest" fallback); category table under tests; `pnpm check` and `pnpm bridge:test` green.
**Objects (human check 2026-10-17, MSFS 2024, DevMode → SimObject Spawner, "All packages"):** all those listed below are created with the Spawn button and stand on the ground. Fire trucks: `ASO_Firetruck02` (airport, white), `Truck Fire Airport Medium` (airport, light green), `Microsoft_Truck_Fire_Medium_Red` (airport, red, animated), `FSDT_Firetruck_Panther_6x6_red` (GSX add-on, animated), city trucks `Truck Fire Long 02`, `Microsoft_Truck_Fire_Long` (animated), `Truck Fire Short`; off-road vehicle `Car Bush Firefighting`. Ambulances: `Car Bush Medic`, `Truck Utility Europe Medic Box`, `Truck Utility Europe Medic Flush`, `Truck Utility NorthAm Medic`, `Van Asia High Roof Medic`, `Van Asia High Roof Medic Japan`, `Van Asia Low Roof Medic` (the last — only in the list, did not spawn). `SUV_NorthAm` is not in the list; `Truck Fire Short 02` is not confirmed. **In brackets the Spawner shows the livery, not part of the `title`:** `Pickup 01` is one container with the liveries `AERIALAD_02`, `FIREFIGHT_01`, `TRAFFIC_DEFAULT` and others, i.e. the objects are modular. The livery is set only by `AICreateSimulatedObject_EX1` (`szLivery`, SDK 2024, checked 2026-10-17); it is not in the local wrapper, so only titles without a livery are used — with the default livery. Without brackets all the titles above look like fire trucks/ambulances; `Pickup 01` without brackets is a utility vehicle (blue-red), not used; the yellow livery of `ASO_Firetruck02` is unavailable without `_EX1` (T19). Whether the plain call creates modular objects — `[VERIFY]` in the human check (the ERoD mod creates `ASO_Firetruck02` with the same call). The user does not have MSFS 2020 — "not checked in 2020".
**User decisions (2026-10-17), replace items 6 and 10 and the assumption about the SUV:**
1. Number of fire trucks — the user's game rule, not ICAO: category 1–5 → 1, 6–7 → 4, 8–10 → 8. No fire commander's vehicle.
2. Fire trucks by landing airport — a game rule. Large airport — OurAirports `airports.csv`, `type = large_airport`; region — by the first letters of the ICAO code. Large, North America (`K`, `C`) → `Truck Fire Airport Medium`; large, other regions → `ASO_Firetruck02`, `Truck Fire Airport Medium` (before 2026-10-17 `Microsoft_Truck_Fire_Medium_Red` instead of the second, see below); not large, North America → `Truck Fire Long 02`, `Microsoft_Truck_Fire_Long`; not large, other → `Truck Fire Short`. `FSDT_Firetruck_Panther_6x6_red` (GSX) — not a default, can be added in the settings.
3. Ambulance by ICAO region — a game rule: `RJ`, `RO` → `Van Asia High Roof Medic Japan`; other `R`, `V`, `W`, `Z` → `Van Asia High Roof Medic`, `Van Asia Low Roof Medic`; `K`, `C` → `Truck Utility NorthAm Medic`; Europe (`E`, `L`) and all others → `Truck Utility Europe Medic Box`, `Truck Utility Europe Medic Flush`.
4. If a group has fewer models than there are vehicles, they alternate in a cycle. All groups are editable in the settings.
**Human check:** MSFS 2024, FBW A32NX, landing on the planned destination runway; MAYDAY on XATC in cruise, answer CONTINUE → 1) when approaching within ≤ 15 NM, fire trucks by category are visible at the threshold (A320 — 4, models by airport, decisions of 2026-10-17); 2) after touchdown they follow the aircraft; 3) after it stops they surround it; 4) after 10 min they disappear; 5) FPS does not drop noticeably. Repeat with PAN (vehicles stand, do not drive), with `MEDICAL` (ambulance at the stand, if the object is found) and in MSFS 2020 (do the titles exist). After the "vehicles drive away" fix (2026-10-17): 6) right after spawning, the vehicles and the ambulance stand and do not roll, on the ground, not under it; 7) right after spawning — where each vehicle faces (toward the runway or away from it), by model (`ASO_Firetruck02`, `Microsoft_Truck_Fire_Medium_Red`); do not introduce a per-model heading correction until the user has looked; 10) the lines "rescue slot N … hdg asked X, sim Y" in the bridge console — whether the heading matches for each model; 11) after touchdown the vehicles drive forwards, not backwards; vehicles ahead on the aircraft's heading wait until the aircraft has passed them, and only then follow; 12) to the circle point on the other side of the aircraft — around, not through the aircraft; 13) following a taxiing aircraft (taxi 200–300 m after touchdown): they drive behind, do not crawl; 14) heading of `ASO_Firetruck02` right after spawning — facing the runway or along it; 15) the vehicles stop when the bridge stops them (lines `arrived on the circle` / `stops`) and no longer drive; 16) all vehicles reach the circle around the aircraft; 17) on slew or a bounce the stage does not jump (no Trailing ↔ Staged alternation in the console); 19) bridge console: lines `created: sim hdg …`, `3 s after the set: …` and `rescue slot N … hdg H, body z V ft/s, track T, to target D m` during movement — send in full; 20) vehicles stand along the whole runway, after the aircraft stops at either end of the runway they arrive in a reasonable time and do not stop halfway; 22) after (b)+(A): smoothness of movement and FPS before and while the vehicles move; lines `position sets: N in 2.0 s, interval avg X ms` — the interval is close to a frame (~16–33 ms); all vehicles drive nose-first (light green and yellow); 21) after switching to movement by the bridge: smoothness (jerks at 10 Hz?), whether the wheels turn and the beacons light up with `SIM DISABLED`, whether they stand on the ground (OnGround = 1), where the nose points standing and moving (forward along travel), lines `in the sim: … m from where the bridge put it` — the discrepancy is close to 0; 18) `MEDICAL`: stop on the runway with the brake — the ambulance is next to it; taxi to the stand and brake — the ambulance appears at the aircraft there (console: "the ambulance is created beside it again"), it is no longer on the runway; 8) after touchdown they start and follow the aircraft, do not crawl; 9) on the circle they stop and stand.

**Done (2026-10-17, branch `task/T54-rescue`):** step `rescue: { level: full | standby | medical }` / `rescue: stop` (`types.ts` `RescueStep`, schema, `compile.ts`, `engine.ts`) → core command `rescue` (`sim/commands.ts` `RescueCommand`: airport `flight.divert`, otherwise destination; runway — planned only at the destination), recorded and replayed like `smoke`. Scenarios: `emergency_mayday` (both branches) and `ped_fire_cabin` → `full`, `emergency_pan` → `standby`, `medical_onboard`, `cmd_medical` → `medical` (before waiting for on-block). Rules of 2026-10-17 — `packages/core/src/sim/rescue.ts` (category groups by T40 family, 1/4/8 vehicles, region by ICAO, model groups, alternation). App (`controller.ts`): the senior fire-truck level (`standby` → `full`, not back), ambulance — separately from the level; runway threshold, heading and length — `runways.csv` (`parseLandingRunway`, 30-day cache), large airport — `airports.csv` `large_airport` (`parseLargeAirports`); to the bridge `rescue_start {level, thrLat, thrLon, hdg, lengthM, fire, medic}` (bridge arguments are flat: titles joined by `|`) and `rescue_stop` (scenario, flight stop, exit). Settings: "Rescue services in the simulator" (on by default), "Siren" (off; Web Audio tone, no WAV file), eight editable title groups and "Reset to defaults". Bridge: `RescueController` (states of item 7, geometry of item 8), commands in the whitelist, `SimConnectSource` creates the vehicles with the plain `AICreateSimulatedObject`, drives `AI WAYPOINT LIST` (`SIMCONNECT_DATA_WAYPOINT` is in `SimConnectLibs/sdk2024` — checked 2026-10-17, the bridge with SimConnect builds), aircraft position for the vehicles — a request every 350 ms while the vehicles are needed. Tests: `core/test/rescue.test.ts`, `scenarios/test/t54.test.ts`, `data-sources/test/rescue.test.ts`, `desktop/test/controller.test.ts` (T54), `replay/test/replay.test.ts` (T54), `bridge/msfs/test/AcarsBridge.Tests/RescueTests.cs`.
**User decisions on implementation (2026-10-17, all 8 proposals approved, items 1 and 2 with amendments):** (1) category group by T40 family: narrow-body A320/B737 — 6–7, wide-body — 8–10, regional — 1–5, except E190, E195, DH8D — category 6 (4 vehicles); a type not in the T40 table — 1–5 (1 vehicle); (2) without a planned runway (diversion or not in the OFP) — the longest, landing on the end whose heading is closer to the wind direction from the landing airport's METAR (live METAR, otherwise from the OFP; the core passes it in the `rescue` command as `windFromDeg`, into the recording); calm, VRB or no METAR — the `le` end; (3) vehicles stand to the right of the centreline in the landing direction, facing the runway; (4) following in a column, vehicle i — 25·(i + 1) m behind; speed — aircraft ground speed + 10 kt (20–60 kt), to the circle — 30 kt; (5) circle — evenly spaced, the first at the nose; (6) ambulance separately from the fire-truck level: one (the first model of the group), 30 m to the left of the aircraft, when it stands (< 1 kt for longer than 2 s) with the parking brake closer than 15 NM to the threshold, for 10 min; (7) seniority only for fire trucks (`standby` → `full`, not back); (8) siren — a tone generated by Web Audio (no WAV file and no licence question), with `full` from touchdown to the first stop.
**Human check 2026-10-17 (MSFS 2024, LEBL, A320 on the runway, script with `rescue_start` at the aircraft's point):** the vehicles and the ambulance are created and stand on the ground, but immediately slowly drive away in a straight line (some farther, onto the grass, some stop); the ambulance moved a little and stopped; one white and three red fire trucks. Cause: a ground object created by `AICreateSimulatedObject` without a route rolls by itself — not described in the SDK documentation; likewise in DevSupport "GroundVehicle … align to terrain but move away in fwd/rev" (topic 3557, 2021, answer from a non-Asobo person: "send their variable "SIM DISABLED" to 1, so you'll turn off their internal simulation").
**User decision (2026-10-17, "A + B"):** (A) each vehicle and the ambulance gets `SIM DISABLED = 1` right after its id is assigned (SimVar: Bool, settable, Miscellaneous Variables SDK 2024, checked 2026-10-17); before the route (touchdown with `full`) — 0, on the circle — 1 again, when the vehicle is closer than 5 m to its point or 30 s after the aircraft stops; on touch-and-go — 1 again. (B) when following — two route points: a target 25·(i + 1) m behind and a point another 30 m farther along the aircraft's heading, so the target is not the last one: in MSFS 2024 stock ground vehicles at the last waypoint drop their speed almost to zero (DevSupport, topic 10439, bug_logged, Asobo employee 2025-02: "needs to be fixed on our side but there are no fix candidates"). To the circle — one point. Fallback if `SIM DISABLED` does not hold or breaks landing on the ground: the events `FREEZE_LATITUDE_LONGITUDE_SET` / `FREEZE_ALTITUDE_SET` / `FREEZE_ATTITUDE_SET` (Key Events SDK 2024; described for aircraft, not confirmed for vehicles) — not implemented, to be applied only if the human check shows that `SIM DISABLED` does not hold the vehicles or breaks their landing on the ground; the user decides after the check. Heading at creation — `SIMCONNECT_DATA_INITPOSITION.Heading` "in degrees", all fire trucks get runway heading − 90°; different directions in the screenshot are either a turn while rolling away or a different front axis of the models `[VERIFY]` after the fix.
**Human check after cba3f71 (2026-10-17, LEBL runway 24, A320, script: `rescue_start full` at the aircraft's point, then slew "touchdown", the aircraft did not taxi):** `SIM DISABLED` holds — the vehicles and the ambulance stand on the ground. All 4 fire trucks stood parallel to the runway (nose toward the threshold), not facing the strip (runway heading − 90°). `ASO_Firetruck02` in slots 0 and 2 — once yellow, once white: the default livery of this modular model is not constant. After the "touchdown" the vehicles drove up and stood around the aircraft at about 50 m, but two red `Microsoft_Truck_Fire_Medium_Red` drove backwards, and the white ASO drove through the aircraft to its point on the other side. Following a taxiing aircraft not checked.
Per the documentation (SDK 2024, checked 2026-10-17): the flag `SIMCONNECT_WAYPOINT_REVERSE` ("the aircraft should back up to this waypoint… only valid on the first waypoint") is not set by us — the AI chooses reverse, when is not described; `SIMCONNECT_DATA_INITPOSITION.Heading` — "Heading in degrees", why the heading was not applied is not described; SimVar `PLANE HEADING DEGREES TRUE` — settable ✓ (Aircraft Misc Variables, default unit radians, we request in degrees). The likely cause of reversing: at touchdown, vehicles standing ahead on the heading got for ~2 s a follow point behind the aircraft, i.e. behind their own backs.
**User decisions (2026-10-17, "All three"; ASO — "Keep"):** (c) right after the id is assigned, together with `SIM DISABLED = 1`, set the heading explicitly — `PLANE HEADING DEGREES TRUE` = runway heading − 90° (ambulance — aircraft heading), then read the heading back and write to the bridge console "rescue slot N <title> hdg asked X, sim Y"; do not introduce a per-model heading correction until the user has looked at these lines; (a) before each new route (touchdown; moving out to the circle) turn the vehicle, while `SIM DISABLED = 1`, nose toward the first route point, then `SIM DISABLED = 0` and the route; (b) to the circle point — in a straight line if the line passes no closer than 40 m to the aircraft, otherwise go around an 80 m arc around the aircraft in the shortest direction in steps of no more than 45° and the last point is the circle point at 50 m; the vehicle's place is its position from the simulator (requested while the vehicle is moving: when following and to the circle), otherwise its place by the runway. `ASO_Firetruck02` stays in the default group: the random yellow/white livery is accepted (a livery can be set only by `_EX1`, T19). (d) user decision 2026-10-17 ("Yes, wait"): at touchdown a vehicle standing ahead of the aircraft on its heading gets no follow point and stands (`SIM DISABLED = 1`) until the aircraft has passed it (it is no longer ahead on the heading); then turns to its point and follows.
**Human check 9d936f7 / 1ad718f (2026-10-17, LEBL, A320, ground script + slew with climb):** the heading in the sim holds as set (console: `hdg asked 155, sim 155` for all; ambulance 245/245). After the slew touchdown the vehicles stood until the aircraft stopped; then drove to the circle (`to the circle, 1 / 5 / 5 / 1 waypoint(s)`), one arrived, three were stopped after 30 s. The log showed SIM ON GROUND chatter during the slew (100–240 kt): the stage jumped Trailing ↔ Staged, with turns on every touchdown. The red `Microsoft_Truck_Fire_Medium_Red`, after turning to the point (asked 217, sim 218), still drove backwards and after "stop" (SIM DISABLED = 1) kept driving. The ambulance stayed where it appeared before the slew.
**User decisions (2026-10-17, "All four"; "Yes, add"):** (1) touchdown — only if SIM ON GROUND = 1 continuously for ≥ 1 s; back to waiting by the runway (touch-and-go, go-around) — only after ≥ 5 s continuously in the air; a short lift-off (bounce, slew) changes nothing (game numbers); (2) the time limit for the way to the circle point per vehicle — length of its route / 15 m/s × 2 + 15 s, not more than 120 s; arrival (≤ 5 m) stops earlier; (3) stopping a moving vehicle: first a route of one point — its last known place, 0 kt (SPEED_REQUESTED), then SIM DISABLED = 1 (SIM DISABLED holds a vehicle without a route, but not one driving along AI WAYPOINT LIST — a human's observation; how to reset a route is not described in the SDK); fallback — remove and re-create at its place (AIRemoveObject + AICreateSimulatedObject) — not implemented, the user decides after the check; (4) `Microsoft_Truck_Fire_Medium_Red` removed from the group "large, other regions" (visually drives backwards: the sim drives it along the heading, the model is drawn the other way round; a heading correction will not help); `Truck Fire Airport Medium` added to the group — now `ASO_Firetruck02` and `Truck Fire Airport Medium` alternate; the model can be restored in the settings. (5) user decision 2026-10-17 ("Yes"): ambulance — at each next stand stop with the brake (stationary > 2 s, closer than 15 NM) farther than 100 m from where the ambulance was created, it is removed and re-created 30 m to the left of the aircraft with its heading (AIRemoveObject + AICreateSimulatedObject, no route), the 10 minutes are counted anew; 100 m is a game number.
**Human check 67e5e3f (2026-10-17, LEBL, ground script + slew):** all models drive backwards, both `ASO_Firetruck02` and `Truck Fire Airport Medium` — the cause is common, not one model; per the log the heading before the route was set and read back (`hdg asked 255, sim 255`), no chatter, the ambulance re-creation worked. The vehicles stood at the start of the runway, the aircraft stopped at the end — the approach took minutes, and the 120 s limit would have stopped them halfway. Visually (reading a screenshot): the ambulance right after creation (heading = aircraft heading) faced the same way as the aircraft — "drawn backwards" is not confirmed for this model.
**User decisions (2026-10-17: "Movement log", "Along the whole runway", "Stop only when stuck", "40 kt to the circle"):** (1) diagnostics in the bridge console (a18290a onwards): the vehicle's heading right after creation (before our set), 3 s after each heading set, and every 2 s for a moving vehicle — heading, `VELOCITY BODY Z` (speed along the vehicle's axis, minus = backwards; Aircraft Misc Variables SDK 2024), actual direction of movement and distance to the target; the fix for reversing — after the log, by agreement; (a) vehicles wait spread along the whole runway length: vehicle i — at 100 + (length − 200)·i/(n − 1) m from the threshold, 45 m to the right of the centreline; one vehicle — the middle of the runway (implementation choice); a runway shorter than 300 m counts as 300 m; (b) speed to the circle — 40 kt instead of 30 (the limit speed of stock vehicles is not found in the SDK — human check); (c) instead of the 120 s limit: a vehicle on its way to the circle stops only if it has arrived (≤ 5 m), has not got closer to its point by at least 10 m in 20 s (stuck), or 5 min after the aircraft stopped.
**User decision (2026-10-17, "150 m, upwind"), replaces the lateral offset, side and spacing of item (a):** a game rule, not a regulation — per a description of ARFF practice (a search summary, not a primary source); the width of the runway strip per ICAO Annex 14 was not checked. The vehicles wait 150 m from the runway centreline (was 45 m), on the upwind side — by the wind direction from the landing airport's METAR relative to the landing heading (the wind comes from the core in the `rescue` command, `windFromDeg`; the app passes the bridge `side: left | right`, function `upwindSide` in `packages/core/src/sim/rescue.ts`); calm, VRB, no METAR or wind exactly along the runway — right in the landing direction. Along the runway — from the touchdown zone, 400 m from the threshold, to (length − 200 m), vehicles spread evenly; one vehicle — at 400 m; on a runway shorter than 600 m all at 400 m. The vehicles face the runway. The rest of item (a), (b) 40 kt and (c) "stop only when stuck" — unchanged.
**Human check b4a213c (2026-10-17, diagnostics):** the heading at creation is applied (`created: sim hdg 157 (INITPOSITION 157)`); for all moving vehicles `body z` > 0, the actual path ≈ heading (0–15°) — the sim drives the vehicles forwards along their PLANE HEADING. Nevertheless all fire-truck models visually drive backwards and right after creation face nose the opposite way; the ambulance (`Truck Utility Europe Medic Box`) is drawn correctly. Conclusion (an observation, not documentation — not described in SDK 2024 or DevSupport): for fire-truck models the visual nose ≈ PLANE HEADING + 180°.
**User decisions (2026-10-17, "(a) All fire trucks", "(2) The bridge moves them itself"):** (a) for all fire-truck slots 0–7 heading + 180° wherever we set it (creation and movement), ambulance — no correction; check a new fire-truck model by a human: where the nose points right after spawning. (2) Fire trucks no longer drive along `AI WAYPOINT LIST`: all vehicles stand with `SIM DISABLED = 1`, and the bridge itself moves the fire trucks about 10 times a second — position and heading (+ 180°) through `SIMCONNECT_DATA_INITPOSITION` ("Initial Position", `SIMCONNECT_DATATYPE_INITPOSITION`; SDK: "can be used to incrementally move or reposition … any other aircraft not controlled by the user", checked 2026-10-17), OnGround = 1. The route, speeds (following — aircraft ground speed + 10 kt, 20–60 kt; to the circle — 40 kt), the 80 m detour, waiting for the aircraft to pass, "stop when stuck / 5 min" — as before, now executed by the bridge; the turn before the route and the workaround for "crawling at the last point" are no longer needed. Console diagnostics: heading at creation, every 2 s — where the vehicle is at the bridge (`moving: hdg …, kt, to target …`) and in the sim (`in the sim: hdg …, body z …, N m from where the bridge put it`).
**Human check 6fa8e6a (2026-10-17, log 17:07–17:14):** with the + 180° turn, the yellow `ASO_Firetruck02` (slots 0, 2) looked backwards for all, and the light green `Truck Fire Airport Medium` — correct. So the turn is needed per model: `ASO_Firetruck02` is drawn along PLANE HEADING, `Truck Fire Airport Medium` — backwards (+ 180°), `Microsoft_Truck_Fire_Medium_Red` per the AI runs — also backwards. The reliable point is this run; the earlier words "both new models drive backwards" referred only to Airport Medium. All four vehicles reached the circle; the sim holds the position exactly (`0.0 m from where the bridge put it`, body z 0.0), but the movement looks like a slideshow: the step ran on the bridge timer, in reality 100–200 ms (5–10 Hz).
**User decisions (2026-10-17, "Yes, (b)", "(A) Every frame"), replace item (a):** (b) the setting "Reversed models" (an editable list of titles), by default `Truck Fire Airport Medium` and `Microsoft_Truck_Fire_Medium_Red` (`DEFAULT_RESCUE_FLIPPED`); the app passes the bridge the argument `fireFlip` — `0`/`1` for each fire truck joined by `|`, the same length as `fire` (empty if there are no fire trucks); the core and the bridge validate it identically; + 180° — only for the marked vehicles; `ASO_Firetruck02` and the ambulance — no correction. A new model is checked by a human: where the nose points right after spawning; if it stands backwards — add it to the list. (A) the movement step — on every rendered frame via the system event "Frame" (SubscribeToSystemEvent, SDK 2024: "Request notifications every visual frame", in the managed wrapper — `OnRecvEventFrame`), and the 100 ms timer — only when there are no frames (pause, menu); the rest as before; the `position sets` counter stays. (C) a hybrid with AI WAYPOINT LIST — only if after (A) there are still jerks or FPS drops; the user decides.

#### T55 — Current airline routes instead of OpenFlights 2014 **R**
User request 2026-10-16 (audit, idea C13). Airline routes are taken only from OpenFlights `routes.dat` of 2014 (T43, T53; `packages/core/src/ext/airline.ts`): "does the company fly from the destination airport" (`airlineDepartsFrom` → `onwardRoute` in `irregular-data.ts`) and "where is the next flight from the hub" (`hubRoutesFor` in `ops-data.ts`). The data is outdated: there are no routes opened after 2014 and no types A20N/B38M; there are closed ones.

**Source analysis (2026-10-16).**
- **AeroDataBox** (the same user key as in T30; OpenAPI `doc.aerodatabox.com/docs/openapi-rapidapi-v1.json`, checked 2026-10-16):
  - FIDS `GET /flights/airports/icao/{ICAO}/{fromLocal}/{toLocal}` — already requested in T30 (Tier 2, 2 units). In the response a flight has `aircraft: {reg, modeS, model}` — `model` as free text ("Aircraft name and model"). Currently `parseConnex` (`packages/data-sources/src/aerodatabox.ts`) does not read this field. Whether `model` is filled for scheduled flights (without ADS-B) and with `withLeg=false` — `[VERIFY]` on a live response.
  - `GET /airports/{codeType}/{code}/stats/routes/daily` (variant with `/{dateLocal}`) — Tier 3, **6 units** (`aerodatabox.com/pricing`, checked 2026-10-16). Response `DailyRouteStatContract`: `routes[]` → `destination` (airport), `averageDailyFlights`, `operators[]` (`name`, `iata`, `icao`). No aircraft type. Per the API description it is available only for airports with ICAO and IATA codes and with a schedule. With ADS-B coverage the data is from actual flights, otherwise from the schedule.
  - Basic plan: 400 units/month, 1 request/s. Terms — as in T30 (non-commercial, attribution, storage ≤ 7 days, identifiers — indefinitely, clause 5.8).
- **Rejected:**
  - **VRS standing-data** (`github.com/vradarserver/standing-data`, CC0 1.0, updated daily, 620 thousand callsigns, 1516 companies). The database is cumulative, without the date of the last flight: the 2026-10-16 snapshot has `AFL2042 UUEE-LFPG` and `DLH1440 EDDF-UUDD` (closed since 2022) and crowdsourcing errors (`AFR1 KJFK-EPWA`). It gives no currency, only volume (AFR has 300 departure airports against 170 in OpenFlights).
  - **OpenSky Network** `/flights/departure` (OAuth2, 4000 credits/day for a registered user, non-commercial only): actual flights, but with a nightly-processing delay. Airports are estimated. Needs one more user account.
  - **adsbdb**: the route data licence forbids copying and inclusion in other databases.
  - **Paid** (Cirium, OAG, FlightAware AeroAPI, Aviation Edge): contradict the stage 0 decision (`08`, item 4).

**Proposal (three levels, from exact to fallback)**
1. **Departures of the destination airport from T30** (no new requests). `parseConnex` additionally saves `aircraft.model` for flights of its own airline. Own flights — operating (`IsOperator`), passenger (`isCargo = false`).
   - T43: at least one own departure → `onwardRoute` = yes.
   - T53: the next flight — an own departure no earlier than STA + the T43 turnaround time (LCC 45–55, others 50–70 min), of the same aircraft family (`aircraft.family`, T40), not to the airport the aircraft came from. Seeded choice among suitable ones. Number, direction and STD — real, instead of the seeded number of T53 item 4. Works after a flight to any airport where the company has departures in the window, not only to the hub.
2. **Route statistics** `stats/routes/daily` of the destination airport — if level 1 has no suitable flight (night, type not mapped, few flights). Candidates — destinations where `operators` includes the own company. Seeded choice, weight — `averageDailyFlights`. Number — as in T53 item 4. Request — at flight start together with T30, only if the cache has no entry younger than 7 days. Cache — a file in `<userData>`, per airport, with the receipt date. Entries older than 7 days are deleted at startup (clause 5.5 of the terms).
3. **OpenFlights 2014** — as now: no key, 401/429, error, 204, no candidates.

- The core receives data only via `CoreInput.data` (written to the flight recording). Re-running a recording is deterministic; purging data older than 7 days — as in T30 (C9).
- Core option `nextLegVersion: 3` (default in the app); recordings with 1 and 2 are replayed as before.
- The table "`aircraft.model` text → T40 family" — in `packages/core/src/ext/`, under tests on real strings from AeroDataBox responses.
- Cost: 2 units per flight (already in T30) + 6 per airport no more than once per 7 days. At 30 flights a month it is enough for about 55 different destination airports.

**Assumptions (approved by the user 2026-10-16):**
- **R1.** The next flight is a real departure of the company with the same aircraft family, not the same aircraft: AeroDataBox has no aircraft rotations. The pilot will not notice; number, direction and time are real.
- **R2.** Mapping the free text `aircraft.model` ("Airbus A320 NEO", "Boeing 737-800") to a T40 family — by a table compiled from real API responses. Unrecognised text → the flight does not fit by type (level 2).
- **R3.** Codeshare and cargo flights are excluded. How `airline`/`codeshareStatus` are filled for codeshare flights is still `[VERIFY]` (T30 C6).
- **R4.** At level 2 the aircraft type is unknown: the next flight is chosen without a type check, weighted by `averageDailyFlights`; number — seeded, without STD.
- **R5.** A disk cache of route statistics for up to 7 days — a change to the T30 C9 decision ("no disk cache"). Only the airport code, company codes and `averageDailyFlights` are stored, the retention is within clause 5.5 of the terms.
- **R6.** The hub (T53 item 1) stays from OpenFlights and the "Home hub" setting. Level 1 does not depend on the hub; level 2 neither.

**User decisions (2026-10-21):** (a) STD at level 2 — computed, as in T53 (STA + turnaround, rounded to 5 min): `next_leg`, `aircraft_change`, `welcome_turnaround` print it, quick turn and `ops.turn_avail_min` depend on it; "without STD" in R4 and in the human check means "without a real STD". (b) Fixtures from live responses are trimmed to identifiers (terms of clauses 5.5/5.8): structure, numbers, airport and company codes, `codeshareStatus`, `model` strings — live; times and `averageDailyFlights` — synthetic; `reg`, `modeS`, gates, terminals removed.
**Check against a live response (2026-10-21, EDDF, the user's key):** `aircraft.model` is filled for all 615 departures (all `quality: Basic, Live`; window −1 h…+11 h, "schedule-only" flights without ADS-B cannot be told apart in this response — for them `[VERIFY]` remains). Codeshare — a separate departure `IsCodeshared` with the marketing company in `airline` (closes T30 C6 for FIDS). 27 different `model` strings, all in the table `ext/aircraft-models.ts`; `Airbus A220-100/-300` and `Regional-Jet` — without a T40 family.
**Done (2026-10-21):** the level-1 next-flight number — ICAO code of the company + digits of the real number (`LH 958` → `DLH958`), like the T53 numbers; the line `SRC AERODATABOX` (`ops.next_src`, C4) — the last line of the three next-flight scenarios when the flight is taken from AeroDataBox data.
**Done when:** `parseConnex` tests with `aircraft.model` (trimmed real fixture); the `model` → family table under tests; next-flight choice by levels 1 → 2 → 3 is deterministic by seed, takes into account the turnaround and the family, excludes the arrival airport; `onwardRoute` from live data; cache: hit, miss, deletion older than 7 days, 429 → level 3; `nextLegVersion` 1/2 replayed as before; `pnpm check` green.
**Human check:** the app with an AeroDataBox key; SimBrief OFP DLH A320 EDDM–EDDF in the daytime → `NEXT LEG`/`WELCOME` show a real DLH flight from EDDF of the A320 family (compare number and time with the EDDF board), not `EDDF-EDDM`; the same flight arriving at night → direction from the route statistics, without STD; without a key → as T53. Record: whether `aircraft.model` is filled for scheduled flights and what codeshare flights look like.
## Stage 7 — Interface

#### T-UI — New visual style of the app

**Goal:** restyle `apps/desktop` (renderer) to the mockup in `design/` without changing the logic or the main ↔ renderer contract.

**Includes:**
- Tokens from `design/tokens.css` in `styles.css` (replace variables per the table in `design/README.md`).
- FMS screen for telex: `design/fms.css`, B612 Mono font bundled locally, width = `FlightSummary.lineWidth`.
- `App.tsx` (top bar, tabs, error banner), `FlightView.tsx`, `FeedList.tsx`, `ScenariosView.tsx`, `SettingsView.tsx` — markup and classes per the `design/spec.md` specification.
- Switches instead of checkboxes (with `role="switch"` / a native checkbox with styling).
- New labels — `i18n.ts` keys (EN + RU).

**User decision (2026-10-15):** the main screen layout follows the `ScreenMain` mockup (PDF "ACARS for MSFS 2024"): top bar with UTC and SIM/NET/CPDLC, side rail, message table with filters and search, on the right "Flight" and the FMS screen with the selected message and scratchpad, bottom status bar, the "Request" window. Filled only with data from `AppState`: flight parameters — ALT/VS/GS/FOB from telemetry (no CLR, ETA, ATD — STD/STA from the OFP instead); filters — All/Airline/Pilot/System (there are no ATC/Weather/NOTAM sections in the data); "Request" assembles the pilot commands the core already understands (`REQ WX`, `REQ ATIS`, `REQ GATE`, `REQ LOADSHEET`, free text) and sends them via `sendPilotMessage`; no position marker on the route line.
Mockup elements without data (CLR, ETA/ATD, WILCO/UNABLE/STANDBY, ATC/Weather/NOTAM/Info & Wx sections, a separate FMS window, position marker, theme switch, "Log"/"Live" buttons) are not implemented — user decision 2026-10-15.
**Excludes:** `store.ts`, `shared/api.ts`, `preload`, `main`, `packages/*`; a second FMS window; CPDLC reply buttons; METAR/PDC requests from the app; data that is not in `AppState`.

**Done when:** `pnpm check` is green; all button/field/row states from `spec.md` §4 are visible; a 1180×820 window has no horizontal scroll; focus is visible on all interactive elements.

**Human check:**
1. `pnpm desktop:dev`, "Flight" tab without an OFP → empty state, the "Load" button is blue.
2. Load an OFP → flight header: callsign, ORIG — DEST, type, schedule, profile.
3. Start a flight with MSFS → bridge/simulator/network statuses are colored by tone, the phase is highlighted in blue.
4. An incoming telex → a feed row with delivery status; text is green on black, width per profile (A320: 24, B777: 27).
5. Switch language EN/RU → nothing is truncated.
6. Tab through all screens → a visible focus ring.

#### T56 — Usability: flight lifecycle, feedback, settings and scenarios

User request 2026-10-21 following the UX audit of `apps/desktop` (user modeling from the code and the running app with a clean profile; no testing with people). The card covers audit items U-03…U-16, U-19, U-20; the numbers are kept for cross-references. U-01, U-02 (readiness checklist, bridge status; done in T70), U-17, U-18, U-21 — excluded.

**Includes:**
- **U-03 (P1). Closing the window during a flight.** Currently `window-all-closed` → `quit` without a question, the session is only in memory, after a restart the flight cannot be continued. Do: a close confirmation while a flight is in progress (`main/index.ts`).
- **U-04 (P1). Ending the flight.** After "Turnaround"/"Finish" the session stays `running`, and then there is only a red "Stop" with the warning "unsent messages will be discarded" — yet after "Turnaround" the queue holds exactly the messages about the next flight (default interval 60 s). The "Turnaround" button has the same name as the setting "I am flying a turnaround". Do: after `DONE` — a neutral "Close flight"; after `TURNAROUND` — "Load next-leg OFP", which closes the flight only after the queue has been sent (or says explicitly how many messages are still waiting); separate the names of the button and the setting.
- **U-05 (P1). Pilot requests.** "New message", "Request" and the `ENTER MESSAGE` line imitate a telex from the aircraft, nothing goes to the network, but the labels "New message", "Will be sent", "Send" say the opposite; the explanation is only in a tooltip and small print. Do: a single "Airline request…" button, in the preview — "The airline receives it as a message from the aircraft", button "Send to airline", the explanation as visible text.
- **U-06 (P2). Credentials and network failures.** The Hoppie code is checked only by format — a wrong code is found out in flight; the reason "Hoppie: stopped" is visible only in the status tooltip and as a line in the "System" feed; there are no "where to get it" links for Hoppie and SimBrief (AeroDataBox has one). Do: links to hoppie.nl and SimBrief; a banner with the reason and an action when the transport stops; a "Check" button for SimBrief and Hoppie (the Hoppie request must respect invariant 6, one request per press; which Hoppie request is suitable for checking the code — `[VERIFY]` against `06-integrations.md`).
- **U-07 (P2). Settings structure.** One page of ~3000 px, 9 groups, the basics (SimBrief, transport, language) next to the expert ones (ICAO hex, AES/GES, rescue vehicle models, callsign template, port); hints up to 6 lines long. Do: "Main" and collapsed "Advanced"; a short hint + "More".
- **U-08 (P2). A single save model.** Currently three: switches — immediately; hub, ICAO hex, vehicle lists — on focus loss with no indication; "Connection" — by a button. The "✓ Saved" mark for all instant settings is drawn at the "Connection" block at the bottom of the page (`Saved what="settings"`). Do: save immediately, a "Saved" mark on the changed row; port and template — also immediately, with value validation.
- **U-09 (P2). "From the next flight".** Each hint words it differently, plus a general banner between groups; language and sound apply immediately. Do: a short "next flight" label on the row, highlighted during a flight.
- **U-10 (P2). The "Scenarios" screen.** 91 items, ~8700 px, no search or group actions, categories do not collapse, the order follows the English category ids; the service entries "Pilot command: REQ LOADSHEET / DIVERT / MEDICAL" are in the general list — switching them off silently breaks requests. Do: search, a category switch, collapsing, a meaningful category order; mark the pilot commands "needed for requests" or move them to a separate group.
- **U-11 (P2). Probability field and the drone table.** A gray placeholder with a number looks like an inactive value; empty = default; an invalid entry silently reverts. Do: an explicit "default N%", a message on invalid input.
- **U-12 (P2). Scrolling between tabs.** `.content` is shared: "Settings" at 2000 px → "Scenarios" opens from the middle (verified). Do: each tab has its own scroll (reset or remembered).
- **U-13 (P2). Inactive rows.** `Row` has no dimming (only `Switch` does): the ICAO address with the data link switched off and the vehicle lists look active. Do: dim the whole row + a hint what to enable.
- **U-14 (P2). Errors in mixed languages.** `AppFailure` details come from main in English: "Not possible now: stop the flight first". Do: error details as codes translated in `i18n.ts`.
- **U-15 (P2). Important items in the feed.** Hints that require action (`flt_nbr_hint`, `atc_needs_data`, `link_needs_data`, `telemetry_lost`, transport failure) look like an ordinary "Info" row among "Phase: …" and OOOI. Do: an "action required" level — highlighted in the feed and in the status line.
- **U-16 (P2). "Time from the simulator" without the bridge.** The flight is "started", the core waits for the first `simUtcMs`, and nothing in the interface shows it. Do: a "Waiting for sim time" state in the "Flight" card.
- **U-19 (P3). Duplicate statuses.** SIM/NET in the top bar repeat the bottom line; "SIM —" does not explain the dash. Do: replace the dash with a word (e.g. "no bridge"); decide whether the duplicate is needed.
- **U-20 (P3). Irreversible actions.** "Clear" for the airline memory and "Delete" for the Hoppie code / AeroDataBox key act immediately. Do: a confirmation.

**Affects:** renderer (`App.tsx`, `FlightView.tsx`, `FeedList.tsx`, `RequestDialog.tsx`, `ScenariosView.tsx`, `SettingsView.tsx`, `Switch.tsx`, `status.ts`, `i18n.ts`, `styles.css`), as well as `main/index.ts` (U-03), `main/controller.ts` / `main/errors.ts` / `shared/api.ts` (U-04, U-06, U-14, U-16) and possibly `main/session.ts` (U-04: waiting for the queue). New labels — EN + RU.
**Excludes:** continuing a flight after a restart (U-03, high cost — a separate card); the readiness checklist and bridge launch (U-01, U-02); a visual redesign; changes to `packages/core` (if U-04 or U-16 need them — agree first).

**User decisions (2026-10-22):**
1. U-04: wait for the queue to be sent and close the flight automatically; the "Close now" button — with a confirmation that N messages will be discarded.
2. U-10: pilot commands — a separate group at the end of the list labeled "needed for requests", cannot be switched off (the category switch does not touch them).
3. U-19: keep SIM/NET in the top bar, replace the dash with a word.
4. U-06: check the code with a single `ping` on press. tech.html: "a single ping" — a connectivity check; the response to a wrong code is not described in the documentation — verified 2026-10-22 with one request using a deliberately wrong code: `error {invalid logon code}` (`06`).

**Done when:** `pnpm check` is green; controller tests for U-04 (closing after `DONE` and `TURNAROUND`, next-flight messages are not lost) and for the U-14 error codes; all new strings exist in EN and RU; a 1180×820 window and the 860 px minimum — no horizontal scroll; focus is visible on the new elements.

**Human check:**
1. Clean profile: Settings → the "where to get it" links for SimBrief and Hoppie open the browser; a wrong Hoppie code → a clear error before the start (if question 4 is resolved "yes") or at the start.
2. A flight in offline mode to the stand (MSFS, any airliner) → "Turnaround" → the next-flight messages reach the FMS; the "Load next-leg OFP" button does not lose them. Repeat with "Finish" → "Close flight" without the red warning.
3. During a flight close the window → a close question; "Cancel" — the flight continues.
4. "Airline request" → destination METAR → the reply arrives in the FMS; the labels do not promise sending "to the network".
5. Settings: toggle the sound at the top of the page → "Saved" is visible on that row; with the data link switched off → the ICAO address field is dimmed.
6. Scenarios: find "drone" by search; switch off the "External events" category with one switch; go from scrolled Settings to Scenarios → the list starts from the top.
7. Enable "Sim time", start a flight without the bridge → "Waiting for sim time" is visible.
8. Language RU: trigger the "flight already in progress" / wrong code error → the text is fully in Russian.

**Done (2026-10-22):**
- U-03: `main/close-dialog.ts` (EN/RU, default "Continue flight"), the question in `index.ts` by `AppController.closeWouldLose` — a flight is in progress and it is not a closed flight with nothing pending.
- U-04: `AppState.ending` (`messages` — interval, data link, network queue; `scenarios` — scenarios that will continue within 30 min of flight time, `CLOSE_WAIT_MS` in `session.ts`: the longest reply wait in the scenarios is 20 min; "watchdogs" like `fuel_monitoring` with `timeout_min: 720` and waiting for the flight phase do not hold it). API `closeFlight(next|close, now)`; the main loop closes the flight when nothing is pending, then with `next` loads the OFP; a load error is the feed line `next_ofp_failed`. Buttons: "Fly next leg" / "No more flights" (the setting is renamed "Turnaround and next-leg messages"), then "Load next-leg OFP" / "Close flight", "Close now". The red "Stop" is not shown after the decision at the gate.
- U-05: a single "Airline request…" button, the explanation as visible text, "The airline receives it as a message from the aircraft", "Send to airline".
- U-06: links dispatch.simbrief.com and hoppie.nl (registration page), "Check": SimBrief — the latest OFP without loading; Hoppie — a single `ping` (`from` `ACARSSIM`, not during a flight, no more than once per 15 s), a wrong code → `hoppie_logon`. The banner "Hoppie stopped sending" with the reason and an "Open Settings" button.
- U-07–U-09, U-13: "Advanced settings" collapsed (sim time, hub, data link, rescue services, recordings, connection); the hint is the first sentence + "More"; everything saves immediately with "Saved" on the row, template and port — on Enter/leaving the field with validation; a "next flight" label (highlighted during a flight) instead of phrases in the hints; inactive rows dimmed with a hint what to enable.
- U-10, U-11: search, collapsing, category switch (`setScenariosEnabled`), order docs → ops → irregular → external → fun → commands; `ScenarioInfo.command`. Probability: "default N%" is always visible, "Default" resets, an invalid entry stays with a message; the same for the drone table.
- U-12: each tab has its own scroll (`App.tsx`). U-14: `AppError.reason` + `params`, `ERROR_REASONS` in `shared/api.ts`, `why.*` texts in `i18n.ts`. U-15: `renderer/src/actions.ts` — open "action required" lines in the feed and in the status line until resolved. U-16: `AppState.waitingSimTime`. U-19: "no bridge", "no flight". U-20: confirmations for deleting the code/key and clearing the memory.
- Tests: `apps/desktop/test/usability.test.ts` (12). The 1180×820 and 860 px layouts were viewed on the renderer with a substituted `window.acars` (Vite without Electron) — no horizontal scroll; not verified in Electron — items 1–8 above.

#### T57 — Message hints: what it means and what to reply

User request 2026-10-21: in the app — hints for a specific message: what it means and what can be replied; the feature can be switched off. The card is a design (questions resolved 2026-10-22, see "User decisions"); the implementation — in a separate session.

**Goal:** a pilot who is not familiar with the airline's language (MEL, NOTOC, CTOT, holdover…) understands the message received and knows which replies the core will accept and what happens without a reply.

**What already exists (the basis):**
- The `outbound` `FeedEntry` carries `scenarioId` (`shared/api.ts`) — the scenario hint attaches to it.
- The core knows the expected reply: `run.reply = { options, default, untilFlightMs }` (`core/src/scenario/engine.ts`), the `REPLY_EXPECTED` event (`05`, "Execution"), but it does not reach `AppState`.
- T49 hints (`shared/atc-hints.ts`, system lines `atc_misrouted_*`, `flt_nbr_hint`) — a model of "a hint only in the app, nothing to the network".
- The pilot command list — `commands/cmd_help.yaml`, the `description`/`description_ru` fields of every scenario (for the "Scenarios" screen).

**Includes (hint layers, shown together):**
1. **What the message means (per scenario).** New optional scenario fields `hint` / `hint_ru` — 1–3 sentences in plain language: who sent it (MAINT CONTROL = maintenance…), why, what is expected from the pilot. They differ from `description`: that one is written for the "Scenarios" screen (when and with what probability it triggers) and reveals the outcomes — the hint must not give away what happens after the reply beyond what the message says. If one scenario has several `send` steps with different meanings, an optional `hint`/`hint_ru` on the `send` step overrides the scenario one; for this the outgoing message carries a reference to the step (see "Affects").
2. **What can be replied (per scenario).** The field `reply_hints` / `reply_hints_ru` on a `wait: reply` step: `{ OPTION: "what this reply means" }` — one line for each option in `options`. Validation on load (`scenarios/src/schema.ts` + a test): the `reply_hints` keys = `options`, both languages.
3. **A reply is awaited now (live state).** `AppState.pendingReplies: { scenarioId, messageId, options, default, minutesLeft }[]` from the core state (`run.reply`; minutes are flight minutes, like `timeout_min`). Under the message: "Awaiting reply: CONFIRM / NEGATIVE · telex to {station} as the first word · without a reply in 12 min it will be NEGATIVE". After a reply or a timeout the line disappears. The reply options are only shown as text — no buttons, no insertion into the scratchpad and no sending; the pilot types the reply themselves (from the aircraft FMS or in `ENTER MESSAGE`).
4. **Pilot commands.** The hint for replies to commands (`cmd_*`) — from the command scenario's `hint`; in the "Request" window each command has one line saying what it does (from the same `hint`).
5. **System lines and ATC.** `system` lines (DCL/OCL/CPDLC, `REPLY_TIMEOUT`, `flt_nbr_hint`) already explain themselves; for DCL/OCL clearances — a hint on how to confirm (only from facts already recorded in T24/T27 and `06`).

**Where it is shown:** a "Hint" block under the FMS screen of the selected message (not inside the FMS screen — it stays as in the cockpit); in the feed, on a message that awaits a reply — an "awaiting reply" label (overlaps with U-15 of T56 — agree the style). The interface font and colors, not the FMS ones.

**Switching off:** the setting "Message hints" (in the "Main" group per U-07), applies immediately, without "next flight". When off — the hint block and the "awaiting reply" labels are not rendered; the core and the flight recording do not change (`pendingReplies` in `AppState` remains). **On by default** (decision 1).

**Invariants:** hints only in the app — nothing goes to the network or the FMS (the formatter, invariant 9, is not affected); the texts are static, without an LLM (invariant 8) and without numbers that are not in the message itself; the core only exposes the waiting state (deterministically, replay does not change); the renderer does not import `@acars/scenarios` — the hint texts come from main (like `title` for the "Scenarios" screen).

**Realism rule (texts), decision 3:** a hint is an explanation of the message in our own words (who sent it, what it is about, what is expected from the pilot) and what can be replied; we put no source links in the hints or in the YAML. There are no normative figures (MEL deadlines, limits, categories) in the hint that are not in the message itself — only a retelling of what is in the message and in the scenario logic.

**Affects:** `packages/scenarios` (schema, 91 YAMLs: `hint` on all, `reply_hints` on 20 scenarios with `wait: reply` — `commands/{cmd_divert,cmd_medical}`, `irregular/*` per the `grep reply:` list), `packages/core` (export of pending replies from the engine state; for a step's `hint` — a step identifier in the send command → `FeedEntry`), `apps/desktop` (`shared/api.ts`: `pendingReplies`, `hint` in the message data, the setting; `main/controller.ts`; renderer: `FlightView.tsx` / `FmsPanel`, `FeedList.tsx`, `RequestDialog.tsx`, `SettingsView.tsx`, `i18n.ts`, `styles.css`), `docs/05` (the `hint`, `reply_hints` fields).
**Excludes:** a glossary — highlighting terms in the message text with a pop-up explanation (a separate card: needs an abbreviation dictionary with sources); replying with a button directly to the network; LLM-generated hints; a training mode/app tour.

**User decisions (2026-10-22):**
1. Hints are **on** by default.
2. Scope — **all layers 1–5** in this card.
3. Texts — **an explanation of the message without source links, and what to reply** (see "Realism rule").
4. A hint on a `send` step — **right away**: the core's send command carries the step identifier, the `outbound` `FeedEntry` — a reference to the step hint (fallback — the scenario hint).
5. Reply options are **only shown** as text, without buttons or insertion.

**Done when:** `pnpm check` is green; a schema test: every scenario has `hint`+`hint_ru`, every `wait: reply` has `reply_hints` for all `options` in both languages; a core test: a pending reply appears after `send`, disappears after a reply and after a timeout, the state serializes; a message from a step with `hint` gets the step hint, without it — the scenario one; a controller test: `pendingReplies` in `AppState`, with the setting off → no hints in the UI (a renderer or controller test — depending on where the decision is made); replay of pre-T57 recordings gives the same messages; a 1180×820 window without horizontal scroll, all strings EN + RU.

**Done (2026-10-22):** schema and compilation — `hint`/`hint_ru` on the scenario and on the `send` step, `reply_hints`/`reply_hints_ru` on `wait: reply` (keys = `options`, a Russian one without an English one is an error; `compile.ts`); core — `OutboundMessage.step` (the `send` step), `run.reply.step`, `AcarsCore.pendingReplies` (with the remaining flight time; old states are read without a step); texts — on all 91 scenarios, `reply_hints` on 20 scenarios (23 waits: the question repeats in the wait loop), step hints where the messages differ in meaning; app — `FeedEntry.hint` (step → scenario; XATC DCL/PDC/CLX clearances — `main/hints.ts`), `AppState.pendingReplies`, `ScenarioInfo.hint`, the `messageHints` setting (on by default, "General", immediate), the "Hint" block under the FMS, the "Awaiting reply" label in the feed (before the subject; the U-15 style from T56 is not done yet), the first sentence of the command hint in the "Request" window. The flight recording does not change. The 1180×820 window was not checked by eye — see the human check, item 6.

**Human check:**
1. `pnpm desktop:dev`, a flight in offline mode, "Scenarios" → "run now" `tech_defect_mel` → select the MAINT CONTROL message → under the FMS screen: what it is, the options CONFIRM / NEGATIVE with explanations, "without a reply in 15 min it will be NEGATIVE".
2. Reply from the aircraft FMS (MSFS, Fenix A320 or any airliner with Hoppie telex) `CONFIRM` → the "awaiting reply" line disappears, the CONFIRM branch message arrived.
3. A message with two different `send` steps in one scenario (e.g. the CONFIRM branch reply of `tech_defect_mel`) → the second message has its own hint, not a repeat of the first.
4. Switch off "Message hints" during a flight → the block and labels disappear at once; switch on → they return.
5. Language RU → hints in Russian, the message texts in the FMS unchanged (Latin, profile width).
6. 1180×820 window: select a message with a long hint and a pending reply (`dest_fire` or `unruly_passenger`) — the right column scrolls, no horizontal scroll; in the feed the "Awaiting reply" label is visible before the subject. The "Request" window (METAR, ATIS, Gate, Loadsheet, Text) — one line under the switch saying what the command does.

#### T58 — Renaming the ATC station ROPS → XATC

**User decision (2026-10-21/22).** In aviation ROPS is the Airbus Runway Overrun Prevention System (present on the A320/A350, including the FBW A32NX): a pilot will read it as an onboard system. The new station name is **XATC**: ICAO location codes starting with `X` are not issued (which is why the station callsigns are `X`+FIR, `X`+airport), so it will not coincide with an airport or a live Hoppie station. Rejected: SATC — the ICAO code of Clorinda airport (Argentina). Registration of "XATC" as a trademark was not checked.

**Includes:** all mentions in the interface (`i18n.ts` EN/RU, settings, status, feed), scenario descriptions and comments, documents and `CLAUDE.md`; code identifiers (`atc/rops.ts` → `atc/xatc.ts`, `RopsStation` → `XatcStation`, the core option and recording header field `rops` → `xatc`, settings `rops*` → `xatc*`, the `scenarioId` of station messages `#rops` → `#xatc`).
**Compatibility (user decision: "everything + reading the old"):** old keys are read: the settings `rops`, `ropsStandAside`, `ropsRandomDirects` from `settings.json`; `rops` in the flight recording header; `rops` in `CoreState`. The new key, if present, takes precedence over the old one. Only the new ones are written.
**Excludes:** the names of the old branches `task/T23-rops-station` etc. Messages to the cockpit do not contain the station name — behavior in the simulator does not change, replay gives the same texts. The station callsigns `X`+FIR / `X`+airport are unchanged.

**Done when:** `pnpm check` is green; tests: settings with old keys → new values; a recording with `rops` in the header is read and gives the same messages; a `CoreState` with `rops` restores the station; `grep -i rops` is empty (except the compatibility code and its tests).

**Human check:** 1) `pnpm desktop:dev` with an old `settings.json` where `"rops": true` is enabled → in settings "XATC station", "Random Direct To" and "Yield to live VATSIM ATC" are on — as they were. 2) A flight with a logon to the station: feed lines and status start with "XATC:", "ROPS" appears nowhere. 3) Language RU and EN — the same.

#### T60 — BlueBird 757 (MSFS): display profile, the B757 family and CPDLC verification
User request 2026-10-25. The BlueBird Simulations 757 was released on 24 Oct 2026: only the 757-200 passenger variant (RR RB211-535E / PW2000, standard or four doors, with and without winglets), for MSFS 2020, in MSFS 2024 — a compatible, not native version; the 757-300 is promised later natively for MSFS 2024, the 757F — as a paid add-on. The FMC is Pegasus; the CDU width, pages and telex display are not described anywhere.
Hoppie in the aircraft (verified 2026-10-25): BlueBird's official feature list (bluebirdsim.com/news/757-feature-list-breakdown, 19 Jul 2026) — "Hoppie ACARS integration supporting DCL and Air-to-Air messaging", not a word about CPDLC; the release announcement (msfsaddons.com, 24 Oct 2026) — "Hoppie ACARS, SayIntentions.AI CPDLC"; the review (msfsaddons.com, 22 Oct 2026) — "CPDLC currently only works with Hoppie … and SayIntentions.AI", the reviewer did not test CPDLC (flies with BeyondATC). Result: CPDLC via Hoppie is **not confirmed** `[VERIFY]` by a human. There was no GSX profile at release (review) — the T22 effects may not work.

The XATC station (T23–T27) speaks the common Hoppie `/data2/…` format and does not depend on the aircraft — no separate 757 code is needed until the check shows differences.
1. Built-in profile `b75x`: ICAO `B752`, `B753` (ICAO Doc 8643), title `\b(B?757)`. Width — base (24), characters — default.
2. T40 family `B757` in `packages/core/src/scenario/aircraft.ts`: codes `B752 B753`; `BOEING`, `NARROW`, `EICAS`. `{seat}` seats — `A–F` (like other narrow-bodies). `{hold}` compartments — only `FWD HOLD` / `AFT HOLD`, without `BULK` and without ULD (a `holdPosition` fix); for the 737 and other narrow-bodies unchanged.
3. Human check (below); the results go to `06-integrations.md`, like Fenix, iniBuilds and PMDG 737. From them — the width and characters of `b75x`, and differences in CPDLC/DCL — a separate station change in this same card or a new one. The aircraft goes into the `08` list, "Stage 0 decisions", item 8, with the CPDLC result.

**Assumptions (approved by the user 2026-10-25):**
1. Profile `b75x` is selected by ICAO `B752`/`B753` and by the title `\b(B?757)`; the exact BlueBird TITLE is unknown — check item 6.
2. `b75x` width = 24, characters — `DEFAULT_ALLOWED_CHARS` (there is no source for the Pegasus 757 CDU width), until the human check.
3. Codes of the `B757` family: `B752 B753`.
4. `BOEING`, `NARROW`, seats `A–F` (3-3 layout — common knowledge); `EICAS` — common knowledge (757/767 were the first with EICAS), a dated primary source not found `[VERIFY]`.
5. Compartments — only `FWD` and `AFT`: FAA AD 2024-13014 (proposal 2023-23521) names on the 757 "forward and aft lower lobe cargo compartments"; the Boeing 757 Airport Planning document could not be read.
6. The AeroDataBox → family table (T55, `aircraft-models.ts`) does not change: there are no 757 rows in the live response (EDDF, 2026-10-21); the next leg for the 757 is a fallback until rows appear.
7. If the aircraft has no CPDLC via Hoppie — as with the iFly 737 (decision 2026-10-10, T49 item 4; `08`, item 8): we do not touch the XATC station, in `08` — "no CPDLC". If the bridge SimVars (parking brake, doors, engines) do not match the cockpit — a separate card for the bridge, modeled on Fenix (LVar).

**Excludes:** LVars and bridge changes; SayIntentions; GSX; the T55 table; scenarios branching on `aircraft.family` (there are no new branches for `B757` — `tech_defect_mel` follows the common one with `EICAS`).
**Done when:** the profile is built in, selected by ICAO `B752`/`B753` and by the title `BlueBird 757-200`; `aircraftInfo('B752')` → `B757`/`BOEING`/`NARROW`/`EICAS`; `{hold}` for `B757` gives only `FWD HOLD`/`AFT HOLD`, for the other families the results on the same seeds are unchanged; `pnpm check` is green.
**Done (2026-10-25):** profile `B75X_PROFILE` (`format/profile.ts`, in `BUILTIN_PROFILES` — available in `--profile` of `hoppie:telex` and `replay`); family `B757` (`scenario/aircraft.ts`), `holdPosition` without `BULK` for the 757 (the `NO_BULK_HOLD` set), results of the other families on the same seeds were checked against the pre-T60 code (a test). Along the way, per the T54 rule "narrow-body jets — 6–7": `RESCUE_CATEGORY.B757 = '6-7'` (4 fire trucks instead of 1, as it was for an unknown type). There is no `RUNWAY_NEEDS` (T50) for the 757 — a diversion only to the OFP airports, as before; the ceiling and VMO/MMO for XATC (`atc/performance.ts`) — also none. Both — a separate change with a source (Boeing 757 ACAP, TCDS), if needed.
**Human check:** MSFS 2024 (compatible version), BlueBird 757-200, the Hoppie code entered in the aircraft settings, the Hoppie network.
1) Telex: `pnpm hoppie:telex --to <callsign> --profile b75x` → where it is shown on the CDU (ACARS → which page), the header, whether the lines fit exactly in 24 characters and the `---` lines, whether the comma is visible, how many lines per page, whether there is an indication (light, sound, EICAS message).
2) Pilot reply: free text from ACARS to the OPS station → did the `telex` arrive in the app feed.
3) PDC: a clearance request to `X`+departure airport → how the clearance is shown, the reply (WILCO/UNABLE or ACCEPT) — how it was sent (text in the feed).
4) CPDLC: is there an ATC LOGON via Hoppie (and not only SayIntentions). If yes: logon to `X`+FIR → `LOGON ACCEPTED`, CURRENT ATC; `REQUEST FL…` → `CLIMB TO`; replies WILCO / STANDBY / UNABLE — how they were sent; FIR boundary: `HANDOVER` → LOGOFF of the old station and whether it logs on to the new one by itself. If not — record where it was looked for (the ATC/ACARS CDU menu, EFB).
5) The app's "Sim" window against the cockpit: parking brake (on/off), doors (both variants: standard and four doors), engine start (RR and PW), flaps, beacon, transponder.
6) The aircraft TITLE from the bridge log (both engine variants) — for `matchTitleRegex`.
#### T61 — All pre-departure messages before pushback when starting 30 minutes or less before departure
User request 2026-10-27, after the iniBuilds A350 test (JAL523 RJTT–RJCC, recording `<userData>/recordings/20261027-083020-JAL523.jsonl`): not all messages arrived before departure.

**Analysis (`pnpm replay run`, 2026-10-27).** Start at 08:30:20, STD 0910 — 39.7 min left, less than the earliest trigger (`crew_info`, −45), so the `sched_out − N` offsets are compressed (`preScale` ≈ 0.86, `lateStartVersion: 3`). At 08:44 `slot_ctot` issued `DELAY 12 MIN` (`set: flight.delay_min`), and all `sched_*` triggers that had not yet fired shifted by 12 min: the preliminary loadsheet arrived at 09:00, the takeoff data at 09:04, the fuel slip at 09:08, and the final loadsheet was scheduled for ≈09:16. The pilot started pushback at 09:11 per the original STD (OOOI OUT 09:11:10) — the final loadsheet was not on board. The same mechanism without a slot: a synthetic flight (`replay synth --boarding 10`, `minGapMs` 60 s) — starting 10 min before STD declares a late-start delay (remainder < 20 min), and only 2 of 5 messages arrive before OUT at 07:45 (`crew_info`, `prelim_loadsheet`); starting 20 and 30 min before without a slot — all 5 arrive before OUT.

There is one cause: a delay (late start, slot) shifts the documents to a time the pilot is not obliged to wait for, and pushback does not send anything that is still outstanding.

**Goal.** For a flight started at any moment from STD − 30 min to STD (and earlier — as now): all pre-departure messages that can arrive on this flight (`time` triggers with `rel: sched_out`, `offset_min < 0` — the base `crew_info`, `slot_ctot`, `prelim_loadsheet`, `takeoff_data`, `fuel_slip`, `final_loadsheet`, and the pre-departure ones from `irregular/`) are sent before OOOI OUT, in their own order, at an interval of `minGapMs`.

**Proposed solution (`lateStartVersion: 4`, written to the recording header; versions 1–3 are replayed as before — invariant 4):**
1. A late start no longer declares a delay: for any time remaining before STD, the `sched_out − N` offsets are compressed to fit the window `[start, STD − margin]` rather than shifted past STD. The late-start delay (code `63`, T43) and `ofp.est_*` stay as they are, but only for estimated times, not for the document schedule.
2. The window accounts for the send interval: if there are more documents than fit at the `minGapMs` step, they go at that step from the start.
3. A slot (`set: flight.delay_min` from `slot_ctot`) does not shift documents that have not yet arrived: CTOT, `est_*`, delay codes and `departure_report` — as now. The pilot's `DELAY` command does shift them, as now (the pilot reported the delay themselves).
4. Safety net at pushback: on the OOOI OUT tick (or entering PUSHBACK — whichever is earlier) all pre-departure scenarios that have not started yet but could start are launched immediately, in the order of their triggers; phase conditions are checked against the pre-pushback phase. Waits inside them (`fuel_slip`: `wait until flight.fuel_stable`, up to 6 min) end on that tick with a `TIMEOUT` outcome / current data. A `PREDEP_FLUSH` event with a list of ids goes to the feed and the recording.
5. Documentation: `05` "Late start" (version 4), `CLAUDE.md` if options change.

**Assumptions (approved by the user 2026-10-27, item 5 — the source is to be found during implementation):**
1. "Departure" for this purpose is OOOI OUT (start of pushback), not STD: the pilot chooses the moment of pushback.
2. The window margin is 2 min before STD (currently 1 min), so that the final loadsheet arrives before the doors close.
3. A slot does not shift documents (item 3). The alternative is to shift them as now and rely only on the safety net of item 4; then with a slot the documents would arrive all at once during pushback.
4. The safety net sends all pre-departure scenarios, including the irregular ones from `irregular/` (`dg_notoc`, `deicing_holdover`, `vip_onboard`…), not only the base documents.
5. In real operations the final loadsheet must be on board before pushback (common practice, primary source with a date `[VERIFY]` — for example EU OPS / IATA AHM 516/590); if no source is found, record it as a game rule.

**Done (2026-11-07):** `lateStartVersion: 4` in `packages/core/src/scenario/engine.ts` (window `[start, STD − 2 min]`, `preEndMs`/`preShiftMs`/`flushed` in state; a slot does not shift documents, the pilot's `DELAY` does; pre-departure scenarios start in trigger order, a document waits for an earlier running document — so `final_loadsheet` does not overtake `fuel_slip`, which waits for fuel; flush and the `PREDEP_FLUSH` event on OUT / PUSHBACK). The app and `replay synth` write version 4; feed line `sys.predep_flush` (6 languages), replay report — `FLUSH`. Item 2 of the card is handled by the core outbox (the first document at the moment of start, then one per `minGapMs`), no separate logic was needed. Assumption 5: the primary source is EU 965/2012 CAT.POL.MAB.105(a) ("prior to each flight", checked 2026-11-07 against the UK CAA Regulatory Library); it has no "before pushback" moment — recorded as a game rule (docs/05). By the time of implementation the JAL523 recording had already been deleted by rotation (the last 20 are kept) — a synthetic flight of the same shape was used instead (start 40 min before, slot, pushback at STD + 1): in version 3 the final loadsheet comes after OUT, in version 4 before it (`packages/scenarios/test/base.test.ts`). Side effect: on an ordinary synthetic flight in version 3 a slot pushed the preliminary loadsheet, takeoff data and fuel slip past pushback — they never arrived; in version 4 they all arrive (replay report snapshot updated, 10 → 13 telex).

**Out of scope:** scenarios after OUT (`departure_report` and others), changes to texts and offsets in YAML, the default `minGapMs` (60 s), the T31 transmission line (its delay is added on top — when starting 2–3 min before STD the messages may still arrive during pushback, and item 4 then catches them).

**Done when:** tests on a synthetic flight (`minGapMs` 60 s) with starts 30, 20, 10, 5 and 2 min before STD: all 5 base documents before OUT (at 2 min — including via the safety net), order `crew_info → prelim_loadsheet → takeoff_data → fuel_slip → final_loadsheet`; the same flight with a slot (scenario `slot_ctot` forced) — the same; the pilot's `DELAY 20` shifts documents as before; the JAL523 recording with `lateStartVersion: 4` — final loadsheet before 09:11:10 (`--check` on it is expected to show a mismatch with the live run); recordings with versions 1–3 give the same messages as before; state serialization in the middle of the window; `pnpm check` green.

**Human check:** 1) Any aircraft, a SimBrief plan with STD in 25–30 min, start the flight in the app → before pushback begins, the crew info, preliminary loadsheet, takeoff data, fuel slip and final loadsheet arrived in the FMS — in that order. 2) The same with a start 5 min before STD and immediate pushback → all documents arrive (some during pushback, one per minute), the feed has a line about the flush. 3) If a slot dropped (`SLOT ALLOCATION`) — the documents still arrive before STD, and `CTOT` in the message and in `departure_report` is unchanged.

#### T62 — App protection: authorship marks, Electron Fuses, copy traces **R**
User request 2026-10-28, a continuation of T19. The legal part exists (`LICENSE`, EULA `apps/desktop/build/license.txt`, `THIRD-PARTY-NOTICES.md`, `"license": "UNLICENSED"`). The technical part does not: `app.asar` unpacks with `npx asar extract`, the .NET 8 bridge decompiles with ILSpy almost back to source, and there is no authorship string in the built JS or in the bridge assembly. A client application cannot be fully protected; the goal is provable authorship, a ban on code substitution in an installed build, and a way to recognise our data in someone else's product.

**Included:**
1. **SPDX header** in every source file (`*.ts`, `*.tsx`, `*.mjs`, `*.cs`, except generated ones and `node_modules`): `// SPDX-License-Identifier: LicenseRef-ACARS-Link-Proprietary` and `// Copyright (c) 2026 AVAILOFF`. An applying script (`tools/`, idempotent) and a check in `pnpm check` (test or script: a file without the header is an error). Generated files (`squawk/`, `eclipses.ts`, `openflights.ts`) get the header from the generator; data under someone else's licence (OpenFlights — ODbL) gets its own SPDX, not ours.
2. **Banner in the bundle:** a copyright and version line at the start of every JS file of main, preload and renderer (`electron.vite.config.ts`, Rollup `output.banner` — survives minification). Bridge attributes `Company`, `Copyright`, `Product` in `.csproj` (visible in the exe properties).
