3. **Electron Fuses** when building the installer: `RunAsNode` off, `EnableNodeOptionsEnvironmentVariable` off, `EnableNodeCliInspectArguments` off, `EnableEmbeddedAsarIntegrityValidation` on, `OnlyLoadAppFromAsar` on. Method — an electron-builder option or an `afterPack` hook with `@electron/fuses` `[VERIFY]` against the electron-builder 26.17 and Electron 44 documentation (fuse names, support for asar integrity validation on Windows). The app currently does not use `ELECTRON_RUN_AS_NODE`, `--inspect` or `utilityProcess` (checked 2026-10-28); `pnpm icon` runs Electron from `node_modules` and is not affected.
4. **Copy traces (canaries)** — per decision 2 below.
5. **EULA** (`apps/desktop/build/license.txt`, both language parts): an explicit ban on reverse engineering, decompiling, unpacking `app.asar` and extracting scenarios, tables and message texts; a clause that rights granted by law independently of the contract (decompilation for interoperability) are not restricted. Review by a lawyer remains `[AT RELEASE]`.
6. Documentation: `CLAUDE.md` ("Commands", if a script appears), the T19 card — a link to T62.
7. The check in `pnpm check` covers `*.ts`, `*.tsx`, `*.js`, `*.mjs`, `*.cjs`, `*.cs`, `*.py` (the Python generators of the squawk tables were added to the list during implementation).

**User decisions (2026-10-28):**
1. No obfuscation (neither JS nor the bridge) until there are signs of copying. Reasons: bundle size and speed, unreadable stack traces, the risk of breaking reflection in the bridge (the SimConnect wrapper already does not work in single-file — `CLAUDE.md`).
2. Canaries only where the pilot will not see them and where they do not affect messages (the realism rule is not relaxed): a unique entry with no path to a message, or a "fingerprint" in the order/formatting of data. No false entries are put into `curfews.ts`, squawks, flight levels, FIRs or other tables that produce messages. The specific places and form — to be listed for the user during implementation before being added, and the approved ones recorded here; canaries to be covered by a test that none of them reaches a message. **Approved 2026-10-28:** one canary — an invented AeroDataBox model string `EMBRAER 190 STD-AVF` → `null` in `packages/core/src/ext/aircraft-models.ts` (no family — the same as the string not being in the table). The user did not choose a label in the YAML scenarios.
3. A unique ID in every build — not done.
4. The EULA is extended in this task (item 5 of "Included").

**Done (2026-10-28):** `tools/spdx/` — `header.mjs` (header by extension: `//` for ts/tsx/js/mjs/cjs/cs, `#` for py; `openflights.ts` — `ODbL-1.0`), `check.mjs` (`pnpm spdx`, `pnpm spdx --write`; files from `git ls-files --cached --others --exclude-standard`), test `tools/spdx/test/header.test.mjs` (Vitest project `@acars/spdx`); `pnpm check` = typecheck + lint + spdx + test. The header was added to 263 files; the generators `tools/eclipse-table`, `tools/openflights`, `tools/squawk-tables/generate.py` write it themselves. The banner is the `acars-copyright-banner` plugin in `electron.vite.config.ts` (`generateBundle`: Rollup `output.banner` did not reach the files). Bridge — `Product`, `Company`, `Copyright`, `AssemblyTitle` in `AcarsBridge.csproj`. Fuses — the electron-builder 26.17 option `electronFuses` (`@electron/fuses` 1.8.0 is its dependency), `grantFileProtocolExtraPrivileges` untouched (the renderer loads via `loadFile`); checked on `electron-builder --win --dir`: `@electron/fuses read` shows the required values, the app starts, a copy with one changed byte in `app.asar` exits with code 1. EULA — items 2 (g), (h) and the statutory-rights clause in both languages. Canary and test `packages/core/test/canary.test.ts`.

**Out of scope:** obfuscation and build IDs (decisions 1, 3), code signing (user decision 2026-10-22 in T19 — not done), licence keys and an activation server, moving logic to a backend, registering a trademark and the program — organisational steps for the user, not code.

**Done when:** all source files have the header, the check in `pnpm check` catches a file without it (test on a fixture); in `out/` every JS starts with the banner; `pnpm desktop:dist` builds an installer with fuses — the check `npx @electron/fuses read --app <exe>` shows the required values `[VERIFY]` the command; the app from the installer starts, the bridge starts; the EULA is extended in both languages; canaries agreed, recorded in the card, and a test confirms that none of them reaches a message; `pnpm check` and `pnpm bridge:test` green.

**Human check:** 1) `pnpm desktop:dist`, install, run — the flight starts, the bridge connects to the simulator as before. 2) In the installation folder change one byte in `resources\app.asar` — the app does not start (integrity check). 3) `"<path>\ACARS Link.exe" --inspect` and a launch with `ELECTRON_RUN_AS_NODE=1` — the debugger does not attach, Node mode does not turn on. 4) `AcarsBridge.exe` properties → "Details" — company, copyright, product.

### Audit 2026-10-28 (cards T63–T69)

Code audit 2026-10-28 (user request): `pnpm check` green (1354 tests), `pnpm bridge:test` — 154. The cards below are P0–P1 findings and two features the user asked to be written up (flight summary, flight resume). Order is by priority: T63 and T64 — P0, T65–T68 — P1, T69 — feature.

#### T63 — Repository backup and automatic check **R** (P0)
The repository has no remote copy (`git remote -v` is empty, 2026-10-28): all code and history is a single copy in `Downloads`. `pnpm check` is run only manually.

**Included:**
1. A remote private repository (kind — decision 1) and a first push of all branches the user decides to keep (decision 3). Push — only after the user's explicit consent (`CLAUDE.md`, "How to work").
2. Before the first push — check that the history contains no secrets or personal data: `.env*`, logon codes, RapidAPI keys, raw OFPs (`reference/ofp/raw/`), flight recordings (`reference/recordings/`) — search the whole history (`git log -p`, `git grep` across all commits). Anything found goes to the user before pushing.
3. Automatic check (kind — decision 2): `pnpm check` and `pnpm bridge:test` on Windows (the bridge and paths are Windows). Without the MSFS SDK the bridge builds without SimConnect (`AcarsBridge.csproj`) — the bridge tests allow this.
4. Cleanup: `.claude/worktrees/T58-rename-atc` (197 MB, not in `git worktree list`) — delete after the user confirms; merged `task/*` branches and `backup/pre-ux-audit-20261017-172759` — a list to the user, delete only the approved ones.
5. Documentation: `README.md` (where the repository lives, how the check runs), `CLAUDE.md` — if a script or hook appears.

**User decisions (needed before implementation):**
1. Where to store: private GitHub, another host, or only a local `git bundle` copy on another disk/cloud on a schedule.
2. The check: GitHub Actions (`windows-latest`) or a local pre-push hook (`pnpm check`) — or both.
3. Which branches to push (`master` and the current task — at minimum).

**User decisions 2026-10-28:** 1) private GitHub `AVAILOFF/acars-link`. 2) GitHub Actions only (`windows-latest`, on every push and PR), no local hook. 3) Push `master`, `task/T63-repo-backup`, `backup/pre-ux-audit-20261017-172759`, `task/ux-audit-fixes`. T63 is built on top of `task/T62-app-protection` (not merged) and is merged into `master` before the first push — the workflow must live in `master`. Cleanup: delete the T58 worktree; delete `task/ux-audit-fixes` and `backup/pre-ux-audit-…` locally after pushing; keep the merged `task/*`. History search (187 commits, all branches): no secrets, `.env*`, raw OFPs or flight recordings; in tests — only dummy logon codes; in `bridge/msfs/package/README.md` — a path with the Windows user name (`C:\Users\avail\…`), acceptable for a private repository.

**Out of scope:** a public repository, releases, signing (T19).

**Done when:** the remote copy exists and contains `master` and the approved branches; the history search found no secrets (or what was found was removed by the user's decision before pushing); the check runs automatically and passes on the current `master`; the abandoned worktree is deleted.

**Human check:** 1) Clone the repository into a new folder, `pnpm install && pnpm check` — green. 2) Make a commit with a deliberate linter error in a separate branch — the check (hook or CI) catches it.

#### T64 — Flight resume after the app is closed or crashes **R** (P0)
Currently the flight lives only in memory: when the window is closed — a warning "The flight cannot be resumed after a restart" (`apps/desktop/src/main/close-dialog.ts`); if the app or PC crashes the flight is lost. For a 10+ hour flight this is the loss of the whole flight. Everything needed for resuming exists: the core state is serializable (`AcarsCore.getState` / `fromState`, docs/03 "Core: contract"), and the flight recording (T11) holds all core inputs in order and is written synchronously (`apps/desktop/src/main/recording.ts`).

**Included:**
1. **Fix in the core:** `AcarsCore.emergencies` (`packages/core/src/core.ts`, the MAYDAY/PAN chain for the next tick, T47) does not make it into `CoreState` — add it to the state (optional field, old states read as an empty list). Test: `getState` between the tick with `ATC_EMERGENCY` and the next one → `fromState` → the chain starts.
2. **Resume method** — decision 1. The core is restored deterministically (invariant 4): the same OFP, scenarios, options and seed from the recording header.
3. **On app start:** if the last flight is not closed (no `end` event and the flight was not stopped by the user) and is no more than N hours old (decision 4) — an offer "Continue flight <callsign> <from>–<to>" / "Discard". On discard the recording stays as is.
4. **What is restored:** core state (phase, OOOI, scenarios, awaited replies, `minGapMs` outbox, XATC, T31 line, flags), the message feed from the `out` events of the recording, `sessionFired`, binding to the same recording (continuing the same file with a `resume` event, or a new file — decision 3). The Hoppie transport starts afresh: polling stations that have already sent messages.
5. **Downtime:** there was no telemetry — the FSM sees a gap (`TELEMETRY_LOST` / `DISCONTINUITY`, docs/04), scenarios with `telemetryLost` wait as now. Messages whose time came during the downtime — decision 2.
6. **Recording format:** a `resume` event (`tools/replay/src/format.ts`) — `pnpm replay run` on a recording with resume gives the same messages that went out in the live flight (`--check`).
7. Documentation: docs/03 (resume), docs/05 (behaviour after downtime), `CLAUDE.md`, the close dialog text (EN/RU) — no longer "cannot be resumed".

**User decisions (needed before implementation):**
1. Method: (a) run the recording through the core at start (no new files, exact by construction; measure the run time of a long flight during implementation — `[VERIFY]` on a 10 h recording); (b) a `getState` snapshot in `<userData>/flight-state.json` every N seconds and on phase change (fast, but a new file and state migrations); (c) snapshot + the tail of the recording appended after it. Proposal — (a) if running a 10-hour recording takes a few seconds, otherwise (c).
2. Messages "slept through" during downtime: send immediately in order with `minGapMs` / send only the latest per scenario / skip stale ones (for example, pre-departure ones after OUT).
3. Recording: append to the same file (a `resume` event) or start a new one with a reference to the previous.
4. The cut-off after which a flight is not offered for resume (for example, 24 h from the last line of the recording).
5. Telexes the core issued but the transport did not manage to send before the crash: the recording has `out` (issued by the core) but no "sent by Hoppie". Options: resend (a duplicate in the cockpit is possible) or treat as sent (a loss is possible). The "resend" option needs a new recording event `sent` from the transport.

**User decisions 2026-10-29:** 1) (a) — run the recording through the core at start; measurement: synthetic RJTT–KLAX 12 h (43,580 ticks, 18 MB) — 1.7 s including Node start, a real DLH5MM recording of 3 h 53 min — the same 32 telexes as live. Before continuing, each telex of the run is checked against the `out` line; if they do not match, the flight is not restored. 2) Messages whose time came during the downtime are sent in order via `minGapMs` (core behaviour unchanged). 3) The recording is appended to the same file, a `resume` event. 4) 24 h from the last write to the file (file modification time: with "simulator time" the times in the recording are simulator UTC). 5) Telexes not issued are resent; a new `sent` event (`n` — the `out` line number, `result`: `sent` | `failed`). 6) (executor's question) "Record flights" off — the recording is still written to the temporary folder `<userData>/flight-temp` and deleted with the flight, resume works always. Branch `task/T64-flight-resume` — from `master` (T70 is already in it).

**Done (2026-10-29, branch `task/T64-flight-resume`):** core — `CoreState.emergencies` (test in `packages/scenarios/test/t47.test.ts`); recording format — `sent`, `resume`, `header.simTime`, `recordedCoreOptions` (shared assembly of core options from the header for replay and the app), the `RESUME` line in the report; app — `recording.ts` (temporary recording, `fileResume`: search, appending, discard), `FlightSession` (`resume`, `suspend`, `sent` lines), `AppController` (`resumeFlight`, `discardResume`, `AppState.resume`, `canResume`; app exit — `suspend` instead of `stop`), `ResumeDialog.tsx`, EN/RU texts, close dialog by `canResume`; tests — `apps/desktop/test/resume.test.ts`, `tools/replay/test/replay.test.ts`. The plan order "T67 before T64" was broken at the user's request (there are no logs yet).

**Out of scope:** moving a flight to another PC, resume in the middle of flight closing (`closing`) — the flight is closed as before if the user declines.

**Done when:** a test on a synthetic flight: stopping at each phase (BOARDING, PUSHBACK, CLIMB, CRUISE, DESCENT, TAXI_IN), resume → subsequent messages match a continuous run of the same flight (with 0 s downtime); a 10 min downtime in CRUISE — behaviour per decision 2; `emergencies` survives serialization; a recording with `resume` passes `replay run --check`; old recordings without `resume` are read; `pnpm check` green.

**Human check:** 1) Any aircraft, flight up to CRUISE, kill the app process in Task Manager, start again → an offer to continue, phase CRUISE, the feed in place, the next scenario message arrives in the FMS. 2) The same via closing the window → the dialog no longer says the flight is lost. 3) Decline the resume → the flight is not loaded, the recording stays in the recordings folder.

#### T65 — Bridge: only the app can connect (Origin and token) (P1)
`AccessPolicy.IsAllowedOrigin` (`bridge/msfs/src/AcarsBridge/BridgeServer.cs`) admits connections without `Origin`, with `Origin: null`, `file://`, and from any `http(s)://localhost:*` / `127.0.0.1:*`. `Origin: null` is sent by a sandboxed iframe (`sandbox`) on any site, so a page in a browser on the same PC can open `ws://127.0.0.1:47830/?protocol=2`, read telemetry (coordinates, aircraft, fuel) and send the commands `smoke_start` / `rescue_start` (T22, T54). The app connects from the main process via Node `WebSocket` without `Origin` (`apps/desktop/src/main/index.ts`, `createSocket`), so it does not need the exceptions for `null`/`file://`.

**Included:**
1. `IsAllowedOrigin`: reject `null`, `file://` and any web origins; no `Origin` — allowed (native clients). The `localhost` exception — only if `pnpm desktop:dev` needs it (check: in dev the renderer does not connect to the bridge — `[VERIFY]` in the code; if not needed — remove it).
2. **Launch token:** when starting the bridge the app generates a random token (`node:crypto`, ≥ 128 bits) and passes it to the bridge (a command-line argument or an environment variable — pick the one not visible to other users of the PC; `[VERIFY]` visibility of process arguments in Windows), the client passes it when connecting (header or query); the bridge answers 403 without the correct token. Comparison — in constant time (`CryptographicOperations.FixedTimeEquals`). A bridge started manually without a token (dev, `bridge/msfs/README.md`) — admits without a token, as now (a console message).
3. The token is not written to logs or flight recordings (invariant 2, by analogy with the logon code).
4. Tests: `AcarsBridge.Tests` — `Origin: null`, `file://`, a foreign site, no `Origin`; with token / without / wrong. Client tests (`apps/desktop/test`) — the token in the URL/header.
5. Documentation: `bridge/msfs/README.md` (connection protocol), docs/06.

**Done (2026-10-29, branch `task/T65-bridge-token`):** `[VERIFY]` items closed: the renderer does not connect to the bridge (no `WebSocket` in `apps/desktop/src/renderer`; `ws://localhost:*` in the CSP is Vite HMR), the `localhost` exception removed; a Windows process command line may be saved by the audit log (event 4688, "Include command line in process creation events", learn.microsoft.com) — so the token goes in the environment variable `ACARS_BRIDGE_TOKEN`, which the bridge reads and clears. Client — the header `X-Acars-Bridge-Token` (not a query: the URL ends up in error texts and in the Kestrel log with `--verbose`); Node `WebSocket` in Electron 44 (Node 24.21, undici 7.29) passes `headers` — verified. Bridge — `AccessPolicy.IsAllowedOrigin` (only no `Origin`), `IsValidToken` (`FixedTimeEquals`), `BridgeServer.Build(…, token)`; app — `bridgeToken` (`randomBytes(32)`, per launch), `bridgeEnv`, `bridgeHeaders`. The manual check file is `bridge/msfs/test/manual/sandbox-iframe.html`.

**Out of scope:** channel encryption (loopback), user authentication.

**Done when:** the tests above are green; the installed app connects to its own bridge; a page with `<iframe sandbox="allow-scripts">` that opens a WebSocket to the bridge is refused; `pnpm check` and `pnpm bridge:test` green.

**Human check:** 1) Install the build, start MSFS and a flight — telemetry flows as before. 2) Open in Chrome a local HTML file with a sandboxed iframe that connects to `ws://127.0.0.1:47830/` (the executor will prepare the file) — a connection error in the page console, nothing changes in the app.

#### T66 — Single app instance (P1)
`apps/desktop/src/main/index.ts` has no `app.requestSingleInstanceLock()`. A second launch (shortcut, `ACARS Link.bat`) creates a second Hoppie transport with the same logon code: double polling (invariant 6), a `callsign already in use` error in one of them, two recordings of one flight, a second bridge on a busy port.

**Included:**
1. `requestSingleInstanceLock()` before creating the window; without the lock — exit before any network request and bridge launch.
2. The `second-instance` event — show and restore the first instance's window.
3. Dev (`pnpm desktop:dev`) — the same behaviour `[VERIFY]`, does not interfere with the electron-vite HMR restart of main.
4. Test (`apps/desktop/test`): the "exit / show window" choice logic is extracted into a function without Electron.

**Done (2026-10-29, branch `task/T66-single-instance`):** `apps/desktop/src/main/single-instance.ts` — `holdSingleInstance` (the lock first thing in `main()`, before `whenReady`, settings, transport and bridge; without it — `app.quit()` and return), `bringToFront` (restore, show, focus; no window yet — nothing); test `apps/desktop/test/single-instance.test.ts`. `[VERIFY]` dev closed: `pnpm desktop:dev` (`electron-vite dev` without `--watch`) does not restart main, HMR is only for the renderer; with `--watch` electron-vite 5.0.0 does `kill()` and an immediate new launch — 5 restarts in a row, the app came up each time. Built app (`out/`): a second launch exits in ~0.85 s, no processes are added.

**Done when:** a second launch of the installed app does not create a duplicate process and brings up the first one's window; `pnpm check` green.

**Human check:** 1) Start the app, begin a flight, launch the shortcut again — no second window appears, the first comes to the foreground, no Hoppie errors in the feed. 2) Minimise the window, launch again — the window is restored.

#### T67 — Logs and diagnostics export **R** (P1)
The installed build has no diagnostics: the bridge is launched with `stdio: 'ignore'` (`apps/desktop/src/main/bridge-process.ts`), the main process writes only `console.error`, the installed app has no console. On a failure the user (and the developer) has nothing but the flight recording.

**Included:**
1. A file log of the main process in `<userData>/logs/` (levels, UTC time, rotation by size/file count — decision 2): app and bridge start/stop, bridge and transport status changes, errors (stack traces), `render-process-gone`, `unresponsive`, unhandled exceptions and rejected promises (`process.on('uncaughtException' | 'unhandledRejection')` — written to the log, the default process behaviour is not changed).
2. Bridge output (stdout/stderr) — to a separate log file with the same rotation.
3. **Secret filter** at the logger input: Hoppie logon code, RapidAPI key, SimBrief name, bridge token (T65) — replaced with `***` (by analogy with `HoppieTransport.clean`). Test: a secret in a message and in a stack trace does not reach the file.
4. An "Export diagnostics" button (Settings or Help): a zip or folder with logs, app and bridge versions, settings without secrets and (via a checkbox) the last flight recording — decision 3. EN/RU.
5. Documentation: `CLAUDE.md` (where the logs are), in-app help.

**User decisions (needed before implementation):**
1. Own implementation (a few dozen lines) or a package (`electron-log` etc. — licence in `THIRD-PARTY-LICENSES.txt`).
2. Rotation: for example, 5 files of 5 MB.
3. Export contents and whether to include the flight recording (it has the callsign, route, AeroDataBox data — T30 C9).

**User decisions 2026-10-29:** 1) own implementation, no packages; 2) rotation — 5 files of 5 MB per log (main and bridge separately); 3) export — a single .zip (own code on `node:zlib`), contents — logs, app/Electron/Node/OS/bridge versions, settings without secrets (for accounts — only "set / not set"), bridge and transport state; the last flight recording — via a checkbox, off by default.

**Done (2026-10-29, branch `task/T67-logs`):** `apps/desktop/src/main/log.ts` — `Redactor` (known secrets case-insensitively, a safety net for `logon=…`, `ACARS_BRIDGE_TOKEN`, `X-Acars-Bridge-Token`), `RotatingFile` (synchronous writes, `main.log` → `main.1.log` … `main.4.log`, disk errors suppressed), `Logger` (UTC, level, stack trace with the `cause` chain), `watchProcess`. The redactor learns secrets from `SecretStore` (`onValue`: every value read and saved; all three are read at start) and the bridge token — immediately. Verified on Electron 44 (2026-10-29): main handles exceptions itself (a dialog) and runs in `warn` mode for rejected promises — so `uncaughtExceptionMonitor` and `unhandledRejection` do not change behaviour. What is logged: start (versions, OS, locale), bridge launch and exit, bridge link/simulator changes (without `connecting`, only changes), Hoppie send statuses and errors (`session.ts`), flight start/stop/resume (without callsign and route), recording errors, failed IPC requests (`AppFailure` — as a line, others — with a stack), `render-process-gone`, `unresponsive`, exit. Bridge — `stdio: pipe`, stdout/stderr lines → `bridge.log` (`bridge-process.ts`). Export — `diagnostics.ts`, `zip.ts` (deflate, UTF-8 names; verified with `Expand-Archive`), `AppController.exportDiagnostics`, IPC `exportDiagnostics`, the "Diagnostics" group in advanced settings (EN/RU), the help item "Logs and diagnostics". Tests — `apps/desktop/test/log.test.ts`, `diagnostics.test.ts`, `bridge-process.test.ts` (output of a real process into the log).

**Out of scope:** sending logs to a server, usage telemetry.

**Done when:** in the installed build after launch and a flight, `<userData>/logs/` contains the main and bridge logs; a renderer crash and a transport error are visible in the log; the secret filter test is green; the export collects files without secrets; `pnpm check` green.

**Human check:** 1) Install the build, a flight with MSFS → in the logs folder, lines about the bridge connection and Hoppie statuses, no logon code. 2) A wrong logon code → an error in the log with `***`. 3) "Export diagnostics" → the file opens, there are no secrets in it (search for the code manually).
#### T68 — Hoppie: logon code only in the POST body (P1)
`buildRequest` (`packages/transport/src/hoppie/protocol.ts`) sends a GET if the URL is shorter than 256 characters: the logon code goes in the query string and can end up in proxy logs, in antivirus software with HTTPS inspection, and in runtime error messages (`fetchHttpClient` already hides the URL — `packages/transport/src/http.ts`). Per the tech.html check of 2026-10-22 (docs/08), POST is allowed and recommended, and GET and POST can be mixed.

**In scope:**
1. All Hoppie requests (`telex`, `cpdlc`, `poll`, `ping`, the code check in `apps/desktop/src/main/credentials.ts`) — POST to `connect.html` with no URL parameters; `logon`, `from`, `to`, `type`, `packet` — in the `application/x-www-form-urlencoded` body. `[VERIFY]` against tech.html that the server accepts `logon` from the POST body (currently in a POST the body carries only `packet`) — a human check with a single `ping` before the merge.
2. `MAX_GET_URL_LENGTH` and the GET branch — delete, or keep for tests only (decide during implementation, without changing network behaviour).
3. Tests in `packages/transport/test`: there is no `logon=` in the URL, the body contains all parameters, a long telex takes the same path.
4. Documentation: docs/06 (Hoppie: request method).

**Done (2026-10-29, branch `task/T68-hoppie-post`):** `buildRequest` — always POST to `connect.html` with no query, all parameters in the body; `MAX_GET_URL_LENGTH` and the GET branch are removed (network behaviour changes only in the method: long telexes were already sent by POST). `[VERIFY]` against tech.html: "You may use either the GET or the POST protocol, or both intermixed" applies to all request variables; the server reads `logon` from the body — a POST `ping` without `logon` → `error (no logon code)`, with an invalid one → `error {invalid logon code}` (2026-10-29). The response to a valid code is human check 1. Tests — `packages/transport/test/protocol.test.ts`, `hoppie-transport.test.ts` (no URL contains the code or a query), `apps/desktop/test/usability.test.ts` (code check).

**Out of scope:** changing the endpoint, SayIntentions (T17).

**Done when:** tests are green; no transport request or code check contains the logon code in the URL; `pnpm check` is green.

**Human check:** 1) Settings → Hoppie → "Check" with a valid code — success, with an invalid one — a code error. 2) A flight with any aircraft: the loadsheet arrives in the FMS, a pilot reply (`REQ WX` or a reply to a scenario) reaches the app.

#### T69 — Flight summary after ON_BLOCK **R**
User request of 2026-10-28. After ON_BLOCK (TURNAROUND / DONE) the app shows only the feed; there are no flight results, although the data is collected: OOOI (`PhaseMachine.oooi`), touchdown rate (`touchdownFpm`, T46 — not shown in the UI), fuel from telemetry, the OFP plan (`OfpTimes`: `schedOutMs`/`schedInMs`, `schedBlockSec`, `estTimeEnrouteSec`; `OfpFuel`: `planRampKg`, `planTakeoffKg`, `planLandingKg`, `enrouteBurnKg`, `taxiKg`), delay codes (T43, `delayParts`), sent messages and scenarios that fired.

**In scope:**
1. **Calculation in the core** — a pure function (a new module, for example `packages/core/src/summary/`): input — OFP, core state/events, telemetry snapshots at OOOI; output — the summary data. The same calculation is available in `tools/replay` (`pnpm replay summary <recording.jsonl>`), so the summary can be reproduced from a recording (invariant 4). Numbers come only from data (invariant 8).
2. **Contents** (final — decision 1): actual OOOI against the schedule (OUT/OFF/ON/IN, late/early), block and flight time against the plan; fuel at OUT, OFF, ON, IN against the OFP plan, actual enroute and taxi burn; touchdown rate (ft/min, if the sim provided it; if not — "no data", we invent nothing); delays with codes; diversion/go-around, if there were any; the number of airline messages and pilot replies, irregular scenarios that fired.
3. **UI:** a summary card in FlightView in TURNAROUND / DONE (EN/RU), it stays until the next OFP is loaded.
4. **Storage** (decision 2): the summary — as a `summary` event line at the end of the flight recording and/or in the flight log `<userData>/logbook.json` (a list, unlimited or the last N).
5. **Export** (decision 3): copy as text / save as CSV or JSON.
6. Documentation: docs/03 (summary in the core contract or next to it), `CLAUDE.md` (`pnpm replay summary`).

**User decisions (needed before implementation):**
1. Summary fields — the whole list from item 2 or a subset; whether ratings are needed ("soft landing") — if so, thresholds from a verified source with a date (realism rule item 2), otherwise only numbers without ratings.
2. Where to store: only in the recording, the flight log, or both; the log is kept longer than 20 recordings (`KEEP_RECORDINGS`).
3. Export: text to the clipboard, CSV, JSON — which is needed.
4. Whether to send the summary to the pilot in the cockpit by telex — by default **no** (a real airline does not send the crew such a summary; if yes — the format from a real source, realism rule item 1).

**User decisions (2026-10-30):** 1) all fields from item 2, no ratings — only numbers and the difference from the plan; 2) both the recording (`summary`) and the `logbook.json` log with no limit on the number of flights; 3) export — JSON; 4) do not send to the cockpit. Assumption (approved 2026-10-30): the airport of actual landing is the one nearest to the touchdown point among the OFP airports (destination, departure, alternates, ETOPS) and the diversion airport, if it is no more than 5 NM away; farther — "unknown airport" with the touchdown coordinates; diversion — a landing at an airport other than the destination.

**Done (2026-10-30, branch `task/T69-flight-summary`):** the phase machine stores the fuel and position of the snapshot at each OOOI (`PhaseMachine.oooiAt`, `goArounds`); `packages/core/src/summary/report.ts` — `flightReport()` / `AcarsCore.report()` (OOOI against `sched_*`, block against `sched_block`, flight against `est_time_enroute`, fuel at OUT/OFF/ON/IN against `plan_ramp`/`plan_takeoff`/`plan_landing`, taxi/enroute burn against `taxi`/`enroute_burn`, touchdown, landing/diversion, go-arounds, delay with codes, message counters in `CoreState.counts`, irregular scenarios from `ScenarioEngine.reportData`). The summary is taken at the moment of `endFlight` (a turnaround after that is already the next flight): a `summary` line after `end` (`tools/replay/src/format.ts`), `pnpm replay summary [--json] [--check]` (`summary.ts`, `run.ts`: `report`, `ended`, `recordedReport`). App: `AppState.report` until the next OFP is loaded, `ReportPanel.tsx` (EN/RU), "Save as JSON…" (`exportReport`), the log `main/logbook.ts` (an unreadable file is set aside as `logbook.json.<time>.unreadable`). Tests: `packages/core/test/report.test.ts`, `tools/replay/test/replay.test.ts` (T69), `apps/desktop/test/report.test.ts`.

**Out of scope:** PIREPs to virtual airlines (smartCARS, phpVMS, etc.), an online log, rating the piloting from the flight profile.

**Done when:** a calculation test on a synthetic flight (`replay synth`): OOOI, block and fuel match the expected values, without `touchdownFpm` — "no data"; a flight with a diversion (T50) — the airport of actual landing; `pnpm replay summary` on a recording gives the same summary as the UI; EN/RU; `pnpm check` is green.

**Human check:** 1) Any aircraft, a full flight to ON_BLOCK, "Close flight" → summary: the OOOI times match the feed, fuel matches the FMS/EFB readings at the stand within 100 kg, the touchdown rate matches what the sim/add-on showed (if it shows one). 2) Load the next OFP → the summary disappears (or stays in the log — per decision 2).

#### T70 — Welcome, interface tour and readiness checklist
User request of 2026-10-28: an introductory welcome and a guide/navigation through the app. Currently a newcomer sees an empty "Flight" tab; the step-by-step instruction exists only in "Help" (`help.ts`, "First flight, step by step"). Closes audit items U-01 (readiness checklist) and U-02 (bridge status), which were not part of T56.

**In scope:**
1. **Welcome wizard** on first launch — a modal `<dialog>` (like `RequestDialog`), 4 steps: (1) what the app does + interface language (applied immediately); (2) accounts — the SimBrief name with "Check", the Hoppie code or the "Offline" transport (the same store actions as in settings); (3) simulator — the live status of the bridge and the simulator; (4) first flight — load the OFP, the flight number in the FMS, connect ACARS in the aircraft, "Start flight"; buttons "To the flight" and "Show the interface". "Skip" at every step; steps 2–3 are optional.
2. **Interface tour** — highlighting elements with a caption (5–6 stops: the side menu, the top bar, the feed and the FMS screen, the request to the airline, the status bar). A custom component with no new dependencies, targets are `data-tour` attributes; "Back / Next / Close", keyboard; no timers (invariant 5).
3. **Readiness checklist** on the "Flight" tab with no OFP (U-01, U-02): SimBrief, Hoppie or offline, bridge, simulator, OFP; for an item not done — a jump to the relevant settings group. The logic is a pure `readiness()` function in the renderer.
4. **Help:** an "App interface" item (EN/RU), buttons "Show the welcome again" and "Take the interface tour".
5. The `welcomeDone` setting (`Settings`), saved via `updateSettings`.

**User decisions (2026-10-28):** format — wizard + tour; for users with an existing `settings.json` the welcome is not shown (`welcomeDone` = true when old settings without the field are loaded), for new ones — once; U-01/U-02 are included in the card; the wizard has 4 steps (XATC, live data and other advanced things are not in the wizard).

**Out of scope:** `packages/*`; launching the bridge with a button from the app; new settings other than `welcomeDone`; visual redesign.

**Done when:** tests of `readiness()` (all combinations), the default `welcomeDone` values for a new and an old `settings.json`, the Help item in both languages; `pnpm check` is green.

**Done (2026-10-28, branch `task/T70-welcome`):** the `welcomeDone` setting (`shared/api.ts`, `main/settings.ts`: false by default, `sanitize` of an old file without the field → true); `renderer/src/welcome.ts` — `readiness()` (SimBrief, network/offline, bridge, simulator, OFP; states `ok`/`wait`/`todo`, the settings field for "Set up"), the wizard steps, the tour stops `TOUR_STOPS`, `placeCard()`; `WelcomeDialog.tsx` — the wizard (`<dialog>`, account forms from `SettingsAccounts`) and `ReadyList`; `Tour.tsx` — the tour (`<dialog>` over the window, shadow highlight, ←/→ arrows, Esc); `store.ts` — `welcome`, `tour`, `focusField`, `finishWelcome` (saves even when the store is busy), `setTour`, `openField`; the checklist in the flight card with no OFP (`FlightView.tsx`), scrolling to the field in `SettingsView.tsx`; "Help": welcome and tour buttons, the `ui` item "What is where in the app" (EN/RU); the `welcome.*`, `ready.*`, `tour.*` texts; tests `apps/desktop/test/welcome.test.ts`, `stores.test.ts`.

**Human check:** 1) Clean profile (rename `%APPDATA%\ACARS Link`), `pnpm desktop:dev` → the welcome. 2) Go through the steps with a real SimBrief/Hoppie, then in "Offline" mode. 3) With MSFS running and with X-Plane 12 — the status at step 3 becomes "connected". 4) Go through the tour: the highlight matches the elements at different window sizes, Esc closes. 5) Restart — no welcome; from "Help" it opens again. 6) An old profile with settings — no welcome.

#### T71 — Update Electron to 44.10.0 (security) (P0)
Audit of 2026-10-30 (user request): `pnpm audit` — `electron` 44.4.5, which is included in the installer, has a high advisory "Mitigation for compromised eMudhra intermediate certificates" (fixed in ≥ 44.9.1) and a moderate one "Pointer lock permission requests…" (≥ 44.5.0). The other findings (`undici` in `@electron/get`, `fast-uri` in `ajv`) are build tools and scenario schema validation; they do not reach the working app over the network.

**In scope:** `electron` 44.4.5 → 44.10.0 (the latest release of the 44 branch as of 2026-10-30, released 2026-10-27; same major version), lockfile; a check that the Node `WebSocket` in the new Electron still passes the bridge token header (T65).

**Out of scope:** moving to Electron 45; updating electron-builder, electron-vite and other build dependencies.

**Done when:** `pnpm audit` has no findings for `electron`; `pnpm check` and `pnpm bridge:test` are green; `pnpm desktop:dist` builds the installer.

**Human check:** 1) `pnpm desktop:dist`, install over the existing one, launch — the window opens, the bridge connects to MSFS, a flight starts. 2) In "Help" or in `logs\main.log` the start line shows Electron 44.10.0.

### Audit 2026-10-31: data licences and release readiness (cards T72–T77)

Release readiness audit of 2026-10-31 (user request): `pnpm check` is green (1422 tests), `pnpm bridge:test` — 172. Check of the sources' terms dated 2026-10-31: NOAA SWPC — public domain (the `spaceweather.gov` footer → `weather.gov/disclaimer`; do not present as your own, do not present modified data as official, do not imply NOAA endorsement), TheSpaceDevs — "free to use the data in any way" (FAQ `github.com/TheSpaceDevs/Tutorials`, `faqs/faq_TSD.md`; attribution optional, they ask to cache and not to send requests from clients); both are closed. Open: EUROCONTROL CAL (no terms on `eurocontrol.int`, only "© EUROCONTROL"; on the EAD site of the same organisation — non-commercial reproduction with the source named), ICAO APAC eANP (`icao.int/pages/copyright.aspx`: "All rights reserved", only with written permission), natTrak (the `vatsimnetwork/nattrak` repository has no licence or API terms; maintained by the VATSIM Tech Team), `SimConnect.dll` (no reply from Microsoft/Asobo in topics 18233 and 18295 as of 2026-10-31).

#### T72 — Public build without CAL/eANP tables and without natTrak **R**
User decision of 2026-10-31. The EUROCONTROL CAL squawk tables (`packages/core/src/atc/squawk/eur-cal.ts`) and ICAO APAC eANP (`icao-apac.ts`) are imported statically by the core and end up in the main-process bundle, NAT tracks come from natTrak (`packages/data-sources/src/nattrak.ts` → `ExtDataUpdate.natTracks` → `OclService.track`, `packages/core/src/atc/oceanic.ts`). There is no permission to distribute them (above), so they must not be in a build for other people. Restating the same tables in our own words does not solve the problem: in the EU a database is protected separately from its text (Directive 96/9/EC, art. 7 — extraction of a substantial part), and ICAO's terms are "All rights reserved". This is not a legal assessment, but the reason a path without third-party data was chosen.

**In scope:**
1. **NAT tracks from the SimBrief OFP.** The OFP has a `tracks` field (in the three real OFPs in `reference/ofp/raw/` and in the fixtures it is empty `[]`/`{}`: the routes are not across the Atlantic). Parse `tracks` in the OFP normalisation (`packages/core/src/ofp/parse.ts`, type — `NatTrack` from `ext/data.ts` or our own, decide by format) and pass it to `OclService` instead of `input.ext.natTracks` (`xatc.ts`). The data comes from the user's SimBrief account, no separate licence is needed; the track is the one the dispatcher built the plan on. `[VERIFY]` before implementation on a real OFP across the Atlantic along a track (for example EGLL–KJFK): the structure of `tracks` (letter, points, levels, validity period), whether the track is in the route string (`general.route`), what happens when the plan is outside the tracks. If a needed field is missing (for example the validity period) — a separate assumption, with the user's approval (realism rule item 5).
2. **natTrak** — per decision 1: remove the adapter (`nattrak.ts`, the call in `service.ts`, the line in `THIRD-PARTY-NOTICES.md`) or keep it only in the personal build as a fallback source when the OFP has no tracks.
3. **Squawks in the public build.** For Europe and Asia/Pacific — the same rule that already applies to the rest of the world (approved 2026-10-09, T24): a random code from the seed in 0001–7777 without special codes (`SPECIAL_CODES`: SERA.13005, FAA NBCAP TBL A-1) and without codes ending in `00` (`randomPlan` in `squawk.ts`). The USA — unchanged (FAA NBCAP, public domain). We do not invent our own "plausible" ranges by country: there is no source for them ("Realism rule", item 1). The CAL and eANP tables stay in the private repository and in the personal build; if EUROCONTROL or ICAO give written permission — the table is included back in the public one.
4. **Two build variants.** The public variant does not contain `eur-cal.ts` and `icao-apac.ts` in the bundle (module replacement at build time — alias/define in `electron.vite.config.ts` — or a separate entry point; choose during implementation, the core stays pure — invariant 3). The variant goes into the core options and is written to the flight recording header (`recordedCoreOptions`, `tools/replay`): a public-build recording gives the same squawks in `replay run --check` (invariant 4). The build script — per decision 2; a check that the public `app.asar` has no strings from the tables (a test or a `desktop:dist` step).
5. Documentation: `THIRD-PARTY-NOTICES.md` (what goes into which build; NOAA SWPC and TheSpaceDevs — closed, dated 2026-10-31), docs/06 ("Squawk tables", "NAT oceanic clearance": source of tracks), docs/08 ("Deferred until the public release": the T24, T27, T28 and SWPC rows), `CLAUDE.md` ("Commands": the new build script).

**User decisions (needed before implementation):**
1. natTrak: remove entirely or keep in the personal build as a fallback source of tracks.
2. Which build is the default for `pnpm desktop:dist`: the personal one (as now) and a separate `pnpm desktop:dist:public` — or the other way round.

**User decisions (2026-10-31):** 1) natTrak stays in the personal build (fallback source), removed from the public one; 2) `pnpm desktop:dist` — the public build, the personal one — `pnpm desktop:dist:personal`; 3) the `tracks` format — from the SimBrief documentation: it is not documented anywhere (Navigraph, `SimbriefOfp` in the MSFS avionics, GitHub, 2026-10-31), so the user will send a real OFP across the Atlantic.

**Done (2026-10-31, branch `task/T72-public-build`), items 2–5 except parsing `tracks`:** the option `xatc.squawkTables: 'full' | 'public'` (`squawk.ts`, `dcl.ts`, `xatc.ts`; written to the recording header, `tools/replay/src/format.ts` checks the value; recordings before T72 — `full`); the public variant: the USA — FAA NBCAP, the rest — `randomPlan`. Build: `ACARS_BUILD=public|personal` for electron-vite, `eur-cal.ts`, `icao-apac.ts` and `nattrak.ts` replaced by empty modules from `apps/desktop/variants/public` (a plugin in `electron.vite.config.ts`, `__ACARS_PUBLIC__` → `src/main/variant.ts` → `controller.ts`); `tools/release/build.mjs public|personal`, `tools/release/verify-public.mjs` (table strings and natTrak in `out/` and `app.asar`), the scripts `desktop:dist` (public) and `desktop:dist:personal`. Tests: `squawk.test.ts`, `dcl.test.ts` (APAC → random), `replay.test.ts` (header), `apps/desktop/test/variant.test.ts` (the stubs match the modules by exports, string check). Documentation: `THIRD-PARTY-NOTICES.md`, docs/06, docs/08, `CLAUDE.md`. **Done (2026-10-31), item 1:** the `tracks` format was taken from a real KATL–EDDF OFP (`json=v2`, docs/06 "NAT oceanic clearance"); `Ofp.natTracks` (`parse.ts`), `XatcStation` gives `OclService` the OFP tracks first, then the downloaded ones (personal build); the fixture `katl-eddf-b752-nat-w.json` (`tools/anonymize-ofp.mjs --with-tracks`), tests `ofp-tracks.test.ts` and "Shanwick: the NAT tracks of a real OFP" in `oceanic.test.ts` (track A of the real OFP → `NAT A` in the CLX without downloading; without track A — `RANDOM ROUTE`). **Limitation:** an eastbound OFP (Gander) gives no clearance, `NAT <letter>` is checked on westbound tracks A–F; a live westbound OFP (EGLL–KJFK) has not yet been captured for the human check.

**Out of scope:** `SimConnect.dll` (a separate card); letters to EUROCONTROL, ICAO and the VATSIM Tech Team (the user writes them; drafts — on request); other "not verified" items in `THIRD-PARTY-NOTICES.md` (Hoppie, VATSpy, OpenFlights).

**Done when:** a `tracks` parsing test on an anonymised real OFP across the Atlantic (a fixture in `packages/core/test/fixtures/ofp/`, `tools/anonymize-ofp.mjs`): `NAT <letter>` in the CLX and the track's levels, as before with natTrak; a plan outside the tracks — random route, no error; `squawk.test.ts` tests: in the public variant EUR and APAC — `source: 'random'`, no special codes and no `…00`; a public-build recording passes `replay run --check`; there is no CAL or eANP data in the public `app.asar`; `pnpm check` is green.

**Human check:** 1) SimBrief: plan EGLL–KJFK along a NAT track, load the OFP into the app (public build), fly to the OEP, send an RCL to XATC Shanwick — in the CLX `NAT <letter>` with the same letter as in the OFP, the level from the track. 2) Public build, departure from Europe (for example EDDF): the PDC/DCL arrives, the squawk is four octal digits, not 7500/7600/7700/2000/7000 and not ending in `00`. 3) Personal build, the same departure — a squawk from the CAL range, as before.

#### T73 — SimConnect statically linked: no Microsoft DLLs in the installer (P0 for the public release)
User decision of 2026-10-31: link the client statically. Currently the bridge (`bridge/msfs`, C#) references the managed wrapper `Microsoft.FlightSimulator.SimConnect.dll` and places the native `SimConnect.dll` next to it (`AcarsBridge.csproj`); both end up in the installer's `resources\bridge`. On top of that, `bridge/msfs/Local.props` (local, in `.gitignore`) points to `C:\Users\avail\SimConnectLibs\sdk2024` — copies from LAND3.RAAS.Skybound, re-signed by its author (Axel Reinemuth), not from the official SDK (`SimConnectLibs\README.txt`: "Personal use only"); the installer of 2026-10-30 contains exactly those. The official MSFS 2024 SDK 1.8.1 (Core) is installed: `D:\MSFS 2024 SDK` (`MSFS2024_SDK`).

**Static linking (checked 2026-10-31 on the development machine):** the SDK provides `SimConnect SDK\lib\static\SimConnect.lib` (950,912 bytes) and the property sheet `SimConnect SDK\VS\SimConnectClient-static.props`: include `SimConnect SDK\include`, dependencies `SimConnect.lib; shlwapi.lib; user32.lib; Ws2_32.lib` (x64; Debug — `SimConnect_debug.lib`). A client linked this way does not import `SimConnect.dll` and does not need it next to it.

**In scope:**
1. **Right away, before the rest:** `Local.props` → the official SDK (`D:\MSFS 2024 SDK`) or delete it (the csproj itself takes `MSFS2024_SDK`); rebuild the bridge and the installer; the Skybound copies are no longer used. The official wrapper 1.8.1 has `AICreateSimulatedObject_EX1` (absent from the Skybound copies, T22) — whether to use it is not decided in this card.
2. **Native layer** `bridge/msfs/native/` (C++, MSVC; Visual Studio Build Tools 2022 with VC Tools is on the development machine): our own DLL `acars_simconnect.dll` with the static `SimConnect.lib` from the official SDK. A flat C API in our own terms, not a copy of the SimConnect API: open/close a session (with an event handle for waiting), describe a data set (FLOAT64, STRING256, INITPOSITION), request/write object data, subscribe to a system event and turn it on/off, create/remove an AI object, take the next message. The layer converts messages into its own simple structure (kind, request/object/event id, data pointer and length), so C# does not repeat the layout of `SIMCONNECT_RECV_*`. The needed set is what the bridge calls now (`SimConnectSource.cs`, `SmokeController.cs`, `RescueController.cs`): `AddToDataDefinition`, `RegisterDataDefineStruct`, `RequestDataOnSimObject`, `SetDataOnSimObject`, `SubscribeToSystemEvent`, `SetSystemEventState`, `AICreateSimulatedObject`, `AIRemoveObject`, `ReceiveMessage`; incoming — OPEN, QUIT, EXCEPTION, EVENT, EVENT_FRAME, SIMOBJECT_DATA, ASSIGNED_OBJECT_ID.
3. **C#:** our own P/Invoke layer to `acars_simconnect.dll` instead of `Microsoft.FlightSimulator.SimConnect`; `SimConnectSource`, `SmokeController`, `RescueController`, `SimQueue` and `ReconnectPolicy` change only at the call boundary, the logic and the bridge protocol (v2) do not. The layer is written from `SimConnect.h` and the SDK documentation at docs.flightsimulator.com; do not decompile Microsoft's managed wrapper (SDK EULA, item 2) and do not look at other ACARS apps' code (invariant 1).
4. **Build:** `AcarsBridge.csproj` builds the layer when the SDK is found (`HasSimConnect`) and places `acars_simconnect.dll` next to the exe; without the SDK — as now, the bridge without SimConnect (CI on `windows-latest` without the SDK stays green). The references to `Microsoft.FlightSimulator.SimConnect.dll` and the copying of `SimConnect.dll` are removed. `pnpm bridge:publish` / `desktop:dist` fail if `bridge/msfs/publish` contains `SimConnect.dll` or `Microsoft.FlightSimulator.SimConnect.dll`.
5. **Tests:** `pnpm bridge:test` — parsing of the layer's messages in C# (a stub native layer or fake messages, no simulator); with the layer built — a smoke test of loading the DLL and opening a session without the simulator (a "no simulator" error → `ReconnectPolicy`, no crash).
6. Documentation: `bridge/msfs/README.md` (build: SDK + MSVC), docs/03 and docs/06 (how the bridge connects to MSFS), `THIRD-PARTY-NOTICES.md` (the `SimConnect.dll` line → "SimConnect client statically from MSFS SDK 1.8.1", status), docs/08 ("Deferred until the public release"), `CLAUDE.md` ("Commands": requirements for `bridge:build`).

**Legally:** the SimConnect client code is still distributed — inside our DLL. There is no written permission from Microsoft/Asobo (topics 18233, 18295 on devsupport.flightsimulator.com have no reply as of 2026-10-31), the line in docs/08 stays open. An argument for this path: the static library and the property sheet for it are supplied by the SDK itself, and it cannot be used without including its code in the program.

**User decisions (needed before implementation):**
1. The form of the layer: a DLL inside the bridge process (recommendation: the bridge stays a single process, only the call boundary changes) or a separate exe (another process and a channel to the bridge).

**User decisions (2026-10-31):** 1) a DLL inside the bridge process (`acars_simconnect.dll`, P/Invoke from C#); no separate exe.

**Done (2026-10-31, branch `task/T73-simconnect-static`):** item 1 — `Local.props` points to `D:\MSFS 2024 SDK`, the Skybound copies are not used. Item 2 — `bridge/msfs/native/` (`acars_simconnect.h/.cpp`, `build.cmd`): a flat C API (open/close, define float64/string256/position, request/set/subscribe/state, create/remove object, `next_message` via `SimConnect_CallDispatch`; messages OPEN, QUIT, EXCEPTION, EVENT (including EVENT_EX1), EVENT_FRAME, SIMOBJECT_DATA, ASSIGNED_OBJECT_ID, others — `OTHER`), `ACARS_SC_ABI` 1 is checked on load; the build is `/MD` (the SDK's static `SimConnect.lib` is built with the dynamic CRT: linking fails with `/MT`), plus `advapi32.lib`, `shell32.lib`; the DLL is 47 KB. Item 3 — `SimConnectNative.cs` (P/Invoke, `ISimConnectNative`), `SimConnectClient.cs` (events like the previous wrapper, data parsing, exception names from `SimConnect.h`); `SimConnectSource` is now always built and accepts a stub `ISimConnectNative` (the logic, protocol v2, `SimQueue`, `ReconnectPolicy` did not change). Item 4 — `AcarsBridge.csproj`: `HasSimConnect` = `SimConnect.lib` found; the target `BuildNativeSimConnect` (incremental, `obj/native`), `acars_simconnect.dll` next to the exe and in publish; the references to `Microsoft.FlightSimulator.SimConnect.dll` and the copying of `SimConnect.dll` are removed; before publish the old `SimConnect.dll` and the wrapper are deleted from the folder, after publish — an error if they are there; `tools/release/verify-bridge.mjs` (in `desktop:dist`: the publish folder and the installation's `resources/bridge`). Item 5 — `SimConnectTests.cs` (data parsing, events, a full session, smoke, open errors and short data — on a stub layer; the real DLL: loading, ABI, opening a session), `apps/desktop/test/variant.test.ts` (`verify-bridge.mjs`); `pnpm bridge:test` 189 tests (without the DLL those that touch it pass idly). Item 6 — `bridge/msfs/README.md`, docs/03, docs/06, docs/08, `THIRD-PARTY-NOTICES.md`, `CLAUDE.md`, `Local.props.example`. **Found during implementation:** `SimConnect_GetNextDispatch` returns E_FAIL on an empty queue (checked on a live MSFS 2024), so we take messages via `SimConnect_CallDispatch`, as the managed wrapper did. **Checked live 2026-10-31 (without interfering with the user's flight):** the bridge from a Release build on a second port with `--source simconnect` connected to a running MSFS 2024 (BlueBird 757, in flight): `hello`, `status` connected/running, telemetry at ~1 Hz (position, altitude, fuel, brake, aircraft title). **Not checked** (human check needed, items 1–5 below): pause, touchdown, smoke (T34) and rescue vehicles (T54) on the new layer, Fenix (`L:S_MIP_PARKING_BRAKE`), MSFS 2020, reconnection after closing MSFS, `pnpm desktop:dist` and the installed build.

**Out of scope:** operation without SimConnect (a package in Community, FSUIPC); loading `SimConnect_internal.dll` from the MSFS 2024 folder (an undocumented file; 2026-10-31: the same 117 exports as the SDK's `SimConnect.dll`, plus `SimConnect_InternalOpen`); X-Plane (T18) — unchanged.

**Done when:** `bridge/msfs/publish` and the installed folder have no `SimConnect.dll` and `Microsoft.FlightSimulator.SimConnect.dll`, and have `acars_simconnect.dll`; `pnpm check` and `pnpm bridge:test` are green (locally with the SDK and in CI without the SDK); `pnpm desktop:dist` builds the installer.

**Human check:** 1) MSFS 2024, any aircraft at the stand: the status bar reads "connected · MSFS", telemetry flows (position, fuel, brake; Fenix A320 — brake from `L:S_MIP_PARKING_BRAKE`), pause → "paused". 2) A full flight: phases through to IN as before, `touchdownFpm` after landing. 3) Smoke at the destination airport (T34) and rescue vehicles on arrival (T54) appear and disappear. 4) MSFS 2020: the same as in 1 — a client from the SDK 2024 with MSFS 2020 `[VERIFY]`; if it does not connect — a separate decision (a second layer with the static SDK 2020 library). 5) Close MSFS during a flight and start it again — the bridge reconnects by itself (T59), without crashing.

#### T74 — "About" and crediting data sources in the interface (P1)
Audit of 2026-10-31, item 4. There is no app version in the interface (only the start line in `logs\main.log` and the diagnostics export — `about` in `apps/desktop/src/main/index.ts` and `diagnostics.ts`). Sources and licences are only files in `resources` (`LICENSE.txt`, `THIRD-PARTY-NOTICES.md`, `THIRD-PARTY-LICENSES.txt`). VATSpy (CC BY-SA 4.0), OpenStreetMap and OpenFlights (ODbL) require crediting the source; it is not in the interface, except for mentions in the settings hints (`messages-*.ts`).

**In scope:**
1. **An "About" section** in "Help" (`help.ts`, `HelpView.tsx`, EN/RU): versions of the app, the bridge (`hello.version`, `bridge.ts`; bridge not connected — "no data") and Electron; the licence (EULA, brief and with a link to the file); the list of data sources — each with the source credit and licence; buttons "Open THIRD-PARTY-NOTICES" and "Open component licences" (`shell.openPath` on files in `process.resourcesPath`, via `window.acars`; in dev — the repository files).
2. **A single list of sources** — a module in `apps/desktop/src/shared/` (name, what we take, licence, the mandatory credit line, whether it is in the T72 build variant). A test checks it against the tables of `THIRD-PARTY-NOTICES.md`: every row of the file is in the list and vice versa.
3. **Credit in place:** at the "Live data and events" group — "© OpenStreetMap contributors"; at "XATC station" — "FIR boundaries: VATSpy Data Project, CC BY-SA 4.0"; at the next flight (T53/T55) — "OpenFlights, ODbL". The texts follow the terms of each source (`THIRD-PARTY-NOTICES.md`, docs/06), the mandatory part is not shortened.
4. Documentation: `THIRD-PARTY-NOTICES.md` (where in the app the sources are credited), help.

**Done (2026-10-31, branch `task/T74-about`):** item 2 — `apps/desktop/src/shared/sources.ts`: a single list of 26 rows (name, what we take, licence EN/RU, the mandatory credit line, `kind` bundled/service, `build` both/personal, a link to the terms where known: OSM, VATSpy, OpenFlights); `sourcesFor('public')` removes EUROCONTROL CAL, ICAO APAC eANP and natTrak. Item 1 — `AboutSection.tsx` at the end of "Help" (hidden during search): versions of the app, the bridge (`BridgeView.version` from `hello.version`; no bridge — "no data"), Electron, the build (public/personal); the licence in brief + three buttons (`LegalDoc`: `eula`, `notices`, `licenses`; `main/legal.ts` — `resources/EULA.txt`, `THIRD-PARTY-NOTICES.md`, `THIRD-PARTY-LICENSES.txt`, from the sources — the repository files); the list of sources in two blocks ("downloaded while running", "included in the app"). IPC `getAbout` / `openLegal` (the path is chosen by main, a file name from the renderer is not accepted — only one of three ids). `electron-builder.yml`: the installer EULA (`build/license.txt`) is placed in `resources/EULA.txt`. Item 3 — `SourceCredit` under the groups "Live data and events" ("© OpenStreetMap contributors"), "XATC station" ("VATSpy Data Project, CC BY-SA 4.0") and "Messages" at the connection/next flight ("OpenFlights.org, ODbL"): the credit text is not translated and not shortened, the caption before it is in two languages. Item 4 — `THIRD-PARTY-NOTICES.md` (the section "Where the app names its sources"). Tests `apps/desktop/test/about.test.ts`: the rows of both `THIRD-PARTY-NOTICES.md` tables ↔ the list (each in a single instance, the personal ones — exactly those marked "personal build only"), the contents of the public list, the wording of the credits, the captions in `SettingsView`, the paths of the licence files and their presence in `extraResources`, `getAbout`/`openLegal` of the controller; `simbrief-bridge.test.ts` — the bridge version in `view`. **Not checked** (human check needed): the look of the page, opening the files from the installed build.

**Out of scope:** source lines in ACARS messages (FMS screen width); the licence decisions from T72/T73.

**Dependency:** T72 — the list shows only the sources of the built variant (personal/public).

**Done when:** a "list ↔ `THIRD-PARTY-NOTICES.md`" test; the section in both languages; in the public variant there is no CAL, eANP or natTrak; `pnpm check` is green.

**Human check:** 1) Installed build → "Help" → "About": the versions match `logs\main.log`, the bridge — after connecting. 2) The buttons open the files from the installation folder. 3) The OSM and VATSpy captions are visible in settings in both languages.
#### T75 — Bridge: restart after a crash and a clear reason when the port is busy (P1)
Audit 2026-10-31, item 5. `startBridge` (`apps/desktop/src/main/bridge-process.ts`) only writes to the log when the process exits; the bridge is started again only when the port or the simulator changes (`restartBridge`, `apps/desktop/src/main/index.ts`). If the bridge dies during a flight, there is no telemetry until the app is restarted. If the port is taken by another process (a bridge started by hand or `ACARS Link.bat`), our bridge exits and the app connects to the other one and gets 403 with the T65 token — the user just sees "bridge not connected", and the client reconnects in a loop (`bridge.ts`, backoff 1…30 s).

**In scope:**
1. **Supervision in main** (installed build only, as now): an unexpected exit (not from `stopBridge`) → start again with a pause of 1, 2, 4 … 30 s; the limit is decision 1; after the limit — stop, with the status "bridge does not start" and text from the last lines of `bridge.log`. A "System" line in the flight feed (`feed.system`, like `sim_changed`): "Bridge restarted" — per decision 2. The flight does not stop. The pause is a main-process timer, the logic is a pure function with injected time (testable, invariant 5 is not affected).
2. **Busy port:** the bridge catches the Kestrel bind error (exception type and text — `[VERIFY]` on a live run, do not guess) and exits with a separate code (for example, 4) and a line on stderr; on this code the app shows "Port <N> is used by another program: close the other bridge or change the port in the settings" and does not restart the bridge in a loop. The bridge protocol (v2) does not change.
3. **Token rejection** (403 on connect): tell it apart from "no bridge" if the Node WebSocket client gives the response code (`[VERIFY]` in the Node/undici documentation for Electron 44); if not — by the sign "our process exited with code 4, and the port answers".
4. Tests: `apps/desktop/test` (a stub process: crash → restart with a pause, the limit, code 4 → status without restart, a normal stop does not restart); `pnpm bridge:test` (exit code when the port is busy).
5. Documentation: `bridge/msfs/README.md` (exit codes), the Help "Troubleshooting" section (`no-sim`/`lost`: busy port), docs/03.

**User decisions (needed before implementation):**
1. Restart limit: proposal — 5 within 5 minutes, then stop until the app is restarted or the settings change.
2. A flight-feed line about the bridge restart: yes (proposal) or only the status and the log.

**User decisions (2026-10-31):** 1) limit — 5 restarts within 5 minutes, then stop until the app is restarted or the settings change; 2) the "Bridge restarted" line in the flight feed — yes.

**Done (2026-10-31, branch `task/T75-bridge-supervisor`):** item 1 — `main/bridge-supervisor.ts`: `BridgeSupervisor` (start/stop, retry after 1, 2, 4 … 30 s, limit 5 within 5 minutes, after the limit — status `failed` with the last 6 lines of bridge output without secrets) and the pure `restartDecision(exits, now)`; time and timers are injected; `startBridge` got an `onExit` parameter (exit code or start error, once); `index.ts` holds the supervisor instead of `stopBridge` (`restartBridge` on a port/simulator change calls `stop()` and `start()` — the limit and the status are reset). State — `AppState.bridgeProcess` (`restarting` / `port_in_use` / `failed`), feed line — `SystemCode` `bridge_restarted` (only while a flight is in progress, like `sim_changed`), status in the top row and in the status line — `status.ts` (`bridge.restarting`, `bridge.portBusy`, `bridge.failed`, EN/RU). Item 2 — `[VERIFY]` cleared by a live run of two bridges on one port (2026-10-31, .NET 8): Kestrel throws `IOException` ("Failed to bind to address http://127.0.0.1:N: address already in use") → `AddressInUseException` → `SocketException` 10048; the bridge catches by types, not by text (`BridgeServer.IsAddressInUse`), writes to stderr `acars-msfs-bridge: port N is already in use by another program (exit code 4)` and exits with code 4 (`ExitCodes.PortInUse`, with 0, 2, 3 there too); the new exe returned 4 when the port was busy. On code 4 the app does not restart the bridge and shows "Port N is used by another program: close the other bridge or change the port in the settings". Item 3 — `[VERIFY]` cleared: the Node `WebSocket` client (Node 24.18 / undici 7.28) gives the same thing for 403 and for a refused connection — an `error` with no fields and `close` 1006 with an empty reason, no response code; so the sign is "our process exited with code 4 and the connection did not open" (if a bridge without a token is on the port and the connection opened, the app works with it and shows nothing). Item 4 — tests: `bridge-supervisor.test.ts` (pause 1, 2, 4, 8, 16 s, the sixth exit within 5 minutes — failure, the window, code 4 without restart, a normal stop does not restart and clears the timer, `start()` resets the limit, the exit of an old process is ignored, no exe, the exe disappeared between attempts), `bridge-status.test.ts`, `bridge-process.test.ts` (`onExit` on a real process), `controller.test.ts`; `pnpm bridge:test`: `PortInUseTests` (a really busy port, exception types, exit codes) — 192 tests. Item 5 — `bridge/msfs/README.md` (exit codes), Help "Troubleshooting" (`no-sim`, EN/RU), docs/03. `tsconfig.test.json`: `env.d.ts` added (the status test pulls in `ui.ts` → `store.ts`). **Not verified** (needs a human check): the installed build — items 1–3 below. **Open question:** item 3 of the human check ("end the app in Task Manager — `acars-msfs-bridge.exe` does not remain") is not covered by the card's "In scope": the bridge does not exit by itself when its parent is gone; after a hard kill of the app the orphan process holds the port with the old token, the next start gets code 4 and "port busy". To be decided separately (for example, `--parent-pid` for the bridge or a Job Object in main).

**Out of scope:** restarting the renderer window (`render-process-gone`/`unresponsive` — already exist); a bridge started by hand in dev.

**Done when:** the tests above are green; `pnpm check` and `pnpm bridge:test` are green.

**Human check:** 1) Installed build, a flight with MSFS, end `acars-msfs-bridge.exe` in Task Manager → after a few seconds the bridge is connected again, the flight continues, the feed has a restart line. 2) Start the bridge by hand on port 47830, then the app → status "port busy" with a hint. 3) End the app in Task Manager (the whole process) → `acars-msfs-bridge.exe` does not remain; a repeated start connects to its own bridge.

#### T76 — Tests: timeout in CI and long runs (P1)
Audit 2026-10-31, item 6. CI run 40952545452 (2026-10-29) failed: `dg_notoc` (`packages/scenarios/test/irregular.test.ts`) took 23.4 s with a common `testTimeout` of 20 s (`vitest.config.ts`). Measured 2026-10-31 locally (16 cores): 1422 tests, 15 longer than 5 s, 5 longer than 10 s; the longest — `t50.test.ts` `runway_closure_dest` 26.2 s, `irregular.test.ts` `tech_defect_mel` 25.1 s, `t44.test.ts` RDY 16.0 s; files — `irregular.test.ts` 209 s, `external.test.ts` 81 s, `base.test.ts` 69 s, `controller.test.ts` 66 s. The same `dg_notoc` locally — 9.8 s: `windows-latest` is about 2.4 times slower. The long tests already have a manually raised timeout (30–60 s). The cause of the duration is dozens of whole synthetic flights with a 1 s step over different seeds.

**In scope:**
1. **Right away:** `testTimeout` in `vitest.config.ts` — 60 s under `CI`, 20 s locally; remove manual timeouts from tests where they are lower than the new value for CI.
2. **Speed-up without losing the check:** a synthetic flight — once per file (`beforeAll`) where tests run the same flight; the number of seeds in statistical tests — as many as the test's assertion needs (a comment says why that many). A step larger than 1 s in cruise — only if a run with that step gives the same core messages as with a 1 s step (a comparison test on all scenarios); otherwise do not.
3. **If CI is still longer than 10 min:** `vitest --shard` into 2–3 parallel jobs of `.github/workflows/check.yml`.
4. Documentation: `CLAUDE.md`, if scripts change.

**Done (2026-10-31, branch `task/T76-test-timeouts`):** item 1 — `testTimeout` 60 s under `CI`, 20 s locally; all manual test timeouts removed (they were 20–60 s). Item 2 — no changes to the core or scenarios: `irregular.test.ts` split — shared flights, `SETUP` and `fly` in `test/irregular-fixture.ts`, "each scenario alone" — one file per synthetic flight (`irregular-alone-{normal,go-around,lbs}.test.ts`; a file is the unit of parallelism and of a shard); `fly` has `until` (stop when what is needed is already received: `dg_notoc` after the first message, `tech_defect_mel` after the reply to CONFIRM), a synthetic flight per type — once per file; RDY (`t44`) — the flight up to the start of cruise (slot, RDY and reply — at the gate); T50 — the draw of the wait duration on the first tick (the scenario copy in the test starts at `BOARDING`: the run dice depend on the seed and the scenario, not on the moment — a separate test compares with the draw on its own trigger for all three scenarios), the core state is read only on the `SCENARIO_STARTED` tick (a copy on every tick was a third of the run); `auto.test.ts` — the flight once per OFP, the Direct To by seed once for two tests; `scenario-engine` — the version 3 run once. The number of seeds with the reason in a comment: `tech_defect_mel` 30 → 15 per type (missing one of three — 0.7 %), random Direct To 20 → 10 (Direct To in 5). A step larger than 1 s was not introduced. Item 3 — `.github/workflows/check.yml`: job `check` (typecheck, lint, spdx, `bridge:test`) and `test` ×3 (`pnpm test --shard=k/3`); a local run of the shards with `CI=1` — every test in one of the three. Measured locally (16 cores, the whole suite): `pnpm test` 191 s → 95 s, tests longer than 10 s — 0 (was 4), the longest — data link T31 in `controller.test.ts`, 8.9–9.8 s (two full flights through the controller and replay, a comment in the test). Profile: ~75 % of core tick time is `buildContext` (`scenario/context.ts`), the context is rebuilt on every tick even without scenarios; not touched (out of scope). CI 2026-10-31, three runs in a row green (41236584885 push, 41237047305 and 41237512682 workflow_dispatch): all together 5:15, 5:08 and 7:18 (was ~19.5 min in one job); `check` ~2.5 min, shards 3:26–7:17.

**Out of scope:** changing the behaviour of the core and scenarios for the sake of test speed.

**Done when:** three CI runs in a row are green; the longest test locally — no more than 10 s or with a comment why longer; the total `pnpm test` time locally is no more than before the card; `pnpm check` is green.

**Human check:** none (everything is checked automatically).

#### T77 — Versions and releases through git
Audit 2026-10-31, item 7. Version 0.1.0 is written in two places — `apps/desktop/package.json` and `<Version>` in `bridge/msfs/src/AcarsBridge/AcarsBridge.csproj` — and has not changed; there are no tags and no CHANGELOG; the installer is built each time under one number (`ACARS-Link-Setup-0.1.0.exe`).

**In scope:**
1. **Scheme:** SemVer, an annotated tag `vX.Y.Z` on a `master` commit. The current `master` is `v0.1.0` (the starting point). Until a public release — 0.x.
2. **`pnpm release <version>`** (`tools/release/`): on `master`, the working copy is clean, the tag does not exist; `pnpm check` and `pnpm bridge:test`; one version in `package.json` and `csproj` (test: the versions match); a `CHANGELOG.md` section from the `Merge task/…` merge commits since the last tag (task title, branch); the commit "Release vX.Y.Z" and the tag; `pnpm desktop:dist`. Push of the tag and the commit — only when the user asks (`CLAUDE.md`, "How to work").
3. **Building the release locally**, not in CI: CI has no MSFS SDK, the bridge is built without SimConnect (and after T73 the static library comes only from the SDK, it must not be put into the repository). CI on a tag only checks (`pnpm check`, `pnpm bridge:test`).
4. **The number in the app and in recordings:** the app version — in the flight recording header (`tools/replay`, old recordings without the field are read) and in "About" (T74).
5. Documentation: `README.md` (how to release), `CLAUDE.md` ("Commands": `pnpm release`), `CHANGELOG.md`.

**User decisions (needed before implementation):**
1. The language of `CHANGELOG.md`: English (like commits), Russian or both.
2. Where installers for other people live: a GitHub Release in the private `AVAILOFF/acars-link` (only members see it) or a separate public repository with only installers and release notes (for example, `acars-link-releases`; creation and push — only at the user's explicit request).
3. Updates: do not do now (proposal) / a "Version X is available" notice with a link (once a day the app reads the latest release from the public repository, decision 2) / auto-update with `electron-updater` (with an unsigned installer — `[VERIFY]` in the electron-builder documentation).

**User decisions (2026-10-31):** 1) `CHANGELOG.md` — in English; 2) installers for other people — a separate public repository with only installers and release notes (creation and push — at an explicit request); 3) updates — do not do now.

**Done (2026-10-31, branch `task/T77-releases`, from `task/T73-simconnect-static`):** item 1 — a local annotated tag `v0.1.0` on `master` (`b8bde22`), not pushed. Item 2 — `pnpm release <X.Y.Z> [--no-dist] [--any-branch]` (`tools/release/release.mjs`, pure functions — `version.mjs`): branch `master` (or `--any-branch` for a trial on a copy), a clean working copy, no tag, the version is greater than the current one and both current ones match; the version in `apps/desktop/package.json` and the bridge `.csproj`, a `CHANGELOG.md` section from `Merge task/<branch> into …: <title>` since the last tag (nested merges too, the branch once; no merges — refusal); `pnpm check` and `pnpm bridge:test` already on the new version — an error (and Ctrl+C) puts the three files back as they were; the commit "Release vX.Y.Z", the tag `vX.Y.Z` ("ACARS Link X.Y.Z"), `pnpm desktop:dist`; no push is made, the script prints the command. Tests — `tools/release/test/version.test.mjs` (Vitest project `@acars/release`): versions in the two files match, the version replacement does not touch anything else, SemVer, merge parsing, the section. A trial on a copy branch: with a commit that breaks `pnpm lint` — refusal, `git status` clean, HEAD, versions and `CHANGELOG.md` as before, no tag; without it — all steps in 245 s, a commit of three files, the tag, section 0.1.1 (T74, T75), `ACARS-Link-Setup-0.1.1.exe`; the trial's branch, tag and installer were deleted. Item 3 — built locally; CI fires on any push, including a tag, `check.yml` was not changed. Item 4 — `appVersion` in the flight recording header (`+dev` from sources; old recordings without the field are read, `tools/replay`), in "About" the version is already there (T74, `app.getVersion()`). Item 5 — README.md "Releases", CLAUDE.md "Commands", `CHANGELOG.md`, `tools/replay/README.md`.

**Out of scope:** code signing (decision 2026-10-22: not doing it); publishing installers (at a separate request).

**Done when:** the tag `v0.1.0` on the current `master` (locally); `pnpm release 0.1.1` on a test copy branch passes all steps and rolls back without a trace on a check error; the version-match test; `pnpm check` is green.

**Human check:** 1) `pnpm release <version>` on `master` → an installer with the new number in `apps/desktop/release/`, a section in `CHANGELOG.md`, a tag in `git tag -n`. 2) Install over the old version — settings, recordings and the flight log are in place, "About" shows the new version.

### Audit 2026-10-31, second: release 0.2.0 (cards T78–T84)

A quality and release-readiness audit on 2026-10-31 after the T72–T77 merge (user request): `pnpm check` is green (1489 tests, 98 files; lint — 2 info in `bridge/msfs/test/manual/sandbox-iframe.html`), `pnpm bridge:test` — 192; `verify-public.mjs` on `apps/desktop/out` and `win-unpacked/resources/app.asar` — clean. The code has no TODO, `.skip`/`.only`, `@ts-ignore`; no secrets in the tree and in git history. Findings: `master` is ahead of `origin` by 16 commits, the T72–T75 and T77 branches and the `v0.1.0` tag are not pushed (T78); .NET 8 support ends 2026-11-10, the installer has runtime 8.0.28 with the current one being 8.0.33 (dotnet.microsoft.com, ".NET and .NET Core Support Policy", checked 2026-10-31; T79); the bridge remains after a hard end of the app (open question of T75; T80); `pnpm audit --prod` — `fast-uri@3.1.8` via `ajv` in `@acars/scenarios`, 2 moderate and 2 low (T81); the installer has the bridge `.pdb`, the production build CSP has `ws://localhost:*`, the bridge is 99 MB in 329 files (T82); the terms of NASA EONET and VATSIM data are not checked, there are no letters from Hoppie and Microsoft/Asobo (T83); the installer `ACARS-Link-Setup-0.1.0.exe` was built 2026-10-31 21:11 before the T73–T77 merge, and the tag `v0.1.0` stands on T71 (`b8bde22`) — different contents under the number 0.1.0; the human checks of T72–T75 and T77 are not passed, including MSFS 2020 on the new SimConnect layer (T84). Order: T78 — first, T79 — before 2026-11-10, T84 — last.
