#### T79 — Bridge on .NET 10 LTS (P0, before 2026-11-10)
Audit 2026-10-31 (second), item 2. The bridge is built for `net8.0` (`bridge/msfs/Directory.Build.props`) and published self-contained (`pnpm bridge:publish`, `--self-contained`): the .NET runtime is in the installer (`resources/bridge`). Per the .NET support page (dotnet.microsoft.com, checked 2026-10-31): .NET 8 — LTS in the maintenance phase, support until **2026-11-10**, the latest patch 8.0.33 (2026-10-25); .NET 10 — LTS, support until 2028-11-14, patch 10.0.14. The published bridge has runtime 8.0.28 (SDK 8.0.422 on the development machine; `mscordaccore_amd64_amd64_8.0.2826…`) — five security patches are already missing. SDK 10 is not installed on the machine (`dotnet --list-sdks` 2026-10-31), only the ASP.NET Core 10.0.1 runtime is there.

**In scope:**
1. `TargetFramework` → `net10.0` (`Directory.Build.props`, the test project); SDK 10 — the latest version with a patch not lower than 10.0.14 (installed by the user); `actions/setup-dotnet` in `.github/workflows/check.yml` → `10.0.x`.
2. A build without warnings (`TreatWarningsAsErrors`): ASP.NET Core / Kestrel 8 → 10 API changes — per the official breaking-change notes (learn.microsoft.com, "Breaking changes in .NET 9" and ".NET 10"), do not guess. Exception types for a busy port (`BridgeServer.IsAddressInUse`, T75) — `[VERIFY]` on .NET 10: `PortInUseTests` with a really busy port must stay green.
3. Test packages (`Microsoft.NET.Test.Sdk` 17.8.0, `xunit` 2.5.3, `xunit.runner.visualstudio` 2.5.3) — up to versions that support `net10.0` (per their NuGet pages); moving to xUnit v3 — not in this card.
4. `acars_simconnect.dll` (C++) does not depend on the .NET version — no need to rebuild; the P/Invoke layer (`SimConnectNative.cs`) — check with the `SimConnectTests` tests with the built DLL.
5. `pnpm licenses` — the licence texts of the .NET 10 runtime packages in `THIRD-PARTY-LICENSES.txt`; `verify-bridge.mjs` — unchanged.
6. Documentation: `CLAUDE.md` ("Commands", `bridge:build`: .NET 10), `README.md` ("Quick start": .NET 10 SDK; the "Status" table), `bridge/msfs/README.md`, docs/03, docs/08 (decision 2).

**Out of scope:** runtime trimming (`PublishTrimmed`) and the bridge size (T82); the bridge protocol (v2) — does not change.

**Done when:** `bridge/msfs/publish` has runtime 10.0.x not lower than 10.0.14; `pnpm bridge:test` is green locally with the MSFS SDK and in CI without it; `pnpm check` is green; `pnpm bridge:publish` passes `verify-bridge.mjs`.

**Human check:** 1) Installed build with the new bridge, MSFS 2024, aircraft at the stand: the status line "connected · MSFS", telemetry flows. 2) X-Plane 12 (or auto-selection without a simulator): the bridge starts, status "no simulator", no crash. 3) A bridge started by hand on 47830, then the app — "port busy" (T75, code 4).

**Note:** there are no updates in the app (T77, decision 3), so .NET runtime patches reach users only with a new installer. The release frequency for the sake of patches is a question to the user at T84.

**Done (2026-10-31):** SDK 10.0.403 (runtime 10.0.14) installed via winget at the user's request; `net10.0`, test packages `Microsoft.NET.Test.Sdk` 18.11.0, `xunit` 2.9.3, `xunit.runner.visualstudio` 3.1.5 (`xunit` 2.9.3 — the latest v2 on NuGet 2026-10-31; the runner — the latest of 3.x, 4.0 not tried — moving to xUnit v3 is not in this card), CI — `10.0.x`. The .NET 9/10 and ASP.NET Core 9/10 breaking changes (learn.microsoft.com, checked 2026-10-31) do not affect the bridge code: Kestrel, `CreateSlimBuilder`, WebSocket and `DllImport` without `DllImportSearchPath` did not change; CET by default (.NET 9) forbids native libraries from rearranging the thread context outside the shadow stack — there are no C++ exceptions in `acars_simconnect.dll`, and the SimConnect client itself is checked only with MSFS (human check 1; if the bridge crashes on connect — `<CETCompat>false</CETCompat>`). `PortInUseTests` with a really busy port are green on 10.0.14. `pnpm bridge:publish` first cleans `bridge/msfs/publish` (otherwise runtime 8.0.28 files stayed in the folder); `pnpm licenses` takes the runtime package versions from `acars-msfs-bridge.deps.json` of the published bridge (before — string sorting of the NuGet cache folders, which would have chosen 8.0.28 instead of 10.0.14).

#### T80 — The bridge exits together with the app (P1)
Audit 2026-10-31 (second), item 6; open question of T75. The app starts the bridge (`startBridge`, `apps/desktop/src/main/bridge-process.ts`) and stops it only from `stopBridge` / `BridgeSupervisor.stop()`. If the Electron process is ended hard (Task Manager, a main crash), `acars-msfs-bridge.exe` remains, holds the port with the old token (T65), and the next start gets code 4 and "Port N is used by another program" (T75) — the user has to end the process by hand. The bridge has no parent watching (a search for `parent`, `StandardInput`, `JobObject` in `bridge/msfs/src` 2026-10-31 — nothing).

**In scope:**
1. Bridge: the argument `--parent-pid <pid>`. At start the bridge opens the parent process (`Process.GetProcessById`; the handle is open while the parent is alive, so PID reuse does not interfere); the parent is already gone — exit at once; the parent ended — the bridge closes the SimConnect / X-Plane session and the server and exits with the code per decision 1. Without the argument — as now (manual start, `ACARS Link.bat`).
2. App: `bridgeArgs` passes `--parent-pid` with the main process `process.pid`.
3. Tests: `pnpm bridge:test` — a stub parent (a short child process): the bridge exits after it ends; a nonexistent PID — exit at once; without the argument the behaviour is as before. `apps/desktop/test` — `bridgeArgs` contains `--parent-pid`.
4. Documentation: `bridge/msfs/README.md` (the argument and the exit code), docs/03.

**User decisions (needed before implementation):**
1. The bridge exit code when the parent is gone: 0 (proposal: this is a normal stop) or a separate code (for example, 5) for the log.

**Decided (2026-10-31):** 1 — code 0; the reason — the line `parent process N has exited, stopping` in the bridge stdout (`bridge.log`).

**Out of scope:** a Windows Job Object in the main process — it needs a native Node module, and the app is built without native modules (`npmRebuild: false`, `electron-builder.yml`); restarting the renderer window.

**Done when:** the tests above are green; `pnpm check` and `pnpm bridge:test` are green.

**Human check:** 1) Installed build, the bridge connected → Task Manager, the "Details" tab, turn on the "Command line" column → "End task" on the `ACARS Link.exe` without `--type=` in the command line (the main process; not "End process tree" — it would end the bridge too) → after 1–2 s `acars-msfs-bridge.exe` is not in the list. 2) Start the app again — the bridge is its own, status "connected", no "port busy".

#### T81 — Dependencies: `fast-uri` and vulnerability checks (P1)
Audit 2026-10-31 (second), item 7. `pnpm audit --prod` (2026-10-31): 4 findings in `fast-uri@3.1.8` (vulnerable `>=3.0.0 <3.1.9`, fixed in 3.1.9) — 2 moderate (GHSA-xhq7-xmqj-769h and host spoofing on WHATWG canonicalisation), 2 low (GHSA-333x-w4r6-6crp and path normalisation); the path — `@acars/scenarios` → `ajv@8.20.0` → `fast-uri`, that is, in the main-process bundle. Right now `ajv` checks only our own YAML scenarios (community packs T20 are not done), so exploitation is unlikely, but the fix is cheap. CI has no vulnerability check.

**In scope:**
1. `fast-uri` ≥ 3.1.9: a new `ajv` version, if it has the needed range, otherwise `overrides` (the place in pnpm 12 — `pnpm-workspace.yaml` or `package.json` — `[VERIFY]` in the pnpm documentation); `pnpm audit --prod` — 0 findings.
2. The vulnerability check — per decision 1: `pnpm audit --prod --audit-level=moderate` and `dotnet list bridge/msfs package --vulnerable --include-transitive`.
3. Documentation: `CLAUDE.md` ("Commands"), if a script is added; `README.md` ("Repository and checks"), if CI changes.

**User decisions (needed before implementation):**
1. Where to check vulnerabilities: a separate CI job on a schedule once a week and by hand (proposal: it does not fail a push because of a new entry in the vulnerability database, but is visible in Actions) / a step in `check` on every push / only by hand before `pnpm release`.

**Decided (2026-11-01):** 1 — a separate CI job `.github/workflows/audit.yml`: on Mondays 06:00 UTC and by hand (`gh workflow run audit`). `fast-uri` 3.1.9 — by updating the lockfile (`pnpm update -r fast-uri`): the `ajv@8.20.0` range is `^3.0.1`, `overrides` are not needed. `dotnet list package --vulnerable` exits with code 0 on findings — the step looks in the output for "has the following vulnerable packages" (wording checked 2026-11-01 on a trial project with `System.Text.Json` 8.0.0).

**Out of scope:** updating all dependencies to the latest versions; Electron (T71 — 44.10.0, the next — per security bulletins).

**Done when:** `pnpm audit --prod` — 0 findings; `pnpm check` is green; the check per decision 1 works (a run in Actions or a sample of the output in the report).

**Human check:** none.

#### T82 — Installer: bridge `.pdb`, production build CSP, bridge size (P2)
Audit 2026-10-31 (second), item 9. 1) `resources/bridge` of the installer holds `acars-msfs-bridge.pdb` (51 KB; it has build paths on the developer machine). 2) The CSP in `apps/desktop/src/renderer/index.html` is `connect-src 'self' ws://localhost:*` in the production build too; the renderer does not connect to WebSocket (a search for `WebSocket`/`ws://` in `src/renderer/src` 2026-10-31 — nothing), the rule is needed only by the Vite dev server (HMR). 3) The bridge is 99 MB, 329 files: a self-contained publish puts in the whole shared ASP.NET Core framework (MVC, Identity, Components and so on), while the bridge needs Kestrel and WebSocket; the installer is 157 MB.

**In scope:**
1. `.pdb` does not get into `bridge/msfs/publish` (an MSBuild property for publish — `[VERIFY]` in the .NET documentation); per decision 1 — it is kept next to the installer in `apps/desktop/release/` for analysing stacks from `bridge.log`. `verify-bridge.mjs` — an error if the bridge folder has a `.pdb`.
2. CSP: in the production build without `ws://localhost:*`, in `desktop:dev` — as now (substitution at build time in `electron.vite.config.ts`); a test or a `verify-public.mjs` step: `out/renderer/index.html` has no `ws://`.
3. Bridge size — per decision 2: trimming (`PublishTrimmed`) — whether it is supported for Kestrel + WebSocket without MVC — `[VERIFY]` in the ASP.NET Core documentation on .NET 10 (after T79); trimming warnings are errors (`TreatWarningsAsErrors`). A trimmed bridge is accepted only if `pnpm bridge:test` is green on the published bridge and human check 1 passes.
4. Documentation: `bridge/msfs/README.md` (what is in the publish folder), `THIRD-PARTY-NOTICES.md` — if the set of .NET files changes.

**User decisions (needed before implementation):**
1. Keep the bridge `.pdb` next to the installer (proposal) or do not keep it.
2. Runtime trimming: try (proposal: after T79, as a separate commit, roll back at any doubt) or leave the size as is.

**Decided (2026-11-01):** 1 — keep: publish puts the `.pdb` as usual, the target `MoveSymbolsOutOfPublish` (`AcarsBridge.csproj`) moves it to `bridge/msfs/symbols/`, `tools/release/build.mjs` copies it to `apps/desktop/release/bridge-symbols-<version>/` (the property `CopyOutputSymbolsToPublishDirectory` was not taken: with trimming the `.pdb` of the trimmed assembly is needed, and publish puts it). CSP: the `productionCsp` plugin in `electron.vite.config.ts` (only `build`; no line in `index.html` — a build error), the check — `tools/release/verify-renderer.mjs`. 2 — try, as a separate commit. Done: `PublishTrimmed` in `AcarsBridge.csproj`; 6 trimming errors IL2026 (JSON through reflection, `JsonArray.Add<T>`) removed — frames became records, serialisation through source-gen `BridgeJson`, the frame text is the same (the `ProtocolJsonTests` test was run on the old code too). The bridge 105.6 MB / 335 files → 25.8 MB / 122, the installer 157 → 130 MB (2026-11-01). The published bridge is checked by `pnpm bridge:smoke` (`tools/release/smoke-bridge.mjs`, a step of `desktop:dist`).

**Out of scope:** the size of Electron and `app.asar`.

**Dependency:** T79 (trimming — on .NET 10).

**Done when:** the installed folder has no `.pdb`; `out/renderer/index.html` has no `ws://`; the bridge size — per decision 2 (a before/after measurement in the report); `pnpm check`, `pnpm bridge:test` are green.

**Human check:** 1) Installed build: a full MSFS 2024 flight (connection, phases up to IN, the T34 smoke and the T54 rescue vehicles at the destination airport) — as before the card. 2) In the installed folder `resources\bridge` there is no `acars-msfs-bridge.pdb`.

### Localization: DE, FR, ES, JA (cards T85–T87)

User request 2026-11-01: more interface languages — German, French, Spanish and Japanese. ACARS messages are not translated (docs/08, decision 7): the core, the formatter and the telex texts do not change; only what the user sees in the app is translated. At present (estimate 2026-11-01) the i18n in the renderer is already shared (`i18n.ts`, `MESSAGES: Record<Language, …>`, `MessageKey` keys from `messages-en.ts`, about 550), but in many places the languages are an en/ru pair: the type `Language = 'en' | 'ru'` (`shared/api.ts`), `Lang` and `t(en, ru)` in `shared/sources.ts`, `{ en, ru }` in `main/close-dialog.ts`, `main/hints.ts`, `renderer/src/welcome.ts`, `*_EN` / `*_RU` blocks in `help.ts`, `LANGUAGES` and the choice by OS locale in `main/settings.ts`, the `help.test.ts` test compares only `en`/`ru`; in the scenario YAML (91 files) — the fields `title_ru`, `description_ru`, `hint_ru`, `reply_hints_ru` (about 370), the schema is in `packages/core/src/scenario/compile.ts`. The volume of translation per language is about 1200 lines (interface, help, welcome, hints, scenario texts).

#### T85 — Localization: several interface languages instead of the EN/RU pair **R**
**Scope:**
1. `Language` — a list (`LANGUAGES` in `shared/api.ts`, shared by main and renderer); everything that is now `{ en, ru }` / `Bilingual` / `t(en, ru)` becomes `Partial<Record<Language, string>>` with a mandatory `en`; a missing language → the English text (fallback, per key — no console error, unlike an unknown key).
2. Help (`help.ts`): the texts of each language in separate files `help-<lang>.ts`; a shape test (same sections, questions, number of blocks, `https` only) — for every language that has help.
3. Scenario translations — separate files `packages/scenarios/locales/<lang>.yaml` (key — scenario id, fields `title`, `description`, `hint`, `reply_hints` and step hints); the existing `*_ru` are moved to `locales/ru.yaml` by a script, the scenario YAML keeps English. Check at compile time: no translation for a non-existent scenario/step, `reply_hints` — one string per option (as now for `_ru`). A completeness report per language — a test (prints how much is untranslated), fails only for `ru`.
4. Language choice: the setting and step 1 of the welcome (T70) — a list of languages with self-names ("Deutsch", "Français", "Español", "日本語"); the default language by OS locale (`de*`, `fr*`, `es*`, `ja*`, otherwise `en`). Languages without a ready translation are not shown in the list (a readiness flag in `LANGUAGES`).
5. The interface font `--font-ui` (`tokens.css`): fallback to the Windows system Japanese fonts (`"Yu Gothic UI"`, `Meiryo`) — in advance, for T87.
6. Documentation: docs/08, decision 7; `CLAUDE.md` — where the translations live; `README.md` — languages.

**User decisions (needed before implementation):**
1. Scenario translations — in separate per-language files (proposal; the scenario YAML does not grow, the translator gets one file) or `_de`/`_fr`/… fields in each scenario, as `_ru` now.
2. No translation — the English text (proposal) or the language cannot be selected until the translation is complete.
3. Russian stays mandatory for every new scenario (proposal: yes, as now; DE/FR/ES/JA — may lag, visible in the completeness report).

**Decided (2026-11-01):** 2 — no translation → **the English text**. 3 — **Russian is mandatory** for every new scenario, DE/FR/ES/JA may lag. 1 — **separate files** `packages/scenarios/locales/<lang>.yaml`, Russian moves to `locales/ru.yaml` too (scenarios keep only English); steps with a hint get stable addresses for references from the translation files.

**Out of scope:** the DE/FR/ES/JA translations themselves (T86, T87); `packages/core` except the scenario translation schema; ACARS messages and the formatter; number and date formats (now `en-US` and UTC everywhere — stays so).

**Done when:** with `LANGUAGES = ['en', 'ru']` the behavior is as before the card (all previous tests green, `ru` complete); a trial language with a single key shows the English fallback without errors; `pnpm check` is green.

**Human check:** 1) Switch EN ↔ RU in settings — all tabs, help, welcome, message hints and scenario descriptions in the chosen language, as before the card. 2) A clean profile with the Windows locale `de-DE` — after T86 the app opens in German, before T86 — in English.

#### T86 — Interface translation: German, French, Spanish **R**
**Scope:**
1. `messages-de.ts`, `messages-fr.ts`, `messages-es.ts` (all `MessageKey` keys), help `help-<lang>.ts`, welcome and overview (T70), the close dialog, XATC/DCL hints, source credits (`sources.ts`), `locales/<lang>.yaml` for all scenarios.
2. Glossary `docs/glossary-i18n.md`: terms that are **not translated** (what the pilot sees in the FMS and in ACARS messages: OFP, EOBT, STA, ETA, CPDLC, WILCO, UNABLE, ROGER, PDC, DCL, METAR, TAF, ATIS, `REQ …` commands), and established translations (gate, stand, loadsheet, diversion, ground handling) for each language.
3. Button and setting names in the help match the interface labels of the same language (as the comment in `help.ts` requires) — a test: every quotation "…" in the help is among the values of `messages-<lang>.ts`, where it can be checked automatically.
4. Layout: long German labels do not break buttons, tabs, the side menu and settings (minimum window width).

**Realism rule (texts):** the translation is made by Claude from the glossary; terms — as in airline and add-on documents and interfaces in that language, not word for word. The translation is an assumption: before merging, a native speaker (an amateur pilot is enough) checks it selectively — **user decision**: who checks and what to do if there is no check.

**User decisions (needed before implementation):**
1. Who checks the translation (native speakers of DE/FR/ES) — or a release with a "machine translation" mark in the language list until checked.
2. Spanish — European (`es-ES`, proposal) or Latin American.
3. All three languages in one card (proposal) or one language per session.

**Decided (2026-11-01):** 1 — there is no native-speaker check for now: a release with a **"machine translation"** mark in the language list (`machine: true` in `LANGUAGES`), removed after the check. 2 — **`es-ES`**. 3 — one card. The way to translate — **a third-party engine, not Claude**: the DeepL API (the user's key, `.env.local`) with the glossary `docs/glossary-i18n.md`, fallback — a local Ollama model; the tool `pnpm i18n` (`tools/i18n`), translation memory `tools/i18n/memory/<lang>.json`, manual edits in the translation files are kept. The glossary was compiled by Claude and not checked against documents (an assumption, recorded in the glossary itself).

**Out of scope:** Japanese (T87); infrastructure (T85).

**Dependency:** T85 merged.

**Done when:** the completeness report — 0 untranslated for `de`, `fr`, `es`; the glossary; `pnpm check` is green.

**Human check:** 1) For each language: all tabs, help, welcome and overview, message hints, scenario descriptions; no English fragments and no truncated text. 2) A minimum-size window in German — buttons and menus do not overlap. 3) A native speaker reads the help "First flight, step by step" and 10–15 hints — the terms are clear to a pilot.

#### T87 — Interface translation: Japanese **R**
**Scope:**
1. The same as T86 items 1–3, for `ja`.
2. Font: the Windows system Japanese fonts from the T85 fallback (item 5); no font files of our own (a CJK font is tens of MB; if there is no system one — a user decision). The FMS screen (`fms.css`, B612 Mono) does not change: it shows only ACARS text in English.
3. Layout of Japanese text: line breaks (`word-break`/`line-break` for CJK), line height, help search (`searchHelp`) for text without spaces.

**Realism rule (texts):** as T86; in Japanese interfaces aviation terms often stay in English or in katakana — chosen by the glossary, native-speaker check is a user decision.

**Decided (2026-11-01):** the check — as T86 (the "machine translation" mark); font — only the system fallback.

**User decisions (needed before implementation):** who checks the translation; if the machine has no Japanese font (rare: Windows 10/11 installs Yu Gothic) — only the system fallback (proposal) or our own font in the installer.

**Dependency:** T85 merged; T86 — preferably earlier (the glossary).

**Done when:** the completeness report — 0 untranslated for `ja`; a help search test in Japanese; `pnpm check` is green.

**Human check:** 1) Japanese: all tabs, help, welcome, hints — kanji without "tofu" boxes, line breaks do not split words pointlessly in the middle. 2) A help search for a Japanese word finds the entry. 3) A native speaker reads the help "First flight" and 10–15 hints.

### Flight kinds: cargo, charter, ferry, business aviation (cards T89–T93)

User request 2026-11-04 (option B — the flight kind as a core input and scenario packs, in stages). Everything is currently designed for a regular airline passenger flight:
- the flight kind is not determined anywhere; field 8b of the FPL and item 18 are not parsed (`ofp/parse.ts`);
- passenger scenarios (`unruly_passenger`, `medical_onboard`, `vip_onboard`, `lost_baggage`, `short_connection`, `connection_*`, `rush_bags_followup`, `unruly_followup`, `ped_fire_cabin`, `cmd_medical`) do not check `ofp.pax > 0`: on a cargo or empty flight an "unruly passenger" and "passenger connection" arrive;
- loadsheets are `PAX … CGO …`; `dg_notoc` always has `CAO NO`;
- the T40 family table (`scenario/aircraft.ts`) has no A300, B767, MD-11 and business jets;
- the sender character is always the airline OPS.

**SimBrief data (checked against `reference/ofp/raw`, OFPs SJX822, ANA613, DAL8955, 2026-11-04):** `atc` has `flight_type` (`S`), `flight_rules` (`I`), `section18` (an item 18 line without brackets) and `flightplan_text`; `weights` has `pax_count`, `bag_count`, `bag_weight`, `freight_added` and `cargo`. **`cargo` = baggage + freight** (`6650 = 266 × 25` with `freight_added = 0`), i.e. `Ofp.weights.cargoKg` currently includes baggage. How SimBrief sets `flight_type` (`N`, `G`, …) — `[VERIFY]` on an OFP planned with another flight type. Values of field 8b — ICAO Doc 4444, App. 2: `S` scheduled, `N` non-scheduled, `G` general aviation, `M` military, `X` other.

#### T89 — Flight kind: recognition and scenario filter **R** (P1)
**Scope:**
1. OFP: `atc.flight_type`, `atc.flight_rules`, `atc.section18` → new `Ofp` fields (`flightType`, `flightRules`, `item18`); from item 18 — the indicators `STS/` (list) and `RMK/`. `weights.freight_added` → `weights.freightKg` (freight only, without baggage). Records before T89 do not have the fields — readers treat `undefined` as null.
2. Core: a pure function `classifyFlight(ofp, override)` (`packages/core/src/scenario/flight-kind.ts`) → `SCHED_PAX | CHARTER_PAX | CARGO | FERRY | BIZAV | MEDEVAC`; a condition variable `flight.kind`. Order of rules: manual choice → `STS/HOSP` or `STS/MEDEVAC` → `MEDEVAC` → pax = 0 and freight > 0 → `CARGO` → pax = 0 and freight = 0 → `FERRY` → `flight_type = G` (later also the jet table of T92) → `BIZAV` → `flight_type = N` → `CHARTER_PAX` → otherwise `SCHED_PAX`. `M` and `X` — by the same rules with no content of their own.
3. Core option and recording header: the flight kind and the manual choice are written into the recording; a recording without the option is replayed without the filter (`recordedCoreOptions`), as is usual for behavior changes.
4. Scenario schema: the field `operations: [sched_pax, charter_pax, cargo, ferry, bizav, medevac]`, checked on load (an unknown value is a load error). No field — the scenario is for all kinds. The passenger scenarios from the list above get `operations` with the passenger kinds; check the list against all YAML at implementation (`base/`, `commands/`, `irregular/`, `external/`, `sim/`). A manual start ("Run now") does not bypass the filter; in the scenario list there is a reason: "not for this kind of flight".
5. UI: on the OFP panel — the determined flight kind and a choice "Auto / Scheduled / Charter / Cargo / Ferry / Business / Air ambulance" for this flight; strings in `messages-<lang>.ts` (T85).
6. A question after loading the OFP (user request 2026-11-04): right after loading — a card in the app window (not an ACARS message: OPS knows the flight kind and does not ask) with the recognized kind and what it was determined by. Texts (proposal):
   - scheduled: "A normal scheduled passenger flight — is that right?";
   - charter: "Looks like a charter today — no connections or transfers. Right?";
   - cargo: "No passengers, freight on board — are we flying cargo?";
   - ferry: "No passengers and no freight — is this a ferry or positioning flight?";
   - business: "A business jet — the messages will come from flight support, not from an airline. Right?";
   - air ambulance: "The plan has STS/HOSP — is this an air ambulance flight?";
   - the line under the question is the basis: "From the OFP: 0 PAX, freight 42.5 t" / "Type of flight in the plan: N" / "Aircraft type: C68A".
   Buttons: "Yes" (primary) and "Another kind…" (the list of kinds from item 5). Without an answer the question does not get in the way: the recognized kind applies, the card stays on the OFP panel until answered. The kind can be changed until OUT (leaving the gate), then it is fixed — scenarios are already running by it; scenario starts do not wait for the answer. The answer is a core input (`override`, item 2) and is written into the recording, like a manual choice. The question logic is in `controller.ts` / Zustand state, not on React timers (invariant 5). A new OFP of the same flight (a reload) — the question again only if the recognized kind has changed. The setting "Ask the kind of flight after loading the OFP" (on by default); when off — the recognized kind silently applies.

**Assumptions (need the user's approval):**
- A1. pax = 0 and freight > 0 → a cargo flight, even on a passenger type (freight in the cabin, belly-only). The aircraft type is not used: cargo variants have the same ICAO code as passenger ones (`B77L` — 777-200LR and 777F, `[VERIFY]` against ICAO Doc 8643).
- A2. pax = 0 and freight = 0 → ferry / positioning (including an empty leg of a business jet).
- A3. `flight_type = N` with passengers → charter. If SimBrief always sets `S` by default, charter is recognized only by manual choice.
- A4. Scenarios without the `operations` field run for all flight kinds (fuel, takeoff data, XATC, external events).

**User decisions (needed before implementation):** 1) Manual choice — for the flight only (proposal) or also "default for the callsign". 2) Whether to show the flight kind in the T69 summary and the flight log (proposal — yes, in one line). 3) The question texts (item 6) and the moment after which the kind is fixed (proposal — OUT).

**Decided (2026-11-04):** A1, A2, A4 — approved; **A3 — rejected**: `flight_type = N` is not used, charter is only by manual choice (the rule `N → CHARTER_PAX` from item 2 is removed; `flightType` is parsed and written, but only `G` takes part in the classification). Decision 1 — manual choice for the flight only. Decision 2 — yes, in one line in the summary and the log. Decision 3 — the texts of item 6 (there is no charter question: charter is not recognized, and after a manual choice the question is closed), fixed at OUT.

**Done (2026-11-04):** items 1–6. Check of the passenger scenario list against all YAML: `operations: [sched_pax, charter_pax]` — `unruly_passenger`, `unruly_followup`, `medical_onboard`, `vip_onboard`, `lost_baggage`, `rush_bags_followup`, `short_connection`, `connection_held` / `_made` / `_missed`, `ped_fire_cabin` and also `connex_list` (connections for passenger announcements). **`cmd_medical` is kept for all kinds** — it is a pilot command, a crew member can fall ill on a cargo flight too (without it the pilot would get `RCVD MEDICAL`). Scenarios with `PAX` strings inside (`arrival_info`, `cmd_eta*`, `cmd_divert`, `curfew_dest`, `divert_arrival`, `drone_activity`, `recovery_followup`, `aurora_on_route`) are not filtered — their texts for cargo and ferry flights are fixed in T90–T91.

**Out of scope:** new texts and templates for the flight kinds (T90–T93).

**Dependency:** none.

**Done when:** OFP parsing tests (`flightType`, `item18`, `freightKg` on `reference/ofp/raw`), `classifyFlight` tests on all rules (synthetic OFPs with pax 0, `N`, `G`, `STS/HOSP`, manual choice), a load test (`operations` with an unknown value fails), an engine test (a passenger scenario does not start on `CARGO`, including by manual start), controller tests for the question (answer "Yes", "Another kind" before OUT, after OUT the change is rejected, reloading an OFP with the same kind — no question, setting off — no question, the answer is in the recording), replay of an old recording gives the previous result; `pnpm check` is green.

**Human check:** 0) Load a cargo OFP — the question "No passengers, freight on board — are we flying cargo?" appears immediately with the basis "From the OFP: 0 PAX, freight …"; "Another kind…" → "Scheduled" changes the kind on the panel; after leaving the gate the choice is unavailable. 1) A SimBrief OFP for a cargo flight (for example PMDG 777F, pax 0, freight > 0) → kind "Cargo"; no passenger scenarios during the flight, in the scenario list they show the reason. 2) An OFP with pax 0 and no freight → "Ferry". 3) An OFP with flight type `N` (if SimBrief lets you set it) → "Charter"; if not — record it in the card. 4) A manual choice of "Scheduled" on a cargo OFP brings the passenger scenarios back.

#### T90 — Cargo flights: scenario pack **R** (P2)
**Scope:**
1. A loadsheet for `CARGO`: no `PAX` line, freight and the number of ULDs (only if present in the OFP or from deterministic logic), a breakdown by decks and holds — only for types where it exists in manufacturer documents (airport planning); without a document — the total.
2. Supernumeraries on a cargo flight — `[VERIFY]` where to take the number from (there is no field in the SimBrief OFP — an assumption).
3. NOTOC on a cargo flight: `CAO YES` for cargo only for freighter aircraft, an extended list of dangerous goods — UN numbers and classes only from a verified source (ICAO Technical Instructions / IATA DGR — paid; a free source with a check date — to be found).
4. Special cargo: live animals, perishables, temperature-controlled pharmaceuticals, human remains, valuables; special handling codes — only from a verified source (IATA — `[VERIFY]` availability).
5. Irregular: a late truck / cargo acceptance cutoff at the hub, offloading a damaged ULD (load recalculation), an error in a position's weight (LMC), a shift of the night sort wave, failure of a thermal container.
6. A `CARGO CONTROL` / `LOAD CONTROL` character instead of `OPS` for cargo messages.
7. T40 families: A300, B767, MD-11 (the types cargo add-ons fly) — codes per ICAO Doc 8643, holds per manufacturer documents.

**Realism rule:** all codes, ULD positions and texts — only from documents with a check date and license; otherwise — an assumption for approval.

**User decisions (needed before implementation):** 1) Priority cargo add-ons for the human check (`[VERIFY]` what exists in MSFS 2024: PMDG 777F, iniBuilds A300-600R F, PMDG 737 BDSF/BCF, etc.). 2) Sources of dangerous and special cargo codes (free or not). 3) T55 next flight for cargo airlines — keep AeroDataBox (check whether it returns cargo flights) or go without it.

**Sources (checked 2026-11-04):** UN numbers, names, classes and the "passenger / cargo aircraft" limits — 49 CFR 172.101 (Hazardous Materials Table, govinfo, edition of 01.10.2025, public domain): UN3480 and UN3090 on passenger aircraft — "Forbidden", on cargo aircraft — 35 kg. IATA special handling codes (AVI, PER, PIL, COL +2…+8 °C, CRT +15…+25 °C, HUM, VAL, ACT, ICE, RLI, RLM, RCL, RCM, RFG, RDS) — the open SAS Cargo list "Special handling codes" (effective from 18.03.2026). Type codes A300/B767/MD-11 — ICAO Doc 8643 (export from doc8643.icao.int).

**Decided (2026-11-04):** code sources — only open ones (above); supernumeraries are not shown (no data); add-ons for the human check and T55 for cargo — unchanged (AeroDataBox as is). Approved assumptions: 1) cargo loadsheets — like passenger ones without the PAX line, without ULDs and a breakdown by decks (CGO total), LMC for freight only, signature `LOAD CONTROL`; 2) no passenger LMC on a cargo flight, no LMC at all on a ferry flight (only with the kind filter, `buildFlightData(…, kind)`; the Rng draws are the same); 3) `cargo_notoc` 25 %, `CAO YES` only for UN3480/UN3090, position `{hold}`; 4) `cargo_special_load` 15 % (AVI, PER, PIL+COL/CRT, HUM, VAL), by codes only; 5) irregular: late truck (5 %, delay 10–30 min), damaged ULD (6 %, only when the freight LMC < 0, same kg), weight correction (6 %, when LMC > 0), sort shift at the hub (4 %, 15–45 min, informational), failure of an ACT container (3 %, delay 15–30 min); 6) families A300 (A306, A30B), B767 (B762/763/764), MD-11 (MD11) — wide-body, holds like other wide-bodies; EICAS on B767 (like B757, no primary source), none on A300 and MD-11; the T54 rescue-service category — 8–10, as for wide-bodies; 7) `dg_notoc` (`CAO NO`) — only `sched_pax`, `charter_pax`, `medevac`.

**Done (2026-11-04):** `packages/scenarios/cargo/` — `cargo_prelim_loadsheet`, `cargo_final_loadsheet`, `cmd_req_loadsheet_cargo` (REQ LOADSHEET on a cargo flight), `cargo_notoc`, `cargo_special_load`, `cargo_late_truck`, `cargo_uld_damaged`, `cargo_weight_error`, `cargo_sort_shift`, `cargo_act_failure`, all `operations: [cargo]`, signature `LOAD CONTROL` / `CARGO CONTROL`. Passenger loadsheets — without `cargo`. Passenger lines in the shared scenarios (`BAGGAGE BELT`, `TRANSFER PAX`, `PAX CARE`, `PAX REBOOKING`, `PAX INFO SENT`, `INFORM PASSENGERS`, `LATE PAX`, `PAX TRANSFER BY ROAD`) — optional `~` lines with the variables `flight.if_pax` / `flight.if_no_pax` (empty/null on a cargo and ferry flight with the filter) and `ops.transfer_pax` (null with the filter when not scheduled). Without the filter (records before T89) the texts are as before. The character is only a signature: the Hoppie station is still from the settings template (`OPS{flight}`).

**Dependency:** T89.

**Done when:** scenarios in `packages/scenarios/cargo/` with `operations: [cargo]`, hints and translations (`locales/<lang>.yaml`), template tests and a run of a synthetic cargo flight through `pnpm replay`; `pnpm check` is green.

**Human check:** 1) A cargo OFP on the chosen add-on: the loadsheet without PAX, readable on the MFD screen (line width of the profile). 2) A NOTOC with `CAO YES` and the reply `ACK`. 3) One irregular scenario (forced) — the text is clear, the reply arrives.
#### T91 — Charter and ferry: scenario pack **R** (P2)
**Scope:**
1. `CHARTER_PAX`: no connections and no CONNEX (`connex_list`, `connection_*`, `short_connection` — not for charter), tour operator representative, late passenger group, passenger count change (LMC), special catering, transfer at the destination airport, sports team with oversize baggage. Passenger irregularities (medical, unruly passenger) remain.
2. `FERRY`: "crew only" loadsheet (no PAX and no cargo), ferry flight with a deferred defect — ferry flight permit (FAA special flight permit, 14 CFR 21.197; EASA permit to fly, Part 21 — verify wording), ferry after maintenance, positioning for the next charter (next flight — from the OFP / T55 or without it).
3. Continuation of the "airline memory" (T14): a defect from `mel_carry_over` / `tech_defect_mel` can produce a next ferry flight to the maintenance base — `[VERIFY]` plausibility with the user.

**Realism rule:** texts of permits and procedures — from regulator documents with a verification date; tour operator and transfer roles — assumptions for approval.

**User decisions (needed before implementation):** 1) Which charter stories to include (tour operator / sports / one-off VIP). 2) Whether to generate a ferry after a defect as a "next flight".

**Sources (verified 2026-11-04):** 14 CFR 21.197(a)(1) — special flight permit for a flight to a maintenance base (FAA); EASA Part 21, 21.A.701 — permit to fly.

**Decided (2026-11-04):** decision 1 — all charter stories (tour operator and transfer, late group / LMC, sports team, one-off VIP); decision 2 — do not generate the next leg. Approved assumptions: 1) no CONNEX, connections or `TRANSFER PAX` line on charter; 2) group story — only when the passenger LMC ≠ 0, the same count (50 %); tour operator, sports, VIP — one story per flight (group `charter_story`, 30 % each); transfer before TOD — 60 %; 3) ferry: "crew only" loadsheet 7 min before STD (no LMC); ferry permit 15 % — registration `N`+digit → `SPECIAL FLIGHT PERMIT`, otherwise `PERMIT TO FLY`, without numbers and restrictions; "after maintenance" 30 % — `RELEASE TO SERVICE SIGNED`; positioning — only with a real AeroDataBox FIDS departure (`ops.next_real`; a statistics route with an invented number does not count); 4) synthetic next flight, aircraft change and turnaround (`next_leg`, `aircraft_change`, `welcome_turnaround`) are not sent on a ferry.

**Done (2026-11-04):** `packages/scenarios/charter/` — `charter_group_lmc`, `charter_tour_rep`, `charter_sports_team`, `charter_vip` (excludes `vip_onboard`), `charter_transfer`; `packages/scenarios/ferry/` — `ferry_loadsheet`, `cmd_req_loadsheet_ferry`, `cmd_req_loadsheet_ferry_none`, `ferry_permit` (ACK), `ferry_after_mx`, `ferry_positioning`. `connex_list`, `short_connection`, `connection_*` — `operations: [sched_pax]`. Variables `ops.ferry_permit`, `ops.next_real`. **Item 3 (airline memory):** there is no separate scenario — an open MEL of this aircraft already arrives on any flight kind via `mel_carry_over` ("MX SLOT AT <destination> AFTER ARRIVAL", closed after arrival); a synthetic "next ferry flight" is not generated (decision 2).

**Dependency:** T89.

**Done when:** scenarios in `packages/scenarios/charter/` and `ferry/`, filter tests (CONNEX does not arrive on charter), replay of synthetic flights; `pnpm check` is green.

**Human check:** 1) Charter (manual selection): no CONNEX and no connections during the flight, at least one charter message arrives (forced). 2) Ferry: "crew only" loadsheet, no passenger scenarios.

#### T92 — Business aviation: scenario pack **R** (P2)
**Scope:**
1. Table of business jets (and popular business-class turboprops) for `classifyFlight` and `aircraft.*`: codes — only from ICAO Doc 8643 with a verification date; per family — manufacturer, alerting system (`CAS` for Garmin / Collins — `[VERIFY]`), without `{hold}` / `{seat}` if there is no data.
2. A `FLIGHT SUPPORT` / `HANDLING` persona instead of the airline OPS; a flight without `airlineIcao` and with a registration call sign (`N123AB`) — check the whole path (Hoppie, XATC, VDGS off, T53/T55 without data).
3. Scenarios: ground handling and FBO confirmation (no brand names unless there is a verified source), customs and advance passenger data (for example CBP eAPIS for the US — per official documentation), catering order, car to the aircraft steps, principal's delay or ETA change, owner changing the next leg, crew hotel, fuel release, PPR / airport slots (only from a verified table), night restrictions — the T42 table.
4. Delivery: Hoppie telex is not available in all business jets — list of addons with datalink `[VERIFY]` (WT CJ4, Citation Longitude, PC-24, CL650 in X-Plane, etc.); where there is none — only SayIntentions (T17) or the app window. Display profiles — T15 / T52.

**Realism rule:** type codes, PPR and customs requirements — only from official sources with a verification date.

**User decisions (needed before implementation):** 1) Priority business jets for the human check. 2) Delivery to addons without Hoppie — app window, SayIntentions, or not supported. 3) "Next leg" without a schedule — do not generate (proposal) or synthetic (assumption).

**Sources (verified 2026-11-04):** type codes — ICAO Doc 8643 (official export doc8643.icao.int, 7261 entries), manufacturer — as in Doc 8643; eAPIS — 19 CFR 122.22(b)(2) (Cornell LII): arrival manifest of a private aircraft — no later than 60 min before departure from a foreign airport.

**Decided (2026-11-04):** decision 2 — delivery to addons without Hoppie unchanged (transport from settings); decision 3 — do not generate the next leg; decision 1 (addons for the check) — in the report. Approved assumptions: 1) the "Business aviation" kind by type — 16 families (Citation, Challenger, Global, Gulfstream, Falcon, Learjet, Phenom, Praetor, PC-24, HondaJet, Vision, Hawker, Eclipse, King Air, PC-12, TBM), BBJ/ACJ — only by flight plan type `G`; CAS is not specified (`aircraft.alert_sys` = null), `{seat}`/`{hold}` — as for an unknown type; T54 rescue service category — 1–5 (as for a type outside the table); 2) messages from the support service (header `FLIGHT SUPPORT`); a business flight does not get loadsheets, NOTOC, CONNEX, `crew_info`, gate (`gate_assignment`, `gate_reassignment`, `arrival_info`), VDGS, synthetic next flight, aircraft change and turnaround, passenger irregularities; 3) scenarios: handling/FBO (no brands), catering, eAPIS (only arrivals in the US from abroad: K, PA, PH), car to the steps and crew hotel before TOD, principal's delay (5 %, delay 15–45 min), fuel release (OFP planned fuel). **Not done by decision:** PPR and slots (no verified table), owner changing the next leg (decision 3).

**Done (2026-11-04):** table — `packages/core/src/scenario/aircraft.ts` (`BIZAV_FAMILIES`, `isBizAviationType`), `aircraft_type` rule in `classifyFlight` (after `G`; `FlightKindInfo.aircraftType`), basis line "Aircraft type: C68A" in the UI (`kind.basis.aircraft_type`). `packages/scenarios/bizav/` — `bizav_handling`, `bizav_fuel_release`, `bizav_catering`, `bizav_eapis`, `bizav_principal_late`, `bizav_arrival`, `cmd_req_loadsheet_bizav` ("MASS AND BALANCE BY CREW"). Variable `ops.us_arrival`. The path of a flight without `airlineIcao` and with a registration call sign is verified by a test (`t92.test.ts`): the station `OPS{flight}` gives `OPSN680AB` (9 characters, like `OPSDLH123` in `06`), XATC enabled — no errors, no VDGS commands, T53/T55 — without data and without synthetics (next-flight scenarios are not sent).

**Dependency:** T89; T17 — if delivery via SayIntentions is chosen.

**Done when:** jet table with a source, scenarios in `packages/scenarios/bizav/`, tests of `BIZAV` recognition by type, run of a synthetic flight without `airlineIcao`; `pnpm check` is green.

**Human check:** 1) A business jet with Hoppie (from decision 1), OFP without an airline, registration call sign: kind "Business aviation", FLIGHT SUPPORT messages arrive, no airline loadsheet and no passenger connections. 2) The pilot's reply to one message gets through.

#### T93 — Air ambulance (MEDEVAC) **R** (P3, later)
**Scope:** a flight with `STS/HOSP` or `STS/MEDEVAC` (or manual selection): messages about the patient and the medical team (no medical details beyond general ones), ambulance at the aircraft steps (`rescue` step at `standby` level, T54/T88), change of destination airport by the patient's condition (DIVERT T50). `STS/` values — ICAO Doc 4444, Appendix 2.

**User decisions (needed before implementation):** whether air ambulance is needed at all; acceptable texts about the patient.

**Out of scope:** training flights (circuits), post-maintenance test flights, mail, military transport — separate cards on request.

**Deferred (2026-11-04, user decision):** there is no T88 card in this branch (it is in the unmerged branch `task/T88-ground-rescue`), there is no `rescue` step at `standby` level; T93 — after T88 is merged and a decision on the patient texts.

**Dependency:** T89, T88.

**Done when:** scenarios with `operations: [medevac]`, a test of recognition by `STS/`; `pnpm check` is green.

**Human check:** OFP with `STS/HOSP` in section 18 → kind "Air ambulance"; after landing, an ambulance at the aircraft on the stand.

### Development ideas 2026-11-05 (cards T94–T102)

User request 2026-11-05: 8 ideas were selected from the product analysis. The cards are drafts: sources marked `[VERIFY]` are not verified, assumptions and game numbers are not approved. For cards with **R**, the realism rule of stage 5 applies; for the others, the game numbers (thresholds, probabilities, windows) still go to the user as a list for approval before code (as in stage 6).

**What already exists and is not duplicated (checked against the code 2026-11-05):** hard landing — `irregular/hard_landing_check` (threshold 350 fpm, `touchdownFpm`); fuel burn versus plan — `irregular/fuel_monitoring`; holding `flight.holding` — only as announced by a scenario (`set: flight.hold`, T48/T50), not detected from telemetry; request to hurry up — `irregular/short_connection`; late arrival against a night curfew — `irregular/curfew_dest` (the `ext/curfews.ts` table has only the start of the restriction, no end); SIGMET — volcanic ash only (`data-sources/src/sigmet.ts`, `isigmet?hazard=va`); NOTAM and the `etops` section of the OFP are not parsed; GSX — VDGS write only (T22), the bridge does not read the GSX state.

**SimBrief data (checked against `reference/ofp/raw`, 2026-11-05):** `origin`, `destination`, `alternate` have a `notam[]` array with fields `notam_id`, `location_icao`, `date_effective`, `date_expire`, `date_expire_is_estimated`, `notam_schedule`, `notam_text`, `notam_raw`, `notam_qcode`, `notam_qcode_category`, `notam_qcode_subject`, `notam_qcode_status`, `notam_is_obstacle` (17–81 entries per airport; for some `notam_qcode` is empty). The `etops` section (in `rjtt-klax-a35k-etops.json`): `rule` (`"370"`), `entry`/`exit` (airport, `pos_lat_fix`/`pos_long_fix`, `elapsed_time`, `min_fob`, `est_fob`, `critical_fuel`, `div_airport`), `suitable_airport[]` (`suitability_start`/`suitability_end`, `fcst_cig`, `fcst_vis`, `plan_rwy`, `metar`), `equal_time_point[]`, `critical_point`; in an OFP without EDTO — `[]` or `{}`.

#### T94 — Flight safety department letter: FDM/FOQA **R** (P1)
**Scope:**
1. Core (pure TS, `packages/core/src/summary/` or a new `fdm/`): event detectors on flight telemetry, without grades or scores — a list of events with magnitude, altitude and time. Candidates (by `TelemetrySnapshot` fields): unstabilised approach at the gate altitude (VS, gear, landing-configuration flaps), speed above 250 kt below FL100 / 10 000 ft, go-around below a set altitude (as information, not a violation). Hard landing is not here: it is already in `hard_landing_check`, the letter only refers to it.
2. A message from the safety department (header like `SAFETY DEPT` / `FDM`) after IN or before the next flight; replies `ACK` / `REPORT` (`REPORT` — the pilot files a report, ASR). No events — no letter.
3. Events go into the T69 flight summary and the flight log, one line per event (if approved, see decisions).
4. Detectors work on recorded telemetry → replay gives the same events.

**Sources `[VERIFY]` before code:** stabilised approach criteria — Flight Safety Foundation ALAR Briefing Note 7.1 (1000 ft IMC / 500 ft VMC, configuration, VS ≤ 1000 fpm); 250 kt limit below FL100 — SERA.6001 (by airspace class) and 14 CFR 91.117 (US); FDM practice — EASA ORO.AOC.130, UK CAA CAP 739.

**Assumptions (user approval needed):**
- A1. A single stabilisation gate altitude (1000 ft AGL), without an IMC/VMC split: we do not know the weather at the time of the approach (or — from the destination METAR, if you approve).
- A2. "Landing flaps" — the type's maximum `flapsIdx` or a table by T40 families; the source of indices by addon — `[VERIFY]`.
- A3. Approach speed against Vapp is not checked: Vapp is not in the telemetry or the OFP.
- A4. The 250 kt limit — everywhere below 10 000 ft MSL, without regard to airspace class.
- A5. Bank, pitch, N1 are not used (they are not in the telemetry); extending the bridge (`PLANE BANK DEGREES`, `PLANE PITCH DEGREES`, X-Plane datarefs) — a separate item by user decision.

**User decisions (needed before implementation):** 1) Whether the FDM letter is compatible with the T69 summary principle "numbers only, no grades" (proposal: yes — these are event facts, without an overall grade). 2) When the letter comes: after IN or before the next flight. 3) Whether to show the events in the summary and the log. 4) Whether a bridge extension (bank, pitch) is needed.

**Dependency:** none (T69 — for item 3).

**Done when:** tests of each detector on synthetic approach telemetry (stable — no events; VS 1400 fpm at 800 ft; gear up at 1000 ft; 280 kt at 8000 ft), a "no events — no letter" test, replay of a recording with an event gives the same letter; `pnpm check` is green.

**Human check:** Fenix A320, approach with VS about −1400 fpm at 800 ft AGL → after IN the FDM letter with this event arrives; on the next flight with a stable approach there is no letter.

**Sources (verified 2026-11-07, numbers only):** FSF ALAR Briefing Note 7.1 — stabilisation by 1000 ft above the aerodrome in IMC / 500 ft in VMC, VS no more than 1000 fpm, landing configuration (https://skybrary.aero/articles/stabilised-approach, https://skybrary.aero/sites/default/files/bookshelf/864.pdf); 14 CFR 91.117(a) and SERA.6001 — 250 kt below 10 000 ft.

**Assumptions (approved by the user 2026-11-07):** A1 — a single gate altitude: 1000 ft above the destination aerodrome elevation from the OFP (`altFt − aerodrome elevation`), not by `aglFt`. A2 — no table by addon: landing flaps = `flapsIdx` at touchdown; an event if at 1000 ft `flapsIdx` is below this value or the gear is up (`gearDown` false). A3 — Vapp is not checked. A4 — 250 kt everywhere below 10 000 ft MSL, without airspace classes. A5 — bank, pitch, N1 are not used, the bridge is not extended. Game numbers: VS steeper than −1000 fpm for at least 5 s in a row between 1000 and 50 ft above the aerodrome; IAS > 255 kt for at least 15 s below 10 000 ft MSL (in climb and in descent); events are not counted while paused (`paused`); a go-around below 1000 ft — an information event; one event of each kind per approach, the letter carries the worst value (magnitude, altitude, time).

**User decisions (2026-11-07):** 1) the letter is compatible with the T69 principle "numbers only" — facts without an overall grade; 2) the letter comes 3 min after ON_BLOCK (before the flight is closed), no events — no letter; 3) events go into the T69 flight summary and the flight log, one line per event; 4) no bridge extension. Hard landing is not detected (it is in `irregular/hard_landing_check`).

**Done (2026-11-07):** `packages/core/src/fdm/monitor.ts` — `FdmMonitor` (state is serialised, `CoreState.fdm`), core option `fdm` (without it — off), recording header field `fdm` (the app writes `true`; recordings without the field play back as before), variables `fdm.count`, `fdm.line1…8`, `fdm.more` (docs/05), scenario `packages/scenarios/base/fdm_letter.yaml` (`SAFETY DEPT FDM`, `EVENTS THIS FLIGHT:`, numbered events with UTC time, `INFO ONLY, NO BLAME`, `REPLY ACK OR REPORT`; ACK → `NOTED`, REPORT → `ASR NOTED, SAFETY DEPT WILL CONTACT YOU`, no reply within 15 min — nothing), `FlightReport.fdm` (summary, flight log, a line per event in `ReportPanel`). A telemetry gap longer than 5 s breaks the series, like a pause (there is no data that the exceedance continued). Hints and translations: ru; DE/FR/ES/JA entered by hand (there was no DeepL key or Ollama in the session). Tests: `core/test/fdm.test.ts`, `scenarios/test/t94.test.ts`, `tools/replay/test/t94.test.ts`.

#### T95 — Company NOTAM summary (P1)
**Scope:**
1. OFP: `origin/destination/alternate.notam[]` → `Ofp.notams` (only the needed fields: id, airport, Q-code, start, end, "end is estimated", schedule, text). Records from before T95 have no fields — `undefined` is treated as an empty list.
2. Core: select significant NOTAMs by Q-code (runway and aerodrome closures, landing aid restrictions, approach procedure changes, aprons and stands — code list from the source), effective in the ETA ± N min window. Without a Q-code — by user decision.
3. A `COMPANY NOTAM` message for the destination and the alternate before TOD (next to `dest_weather`), no more than N items, marked "as of OFP release" with the release time; the NOTAM text goes through the formatter (upper case, width, allowed characters).
4. Pilot command `REQ NOTAM [ICAO]` — by user decision.
5. By user decision: `runway_closure_dest` on a real closed runway from a NOTAM, if there is one.

**Sources `[VERIFY]` before code:** decoding of Q-codes — ICAO Doc 8126 (AIS Manual) / ICAO Doc 8400; format and meaning of the SimBrief `notam_*` fields — SimBrief API documentation.

**Assumptions (user approval needed):**
- A1. List of significant Q-codes (compile from the source and show before code).
- A2. NOTAMs without a Q-code (in the samples — 9 of 54 at the destination) are skipped.
- A3. `notam_schedule` (hours of effect) is not parsed: a NOTAM is in effect for the whole `date_effective`–`date_expire` interval.
- A4. Selection window — ETA ± 1 h (game number).

**User decisions (needed before implementation):** 1) Whether the `REQ NOTAM` command is needed. 2) Whether to link `runway_closure_dest` to a real NOTAM. 3) Limit on items in a message.

**Sources (verified 2026-11-07):** Q-codes — FAA JO 7930.2U, Appendix B "International NOTAM (Q) Codes" (https://www.faa.gov/air_traffic/publications/atpubs/Notam_html/appendix_b.html; a US government document, public domain; we take only the codes). The SimBrief `notam_*` fields are not documented — taken from 4 OFPs in `reference/ofp/raw` (400 records), docs/06 "SimBrief".

**Assumptions and decisions (approved by the user 2026-11-07, "yes, all recommendations"):**
- A1. Significant subjects (letters 2–3) by importance: `FA` aerodrome; `MR MT MD` runway, threshold, declared distances; `IC IG IL ID IS IT IU` ILS, `GA` aerodrome GNSS; `PI PU PM PA` approach, missed approach, minima, STAR; `LA LP LV LR LE LC LZ LH` approach and runway lights, PAPI/VASIS; `MN MP MK` apron, stands. We do not take taxiways and their lights (`MX LX LY`), obstacles (`OB OL`), warnings `W…`, other. "Back to normal" statuses (`AK AO AF CC HV CN AX`) — not taken.
- A2. NOTAMs without a Q-code are skipped (22 of 400 in the samples).
- A3 (changed from the draft). The schedule (`notam_schedule`, field D) is not parsed, but not ignored either: it is shown as a `SKED …` line under the item. Reason: at RJBB `RWY 06R/24L CLSD` is in effect only 1500–2130 on separate days, and the arrival is at 10:22Z — "in effect for the whole period" would be untrue.
- A4. Window — ETA ± 1 h, the same window for the alternate.
- Decisions: 1) `REQ NOTAM [ICAO]` — yes (without ICAO — the destination; an airport from the OFP — the summary, otherwise `NO NOTAM DATA`); 2) we do not link `runway_closure_dest` to the NOTAM; 3) up to 5 items per airport, the item text — up to 2 display lines (48 characters); no significant ones at either the destination or the alternate — no message.

**Done (2026-11-07):** parsing of `notam[]` → `Ofp.notams` (`ofp/types.ts`, `parse.ts`; different field types, a record that cannot be read is skipped; NOTAMs shift together with the OFP date — `align.ts`); selection and text — `packages/core/src/ext/notam.ts`; variables `ofp.notam_dest`, `ofp.notam_altn`, `ofp.notam_count`, `ofp.release`, `cmd.notam` (`context.ts`), command `REQ_NOTAM` (`pilot.ts`, `types.ts`, `engine.ts`); scenarios `base/company_notam` (200 NM before TOD, `AS OF OFP RELEASE HHMMZ`) and `commands/cmd_req_notam`, a line in `cmd_help`; app help (commands), RU texts and machine translation DE/FR/ES/JA (the Japanese descriptions were corrected by hand); docs/05, docs/06. Tests: `packages/core/test/notam.test.ts` (fixture `fixtures/ofp/notams-rjbb-rjgg.json` — 14 real NOTAMs, only their fields; on `reference/ofp/raw` — if the folder exists), `packages/scenarios/test/t95.test.ts`. The `SKED` line is limited to 48 characters (2 lines), not one line: at 24 characters the hours of effect were lost (`07 09 13-14 16 20-21` without `1500/2130`).

**Out of scope:** live NOTAMs during the flight (there are no sources without an account — T38 analysis).

**Dependency:** none.

**Done when:** tests of `notam[]` parsing on `reference/ofp/raw` (including an empty Q-code), a test of selection by Q-code and ETA window, a test of formatting a long text, replay of an old recording unchanged; `pnpm check` is green.

**Human check:** a SimBrief OFP to an airport with a closed runway or an inoperative ILS (visible in the SimBrief briefing) → before TOD a `COMPANY NOTAM` with this item arrives, readable on the Fenix A320 and PMDG 777 displays.

#### T96 — EDTO (ETOPS) monitoring **R** (P2)
**Scope:**
1. OFP: `etops` section → `Ofp.edto` (rule, entry and exit points, ETP, critical point, route alternates with suitability windows); empty `[]` / `{}` → null.
2. Scenarios for EDTO flights: before the entry point (EEP) — a message with the alternates (ERA) and their live METAR/TAF (T13 adapter) in the suitability windows; at the ETP — OFP estimated fuel (`est_fob`), actual and critical (`critical_fuel`); after the exit point (EXP) — exit confirmation.
3. If the alternate's forecast in the suitability window is below minima — OCC proposes another alternate from `suitable_airport[]`; pilot reply `ACCEPT` / `UNABLE`. The airport for `DIVERT` (T50) — from this list, if the flight is EDTO.
4. All figures — from the OFP and METAR/TAF, no generation (invariant 8).

**Sources `[VERIFY]` before code:** planning and en-route minima for EDTO alternates — ICAO Annex 6 Part I, ICAO Doc 10085 (EDTO Manual), EASA AMC 20-6, FAA AC 120-42B; meaning of the fields `etops_condition`, `div_*` — SimBrief documentation.

**Assumptions (user approval needed):**
- A1. After departure an alternate is considered unsuitable if the forecast in the suitability window is below landing minima; runway minima are unknown — a simplified bound is needed (game numbers for the lower bound of cloud base and visibility) — for approval.
- A2. The weather check — only at the moment of the message at the EEP and every N min until the EXP (game number), respecting the aviationweather.gov limits.
- A3. Based on OFP data only: an aircraft without an `etops` section gets no messages.

**User decisions (needed before implementation):** 1) Whether an alternate change with a pilot reply is needed, or information only. 2) Whether a link to XATC (oceanic areas, T27) is needed.

**Dependency:** T13 (METAR/TAF), T50 (DIVERT).

**Done when:** tests of `etops` parsing on `rjtt-klax-a35k-etops.json` and on an OFP without EDTO, a test of the EEP/ETP/EXP triggers on a synthetic RJTT–KLAX flight (`pnpm replay synth`), a test of alternate change on a bad forecast (TAF fixture); `pnpm check` is green.

**Human check:** iniBuilds A350 or PMDG 777, OFP with EDTO (for example, RJTT–KLAX): at the EEP a message with the ERA and weather arrives, at the ETP — fuel, after the EXP — confirmation; texts are readable on the display.

#### T97 — Hazard reports along the route (ride reports) **R** (P2)
**Scope:**
1. `packages/data-sources`: international SIGMETs for turbulence, icing and thunderstorms (extension of `sigmet.ts`, currently `hazard=va`) and PIREPs (mainly US); cache and limits — as for the existing adapters. Data goes into the core input `CoreInput.data` and into the recording.
2. Core: hazards in the route corridor ahead, in a layer around the planned or current flight level, not older than N min → a `RIDE REPORT` / `SIGMET` message (hazard type, intensity, layer, segment, report age, aircraft type for PIREPs).
3. Hint: another flight level can be requested via XATC (T25, already exists); the station itself does not change the level.
4. No more than one message per FIR or N min; not in sterile phases.

**Sources `[VERIFY]` before code:** aviationweather.gov Data API — `/api/data/isigmet` (parameter `hazard`), `/api/data/pirep`, `/api/data/airsigmet` (US): parameters, format and terms of use (US government data — check the status and User-Agent requirements, as in `common.ts`).

**Assumptions (user approval needed):** corridor (NM from the route), layer (± ft), maximum PIREP age, the "ahead" horizon (min), message frequency — game numbers as a list.

**User decisions (needed before implementation):** 1) Information only, or also a flight level proposal from OCC (numbers — from the SIGMET, not from an LLM). 2) On by default or not.

**Dependency:** T13.

**Done when:** adapter tests on saved API responses (no network), a test of selection by corridor and layer, a frequency test, replay of a recording with data; `pnpm check` is green.

**Human check:** a flight over the US on a day with active turbulence SIGMETs/PIREPs (visible on aviationweather.gov) → a `RIDE REPORT` arrives with the same segment and layer.

#### T98 — Ground handling by the actual GSX state **R** (P1)
**Scope:**
1. Bridge (MSFS 2024): reading the GSX state — boarding, deboarding, refuelling, catering, loading, pushback — into an optional telemetry field (for example, `ground`); bridge protocol version. Without GSX or in X-Plane the field is absent.
2. Core: condition variables (`ground.boarding`, `ground.refuel`, …) and events "boarding finished", "refuelling finished".
3. Base scenarios: final loadsheet — after GSX boarding ends (if GSX is active), otherwise on schedule, as now; fuel slip — when GSX refuelling ends, together with the existing fuel stabilisation; message `BOARDING COMPLETED <pax>` (numbers from the OFP and LMC, not from GSX, if GSX does not provide them).
4. If boarding lags behind schedule by more than N min — a delay code from `delay-codes.ts` and a line in the T69 summary.
5. Recording: the GSX state is part of the telemetry → replay reproduces it.

**Sources `[VERIFY]` before code:** names and values of the GSX L:vars (`FSDT_GSX_*`) — official FSDT documentation / FSDT forum; reading L:vars via SimConnect in MSFS 2024 — MSFS 2024 SDK; GSX Pro behaviour with Fenix/PMDG (the addons have their own GSX integration).

**Assumptions (user approval needed):**
- A1. GSX is considered active if its variables can be read and at least one service has been requested; otherwise — the previous time-based logic.
- A2. The boarding lag threshold (min) — a game number.
- A3. The passenger count in the message — from the OFP (with LMC), not from GSX.

**User decisions (needed before implementation):** 1) Whether to wait for GSX indefinitely or with a fallback timeout (proposal — timeout, then by schedule). 2) Whether item 4 (delay) is needed.

**Dependency:** T21 (bridge channel), T22 (VDGS, the same GSX).

**User decisions (2026-11-07):** 1) timeout: the final loadsheet waits for GSX boarding to end for no more than 10 min after its own time (game number), then — by schedule; 2) item 4 (delay code and summary line) — not doing it, later. A1–A3 approved.

**Sources (verified 2026-11-07):** names and values of the L:vars — FSDreamTeam, "GSX Pro – Ground Services for MSFS", manual version 4.0.9 Pro, p. 106 "DEVELOPERS – Interfacing with GSX" (`<Addon Manager>couatlGSXGSX_manual_MSFS.pdf` on the user's machine): `L:FSDT_GSX_{DEBOARDING,CATERING,REFUELING,BOARDING,DEPARTURE,DEICE}_STATE` — 1 can be called, 2 unavailable, 3 skipped, 4 requested, 5 in progress, 6 completed; `L:FSDT_GSX_BOARDING_CARGO` — 1 while baggage is being loaded; the variables are read-only. Reading an L:var via SimConnect — `AddToDataDefinition("L:NAME", "number")`, FLOAT64 (MSFS 2024 SDK, verified 2026-10-23, works in the bridge for Fenix). Behaviour with Fenix/PMDG: the L:vars are variables of GSX itself, the addon does not change them; whether addons with their own GSX integration (Fenix — its own boarding via the EFB) put the GSX services into the same states — `[VERIFY]` by a human (below).

**Done (2026-11-07):** bridge — 6 L:vars in the telemetry definition (`SimVars.cs`), field `ground` (`Protocol.cs`, omitted when all are 0), tests `GsxTests.cs` (parsing a SimConnect frame, field present/absent, X-Plane without the field). Core — `TelemetrySnapshot.ground` and shape check, `scenario/ground.ts` (activity A1, events, variables), events `GSX_BOARDING_DONE`/`GSX_REFUEL_DONE` as triggers. Scenarios — `final_loadsheet`, `fuel_slip` (`if` steps: without GSX nothing changes), new `base/boarding_completed` (texts in `locales/*.yaml`, 5 languages); the GSX refuelling wait in `fuel_slip` uses the same 10 min limit that was approved for the loadsheet. Tests — `packages/scenarios/test/t98.test.ts`, `tools/replay/test/t98.test.ts`. With T61 (`lateStartVersion: 4`): the GSX wait is cut off by a late send at pushback. The bridge protocol and `telemetryVersion` were not changed — the field is optional, compatible (like `simUtcMs`).

**Done when:** bridge reading tests (SimConnect stub), a protocol test (field present / absent), core tests: the loadsheet waits for boarding to end, without GSX — by schedule, timeout; replay of an old recording without the field — the same result as before; `pnpm check` and `pnpm bridge:test` are green.

**Human check:** MSFS 2024 + GSX Pro + Fenix A320: request boarding in GSX — the final loadsheet arrives only after it ends; a flight without GSX — loadsheet by schedule, as before.

#### T99 — OCC reacting to aircraft behaviour (P2)
**Scope:**
1. Core: telemetry detectors (pure TS, condition variables):
   - actual holding — a heading turn of 360° or more within N min at almost constant altitude (currently `flight.holding` is only announced by a scenario);
   - deviation from the OFP route by more than X NM for longer than Y min (position on the route — `atc/route.ts`);
   - cruise level differs from the plan by 2000 ft or more for longer than N min;
   - taxi-out longer than the OFP planned time + X min;
   - return to the stand after OUT before OFF.
2. `irregular/` scenarios with OCC questions and pilot replies: reason, expected delay, remaining time in holding (`EAT`); holding from the detector starts the existing `hold_fuel_watch` chain. Figures in OCC replies — only from data (invariant 8).
3. Do not ask if the core knows the reason: Direct To, level change or holding issued by XATC; a diversion; a scenario has already announced the holding.

**Assumptions (user approval needed):** all thresholds (N, X, Y, 2000 ft) — game numbers as a list; the holding detector does not distinguish holding from a manoeuvre for a visual approach — an altitude-above-aerodrome limit for approval.

**User decisions (needed before implementation):** 1) Which detectors are needed in the first version. 2) How many OCC questions per flight at most (group and `max_per_flight`, T39).

**Dependency:** T39 (groups), T48 (link to XATC).

**Done when:** tests of each detector on synthetic telemetry (holding, route deviation, other level, long taxi, return to stand) and for false positives (Direct To from XATC, approach vectors), replay; `pnpm check` is green.

**Human check:** any airliner, in cruise manually fly a hold over a route waypoint → after N min OCC asks about the delay; after a Direct To from XATC there is no question about leaving the route.
#### T100 — Special passengers in the loadsheet (SSR) **R** (P2)
**Includes:**
1. An SI line in the preliminary and final loadsheet: special passenger codes (for example WCHR, WCHS, WCHC, UMNR, PETC, AVIH, BLND, DEAF) with counts; consistent with the passenger count and LMC.
2. Follow-ups before TOD or on arrival: an ambulift or seats at the stand for WCH*, meeting and handover of UMNR, an animal in the hold (AVIH — hold heating, only for types where this is in the documents).
3. Only for `operations: [sched_pax, charter_pax]` (T89).
4. Counts come from the seed (`Rng`), deterministically.

**Sources `[VERIFY]` before code:** SSR codes — IATA AIRIMP / Reservations Services Manual (paid; check that public airline descriptions can be used, and the licence); format of the SI line in the loadsheet — IATA AHM 517 / AHM 516; PRM assistance requirements — EU Regulation 1107/2006.

**Assumptions (user approval needed):** code frequencies per flight (there are probably no public per-flight statistics — game numbers); the set of codes for the first version.

**User decisions (needed before implementation):** 1) Whether the follow-ups (item 2) are needed or only the SI line. 2) Whether a link to `medical_onboard` (WCHC and a medical case) is needed or not.

**Dependency:** T89.

**Done when:** loadsheet tests with the SI line (width, display profile), consistency with the passenger count and LMC, the scenario does not run on `CARGO`/`FERRY`, replay with the same seed; `pnpm check` is green.

**Human check:** PMDG 777 (the narrowest of the verified displays — `[VERIFY]` against the profiles): the SI line fits and is readable; if the SI has WCH*, an ambulift message arrives before TOD.

#### T101 — Early arrival handling (P2)
**Includes:**
1. Core: compare the estimated arrival time (the same calculation as `cmd_eta`) with the scheduled `sched_in` and with the end of the destination airport's night curfew.
2. Scenario (before TOD, in cruise): if the ETA is earlier than the end of the night curfew — `NO ARR BEFORE <time>Z, REDUCE SPEED`; if the ETA is earlier than `sched_in` by N minutes or more — `EARLY ARR, STAND NOT AVBL BEFORE <time>Z` (the stand is occupied until the scheduled time). Replies `ABLE` / `UNABLE`; `UNABLE` — holding on the taxiway or at the stand.
3. The night curfew table `ext/curfews.ts` gets the end of the curfew — only for airports that have it in the source, with source and verification date (as in T42). No entry — no curfew message.
4. Not together with `short_connection` and `curfew_dest` on the same flight (T39 group).

**Assumptions (user approval needed):** the early arrival threshold (min); the probability that the stand is occupied until the scheduled time (game number); the text for the case "flight arrives before opening" at airports with restrictions on departures only (the T42 table has landing curfews only).

**User decisions (needed before implementation):** 1) Whether the "stand not ready" case is needed without a night curfew or only the curfew. 2) Enabled by default or not.

**Dependency:** T42 (curfew table), T39.

**Done when:** tests: ETA earlier than the end of the curfew → message; airport without a curfew end → no message; early arrival without a curfew → stand message (if approved); not together with `short_connection`; replay; `pnpm check` is green.

**Human check:** a flight to an airport with a night curfew, arriving before it ends (for example, a night flight with a tailwind; the airport from the table after verification) → in cruise a request arrives to reduce speed, with the curfew end time in UTC.

#### T102 — VDGS display at the departure stand: airline TOBT (A-CDM) **R** (P2)
User request 2026-11-05: VDGS messages before departure too, realistic only. Closes part of item 6 of T45 ("departure display — if GSX supports it").

**What is already known (verified 2026-11-05):**
- GSX: its own messages cycle "while the aircraft is on the stand", the cycle ends at pushback ("VGDS Message Editor User Manual", `06`, "GSX VDGS", second source) — a departure display is technically available through the same `acars-sim.json` file, with `conditions.airports` = the departure airport.
- GSX itself shows the standard `flight_information` message at departure (standard file `parkingServices\vgds_standard_messages.json` dated 2026-05-29): `DESTN`/`FLTN`, `ETD`/`TTD` (countdown), `TOBT`/`TSAT`, `RWY`/`VIA`; and `passenger_cargo_info` (`PAX`/`CARGO`/`FUEL`). The data comes from SimBrief, only with a SimBrief login in GSX and a matching OFP ("GSX_manual_MSFS.pdf", p. 105: on TS-24/TS-42 — destination, ETD and countdown). This time is static: GSX knows nothing of the core's delays and slots — the display and OPS telexes may diverge.
- Overriding the standard message: your own message with the same `id` (`flight_information`) — sample `SDK\Samples\VGDS\sample_chock_override.json` (override of `chock_and_gate_display`); when the `conditions` are not met, GSX shows the standard one (editor manual, "Override and Filtering Interaction").
- A real departure display — Düsseldorf Airport, "Airport-CDM Flight Crew Briefing", version 5.0 of 2022-08-16, item 2.8 (SafeGate display): TOBT and its changes — in UTC from TOBT − 30 min, with a countdown; after TOBT is reached the line is empty; TSAT — as a scrolling line after TOBT from TOBT − 7 min; TOBT deleted — TOBT, TSAT and the countdown disappear, "FLIGHT SUSPENDED - NEW TOBT REQUIRED" is published; AOBT/AIBT — 5 min. Toronto Pearson, "A-CDM key concepts": TSAT is shown on the stand AVDGS, otherwise the airline/handler passes it on. CAAS Singapore, AIP SUP 167/15: TSAT on the display may be revised.

**Includes:**
1. Scenario `sim/vdgs_departure` (before OUT, at the departure stand, only A-CDM airports from item 4): override of `flight_information` — flight, destination, **airline TOBT** = planned OFP off-block + core delay (`flight.delay_min`: late start, pilot `DELAY`, irregular situations), from TOBT − 30 min; after TOBT is reached there is no TOBT line. The file is rewritten on every TOBT change (GSX rereads it by itself).
2. Readiness time unknown (a scenario announced a delay without a new time) → red lines "FLIGHT SUSPENDED" / "NEW TOBT REQUIRED" (by display width, A3); new time → TOBT again.
3. Core: at OUT the message is removed (GSX ends the cycle at pushback anyway, but it must not be in the file); `vdgs_arrival` does not change.
4. Table of A-CDM airports (`ext/acdm-airports.ts`): only those that fully implemented A-CDM, each entry with a source and verification date. No entry — no override, the standard GSX screen is on the display.
5. Only `operations: [sched_pax, charter_pax, cargo, ferry]` (as for `vdgs_arrival`, without `bizav`); `ofp.airline` is required.

**Sources `[VERIFY]` before code:** the list of A-CDM airports — EUROCONTROL (the list of those that fully implemented A-CDM, licence of use); whether real displays show CTOT — airport A-CDM manuals (Incheon A-CDM Operation Manual, Rev. 3 "changed VDGS display"; EUROCONTROL Airport CDM Implementation Manual v5.0); GSX variables `${tobt_message}`/`${tsat_message}` — where GSX gets them from (editor manual: "SimBrief flight plan"), so as not to duplicate.

**Assumptions (user approval needed):**
- A1. Düsseldorf timings (TOBT − 30 min, empty line after TOBT) — for all A-CDM airports in the table.
- A2. We do not show TSAT: the core has no A-CDM departure queue; any calculation (for example, CTOT − taxi) is a heuristic. Option — do not output the TSAT line at all.
- A3. The wording "FLIGHT SUSPENDED / NEW TOBT REQUIRED" is Düsseldorf's; for narrow (6 characters) we need our own abbreviations — a list for approval (there is no source for abbreviations).
- A4. CTOT on the display — only if the source from the "[VERIFY]" item confirms it; otherwise CTOT stays only in the `slot_ctot` telex.
- A5. Whether to override `flight_information` entirely (then RWY/VIA and the GSX `TTD` countdown disappear — they must be repeated from the OFP) or add our own message after the standard one (then the display has two different TOBTs).
- A6. `small` (4 characters) is not written.

**User decisions (needed before implementation):** 1) A5 — override or addition. 2) Whether item 2 (FLIGHT SUSPENDED) is needed. 3) Enabled by default or not.

**Decided (2026-11-05):** decision 1 — override of `flight_information` (RWY and SID are repeated from the OFP, the countdown is our own, the file is rewritten once per minute of flight time, the same text is not rewritten); decision 2 — item 2 is needed; decision 3 — enabled by default. Approved A1 (DUS timings for all airports in the table), A2+A4 (no TSAT and CTOT), A3 (narrow: `FLIGHT / SUSPND / NEW / TOBT / REQRD`), A6 (no small); the table — 35 airports from the EUROCONTROL page. Additionally approved: A7 — "delay without a new time" = the aircraft has not left the blocks 15 min after TOBT (by the DUS rule "otherwise TOBT is deleted"; 15 min — no source), a new pilot `DELAY` gives a new TOBT; A8 — an ATFM slot (`slot_ctot`) does not shift TOBT (A-CDM: CTOT affects TSAT), the other `set: flight.delay_min` and `DELAY` do shift it. `[VERIFY]` check: EUROCONTROL — 35 airports, the page has no date or licence; there is no CTOT on the DUS display (v5.0, item 2.8); the GSX variables `${tobt_message}`/`${tsat_message}` are not verified — there are no GSX files on the development machine (overriding `flight_information` removes them from the display anyway). Without a decision: the countdown format `TTD nnMIN` (wide) / `TTD nn MIN` (x) — by the `TTD` label of the standard GSX message; there is no countdown on narrow (no room); the display appears no earlier than `sched_out` − 60 min (shifted by the delay), before that — the standard GSX screen. **Done** (T102): `sim/vdgs_departure.yaml`, the `vdgs` step with `at`/`replaces`/`stop`, variables `flight.tobt`, `flight.minutes_to_tobt`, `ofp.orig_acdm`, `ext/acdm-airports.ts`. **Fixed after a check in MSFS (2026-11-05, EDDF A19):** `id: flight_information` alone is not enough — GSX showed its own SimBrief TOBT; the override requires the file field `overrides.stock` (sample `sample_chock_override.json`, editor manual "Override System"), it is now written. Second: the `sched_out − 60` trigger was compressed by a late start (loading in 35 min + `DELAY 20` → the display only around TOBT − 33) and changed the compression of the other pre-departure messages at A-CDM airports; now `est_out − 180` (not compressed, not part of the calculation), the display from the first tick. Third: at stand EDDF A19 the display is SafeDock FLEX, and we had no `flex` variant — GSX showed its own; the `flex` type (10×5) was added, and the departure display has its own lines (flight, destination, TOBT, `TTD nn MIN`, runway; SID does not fit). Result of the check at EDDF A19: the display is drawn by the Aerosoft EDDF 2.5 airport script (`addVdgsMessage` with `id: flight_information` every 30 s, VATSIM CDM/SimBrief) — it takes precedence over our file (GSX manual, p. 201); repeat the human check at an A-CDM airport without such a script. **Checked by a human 2026-11-05, EGLL (iniBuilds, the airport script does not touch the display), narrow display:** the standard GSX screen is replaced from the first minute of the flight (`DAL / 8947 / KSGF / TOBT / 1125` at STD 1125Z); after `DELAY 20` TOBT 1145 is outside the 30-min window — the time lines are empty from the next page (recording `20261105-110005-DAL8947`).

**Dependency:** T22 (VDGS), T43/T44 (delays, slot).
